\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{skel}}_{t}\cup\mathcal{Z}^{**}_{\epsilon\mathrm{lvl}}(\hat{M}_{t})
, we have

\psi_{N_{t}}(\hat{M}_{t}||M^{\dagger}):=\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}N_{t}(z)\mathrm{KL}(\hat{M}_{t}(z)||M^{\dagger}(z))\geq(1+\epsilon_{\mathrm{tst}}(t))\log(t).

(C.10)

Let
M_{t}^{\dagger}
be the model as given by (
C.6
).
On
\mathcal{E}_{t}\cap\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{t}\cap(t\in\mathcal{T}_{0}^{+})\cap(z_{0}\in\mathcal{Z}^{**}_{\epsilon\mathrm{lvl}}(\hat{M}_{t})
and by (
S
TEP
1
), we have
M_{t}^{\dagger}\in\mathrm{Cnf}(\hat{M}_{t})
so (
C.10
) holds in particular for
M^{\dagger}\equiv M^{\dagger}_{t}
and

\displaystyle(1+\epsilon_{\mathrm{tst}}(t))\log(t)

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{\leq}\psi_{N_{t}}(\hat{M}_{t}||M^{\dagger}_{t})

\displaystyle\overset{(\ddagger)}{=}\sum_{z\notin\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{skel}}_{t}}N_{t}(z)\mathrm{KL}(\hat{M}_{t}(z)||M(z))\overset{(\lx@sectionsign)}{\equiv}\psi_{N^{\prime}_{t}}(\hat{M}_{t}||M)

(C.11)

where

(\dagger)
holds on
\mathcal{E}_{t}\cap\mathcal{E}_{t}^{\prime}\cap(t\in\mathcal{T}_{0}^{+})\cap(z_{0}\notin\mathcal{Z}^{**}_{\epsilon\mathrm{lvl}}(\hat{M}_{t}))
following (
C.10
);

(\ddagger)
follows by construction of
M^{\dagger}_{t}
; and

(\lx@sectionsign)
introduces
N^{\prime}_{t}(z):=\mathbf{1}(z\notin\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{skel}}_{t})N_{t}(z)
.
We conclude the proof with a combinatorial argument invoking Sanov’s theorem.
For
n\in\mathbf{N}^{\mathcal{Z}}
, we denote
\hat{M}_{(n)}
the observed model under the deterministic number of visits
N_{t}=n
; Note that
\hat{M}_{t}=\hat{M}_{(N_{t})}
by definition.
Let
\mathcal{M}_{(n)}
the discrete space of Markov decision processes where rewards and kernels at
z\in\mathcal{Z}
are all the possible empirical distributions that can be obtained after
n(z)
samples.
Further denote
[\log^{2}(t)]:=\{0,\ldots,\lfloor\log^{2}(T)\rfloor\}
.
We have

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\mathbf{1}(z_{0}\in\mathcal{Z}^{**}_{\epsilon\mathrm{lvl}}(\hat{M}_{t}),t\in\mathcal{T}^{+}_{0},\mathcal{E}_{t},\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{t})\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{\leq}\mathbf{E}\left[\mathbf{1}\left(\psi_{N^{\prime}_{t}}(\hat{M}_{t}||M)\geq(1+\epsilon_{\mathrm{tst}}(t))\log(t)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(\ddagger)}{=}\sum_{n\in[\log^{2}(t)]^{\mathcal{Z}}}\mathbf{E}\left[\mathbf{1}\left(\psi_{n}(\hat{M}_{t}||M)\geq(1+\epsilon_{\mathrm{tst}}(t))\log(t),\atop(\forall z\notin\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{skel}}_{t},N_{t}(z)=n(z)),(\forall z\in\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{skel}}_{t},n(z)=0)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\in[\log^{2}(t)]^{\mathcal{Z}}}\sum_{M^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{(n)}}\mathbf{E}\left[\mathbf{1}(\psi_{n}(M^{\prime}||M)\geq(1+\epsilon_{\mathrm{tst}}(t))\log(t))\mathbf{1}(\hat{M}_{(n)}=M^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\in[\log^{2}(t)]^{\mathcal{Z}}}\sum_{M^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{(n)}}\mathbf{1}(\psi_{n}(M^{\prime}||M)\geq(1+\epsilon_{\mathrm{tst}}(t))\log(t))\mathbf{P}\left(\hat{M}_{(n)}=M^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\lx@sectionsign)}{\leq}\sum_{n\in[\log^{2}(t)]^{\mathcal{Z}}}\sum_{M^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{(n)}}\mathbf{1}\left(\psi_{n}(M^{\prime}||M)\geq(1+\epsilon_{\mathrm{tst}}(t))\log(t)\right)\exp\Big(-\psi_{n}(M^{\prime}||M)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n\in[\log^{2}(t)]^{\mathcal{Z}}}\sum_{M^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{(n)}}\exp\Big(-(1+\epsilon_{\mathrm{tst}}(t))\log(t)\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{(\$)}{\leq}\left(1+\lfloor\log^{2}(t)\rfloor\right)^{\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert(3+\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert)}\left(\frac{1}{t}\right)^{1+\epsilon\mathrm{tst}(t)}

\displaystyle\overset{(\#)}{\leq}\exp\left(-\left(1+\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{\mathrm{tst}}(t)\right)\log(t)\right).

In the above,

(\dagger)
is obtained by invoking (
C.11
);

(\ddagger)
follows by construction of
N^{\prime}_{t}
;

(\lx@sectionsign)
is a consequence of an all-time Sanov theorem (
Lemma

C.28
);

(\$)
follows from classic combinatorial bounds (
Lemma

C.29
); and

(\#)
is obtained by using
\epsilon_{\mathrm{tst}}(t)\geq 4\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert(3+\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert)\log\log(t)\log(t)^{-1}
(see
6
).

\blacksquare

We conclude the proof of
Lemma

C.5
by summing (
C.9
) of (
S
TEP
2
) for
t\geq 1
.

\blacksquare

C.2.4
Uniform exploitation: Fundamental property of co-exploration

Before moving on with the control of the dominant in the decomposition (
C.4
), we establish a fundamental property of the exploitation mechanism of
ECoE
, that guarantees that all components of a co-exploration structure
\mathfrak{Z}_{0}
are explored uniformly often relatively to the number of initial exploitation times with the so-called structure, see
Lemma

C.6
.
This result holds whether the structure is closed or not.

Lemma C.6
.

Let
\mathfrak{Z}_{0}
be a co-exploration structure.
Let
k(i)
be the
i
-th phase with co-exploration structure
\mathfrak{Z}_{0}
, i.e.,
\mathcal{T}_{0}^{+}(\mathfrak{Z}_{0})=\{t_{k(1)},t_{k(2)},\ldots\}
.
There exists
\alpha>0
such that

\mathbf{P}\left(\exists z\in{\textstyle\bigcup\mathfrak{Z}_{0}},\penalty\ N_{t_{k(i)}}(z)<\alpha\sqrt{i}\right)=\oh\left(i^{-2}\right)\hskip 10.00002pt\text{as $i\to\infty$.}

(C.12)

Proof.

The co-exploration structure is decomposed into its communicating components (in
\hat{M}_{t}
) as
\mathfrak{Z}_{0}\equiv(\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{1},\ldots,\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{c})
.
Remark that
\mathcal{T}^{+}_{0}(\mathfrak{Z}_{0})=\{t_{k(i)}:i\geq 1\}
and that
\mathcal{T}^{+}(\mathfrak{Z}_{0})=\bigcup_{i=1}^{\infty}\{t_{k(i)},\ldots,t_{k(i)+1}-1\}
.
Given a component
\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{u}
of
\mathfrak{Z}_{0}
, we write
\mathcal{I}^{u}:=\{i:S_{t_{k(i)}}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{u})\}
the indices of initial exploitation times starting in the component
u=1,\ldots,c
.

(
S
TEP
1
)

There is
\alpha>0
such that for all component
u\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
and
z_{u}\in\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{u}
, we have

\mathbf{P}\left(N_{t_{k(i)+1}}(z_{u})<\alpha\ell\text{\penalty\ and\penalty\ }\ell\leq\lvert\mathcal{I}^{u}\cap\{1,\ldots,i\}\rvert\right)=\oh(\ell^{-2})\hskip 10.00002pt\text{as\penalty\ $\ell\to\infty$}

(C.13)

Proof.

The proof shares many similarities with
Lemma

C.3
.

Fix a component
u\in\{1,\ldots,c\}
and
z_{u}\in\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{u}
.
Introduce the reward function
f(s,a):=\mathbf{1}((s,a)=z_{u})+\mathbf{1}(s\notin\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{u}))
.
Let
g^{f}
,
b^{f}
be the gain and bias functions for the reward function
f
obtained by iterating the uniform policy on
\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{u}
, extended to the uniform policy outside of
\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{u})
.
Let
\Delta^{f}(s,a)=g^{f}(s)+b^{f}(s)-f(s,a)-p(s,a)b^{f}
be the associated gap function.
Note that
\mathrm{sp}(g^{f})=0
regardless of whether
\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{u}
is closed (
Definition

C.4
) or not.
Denote
\alpha:=\min(g^{f})>0
and
\beta:=\max\{\mathrm{sp}(b^{f}),\lVert\Delta^{f}\rVert_{\infty}\}<\infty
.
Denoting
[i]:=\{1,\ldots,i\}
, we have

\displaystyle N_{t_{k(i)+1}}(z_{u})

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{j\in\mathcal{I}^{u}\cap[i]}\sum_{t=t_{k(j)}}^{t_{k(j)+1}-1}f(Z_{t})

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{=}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{I}^{u}\cap[i]}\sum_{t=t_{k(j)}}^{t_{k(j)+1}-1}\left(g^{f}(S_{t})+\left(e_{S_{t}}-p(Z_{t})\right)b^{f}-\Delta^{f}(Z_{t})\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\ddagger)}{\geq}\alpha\left(\sum_{j\in\mathcal{I}^{u}\cap[i]}\left(t_{k(j)+1}-t_{k(j)}\right)\right)-\beta+\sum_{j\in\mathcal{I}^{u}\cap[i]}\sum_{t=t_{k(j)}}^{t_{k(j)+1}-1}\left(\left(e_{S_{t+1}}-p(Z_{t})\right)b^{f}-\Delta^{f}(Z_{t})\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\lx@sectionsign)}{\geq}\alpha T_{i}^{u}-2\beta\left(1+\sqrt{T_{i}^{u}\log\left(\frac{1+T_{i}^{u}}{\delta}\right)}\right)

where

(\dagger)
follows by
g^{f}(s)+b^{f}(s)=f(s,a)+p(s,a)b^{f}+\Delta^{f}(s,a)
;

(\ddagger)
uses that by design of panic times, at most one episode
k(j)
isn’t regenerative, i.e., is such that
S_{t_{k(j)}}\neq S_{t_{k(j)+1}}
; and

(\lx@sectionsign)
bounds the RHS martingale with a time-uniform Azuma-Hoeffding inequality (
Lemma

C.26
) with probability
1-\delta
, and
T_{i}^{u}
is a short-hand for
\sum_{j\in\mathcal{I}^{u}\cap[i]}(t_{k(j)+1}-t_{k(j)})
.
Remark that

\beta\sqrt{T_{i}^{u}\log\left(\frac{1+T_{i}^{u}}{\delta}\right)}<\frac{1}{4}\alpha T_{i}^{u}\iff\delta>\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha\sqrt{T_{i}^{u}}}{4\beta}+\log(1+T_{i}^{u})\right)\overset{(\dagger)}{=}\oh\left(\lvert\mathcal{I}^{u}\cap[i]\rvert^{-2}\right)

where
(\dagger)
holds after
T_{i}^{u}\geq\lvert\mathcal{I}^{u}\cap[i]\rvert
.
We conclude accordingly.

\blacksquare

(
S
TEP
2
)

There exists a constant
\alpha>0
such that

\forall i\geq 1,\forall z\in\bigcup\mathfrak{Z}_{0},\hskip 10.00002pt\mathbf{P}\left(N_{t_{k(i)}}<\alpha\sqrt{i}\right)=\oh\left(i^{-2}\right).

(C.14)

Proof.

Since
\mathfrak{Z}_{0}
has
c
components, the more visited component
u\in\{1,\ldots,c\}
is such that
\lvert\mathcal{I}^{u}\cap[i]\rvert\geq\lceil\frac{i}{c}\rceil
.
In particular and following from the square trick (
ST
), for some
j<i
, the component
u
has been chosen to start a exploitation phase, i.e., there exists
j\in\{1,\ldots,i-1\}
such that

\min\left\{N_{t_{k(j)}}(z):z\in\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{u}\right\}\leq\left(\min\left\{N_{t_{k(j)}}(z):z\in\bigcup\mathfrak{Z}_{0}\right\}\right)^{2}

(C.15)

and such that
\lvert\mathcal{I}^{u}\cap[j]\rvert\geq\lceil\frac{i}{c}\rceil-1
.
Meanwhile, by combining
\lvert\mathcal{I}^{u}\cap[j]\rvert\geq\lceil\frac{i}{c}\rceil-1\geq\frac{i-c}{c}
with (
C.13
) from (
S
TEP
1
), we readily get

\mathbf{P}\left(\log\min\left\{N_{t_{k(j)+1}}(z):z\in\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{u}\right\}\geq\log\left(\alpha\cdot\frac{i-m}{m}\right)\right)=1-\oh\left(i^{-2}\right).

(C.16)

Because visit counts are monotone with respect to time, combining (
C.15
) and (
C.16
), we obtain that with probability
1-\oh(i^{-2})
, we have

\displaystyle\min\left\{N_{t_{k(i)}}(z):z\in\bigcup\mathfrak{Z}_{0}\right\}

\displaystyle\geq\min\left\{N_{t_{k(j)+1}}(z):z\in\bigcup\mathfrak{Z}_{0}\right\}

\displaystyle\geq\exp\left(\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\alpha\cdot\frac{i-m}{m}\right)\right)=\Omega\left(\sqrt{i}\right).

This proves the claim.

\blacksquare

This concludes the proof of
Lemma

C.6
.

\blacksquare

C.2.5
Wrong exploitation with correct empirical kernel supports

We continue with the dominant term of wrong exploitation, corresponding to exploitation times with
z_{0}\in\mathcal{Z}^{**}_{\epsilon\mathrm{lvl}}(\hat{M}_{t})
, wrong data on leveled optimal pairs
\mathcal{Z}^{**}_{\epsilon\mathrm{lvl}}(\hat{M}_{t})
yet
t
an initial exploitation time with closed co-exploration structure.
Because the co-exploration mechanism guarantees that all components of
\mathfrak{Z}_{0}
are explored nearly uniformly fast (up to a square root), the empirical data on
\mathcal{Z}^{**}_{\epsilon\mathrm{lvl}}(\hat{M}_{t})
becomes uniformly approximately correct quickly.
Therefore, the amount of such initial exploitation times is determined by the required precision of the empirical data on
\mathcal{Z}^{**}_{\epsilon\mathrm{lvl}}(\hat{M}_{t})
in
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{t}
, which is of order
{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}(t)
.

We end up with a bound of order
{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}(T)^{-6}
, see
Lemma

C.7
thereafter.

Lemma C.7
.

Let
z_{0}\notin\mathcal{Z}^{**}(M)
.
Let
\mathcal{E}_{t},\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{t}
be the good events as given by (
C.3
).
For all closed co-exploration structure
\mathfrak{Z}_{0}
, we have

\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\mathbf{1}\left(z_{0}\in\mathcal{Z}^{**}_{\epsilon\mathrm{lvl}(t)}(\hat{M}_{t}),\atop t\in\mathcal{T}^{+}_{0}(\mathfrak{Z}_{0}),(\mathcal{E}_{t}^{\prime})^{c}\right)\right]=\OH\left(\frac{1}{{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}(T)^{6}}\right).

Proof.

Fix
\mathfrak{Z}_{0}\equiv(\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{1},\ldots,\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{c})
a closed co-exploration structure.
Recall that
\mathcal{T}^{+}_{0}(\mathfrak{Z}_{0}):=\mathcal{T}^{+}(\mathfrak{Z}_{0})\cap\mathcal{T}^{+}_{0}
denotes the initial exploitation times with co-exploration structure
\mathfrak{Z}_{0}
.
By definition, see (
C.5
),
\mathcal{T}^{+}(\mathfrak{Z}_{0})
is a union of phases
\{t_{k},\ldots,t_{k+1}-1\}
and we denote
k(i)
the
i
-th phase for which
t_{k(i)}\in\mathcal{T}^{+}(\mathfrak{Z}_{0})
.
We have

\mathcal{T}^{+}_{0}(\mathfrak{Z}_{0})=\{t_{k(i)}:i\geq 1\}\text{\hskip 10.00002ptand \hskip 10.00002pt}\mathcal{T}^{+}(\mathfrak{Z}_{0})=\bigcup_{i=1}^{\infty}\{t_{k(i)},\ldots,t_{k(i)+1}-1\}.

Given a component
\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{u}
of
\mathfrak{Z}_{0}
, we write
\mathcal{I}^{u}:=\{i:S_{t_{k(i)}}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{u})\}
the indices of initial exploitation times starting in the component
u=1,\ldots,c
.

(
S
TEP
1
)

There exists a constant
\alpha>0
such that, as
T\to\infty
, we have

\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\mathbf{1}\left(t\in\mathcal{T}^{+}_{0}(\mathfrak{Z}_{0}),\atop(\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{t})^{c}\right)\right]\leq\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum_{z\in\bigcup\mathfrak{Z}_{0}}\mathbf{1}\left(C(M)\lVert\hat{M}_{t_{k(j)}}(z)-M(z)\rVert^{*}\geq{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}(T),\atop N_{t_{k(j)}}(z)>\alpha\sqrt{j}\right)\right]+\frac{1}{\alpha}.

Proof.

This follows from the following direction computation:

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\mathbf{1}(t\in\mathcal{T}^{+}_{0}(\mathfrak{Z}_{0}),(\mathcal{E}_{t}^{\prime})^{c})\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}(t_{k(j)}<T,(\mathcal{E}_{t_{k(j)}}^{\prime})^{c})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}\left(t_{k(j)}<T,(\mathcal{E}_{t_{k(j)}}^{\prime})^{c},\atop(\forall z\in\bigcup\mathfrak{Z}_{0},N_{t_{k(j)}}(z)\geq\alpha\sqrt{j})\right)\right]+\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}(\exists z\in{\textstyle\bigcup\mathfrak{Z}_{0}},N_{t_{k(j)}}(z)<\alpha\sqrt{j})\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{=}\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}\left(t_{k(j)}<T,{(\exists z\in\bigcup\mathfrak{Z}_{0},\kappa(M)\lVert\hat{M}_{t_{k(j)}}(z)-M(z)\rVert^{*}\geq{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}(t_{k(j)}))\atop(\forall z\in\bigcup\mathfrak{Z}_{0},N_{t_{k(j)}}(z)\geq\alpha\sqrt{j})}\right)\right]+\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\oh\left(\frac{1}{j^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\ddagger)}{\leq}\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}\left(t_{k(j)}<T,{(\exists z\in\bigcup\mathfrak{Z}_{0},\kappa(M)\lVert\hat{M}_{t_{k(j)}}(z)-M(z)\rVert^{*}\geq{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}(T))\atop(\forall z\in\bigcup\mathfrak{Z}_{0},N_{t_{k(j)}}(z)\geq\alpha\sqrt{j})}\right)\right]+\OH(1)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum_{z\in\bigcup\mathfrak{Z}_{0}}\mathbf{1}\left(\kappa(M)\lVert\hat{M}_{t_{k(j)}}(z)-M(z)\rVert^{*}\geq{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}(T)\atop N_{t_{k(j)}}(z)>\alpha\sqrt{j}\right)\right]+\OH(1)

where

(\dagger)
unfolds the definition of
(\mathcal{E}_{\tau_{j}}^{\prime})^{c}
and invokes
Lemma

C.6
; and

(\ddagger)
follows by monotonicity of
{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}
.
This proves the claim.

\blacksquare

Continuing from (
S
TEP
1
), we prove
Lemma

C.7
.
We write

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\mathbf{1}(t\in\mathcal{T}^{+}_{0}(\mathfrak{Z}_{0}),(\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{t})^{c})\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{\leq}\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum_{z\in\bigcup\mathfrak{Z}_{0}}\mathbf{1}\left(C(M)\lVert\hat{M}_{t_{k(j)}}(z)-M(z)\rVert^{*}\geq{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}(T),\atop N_{t_{k(j)}}(z)>\alpha\sqrt{j}\right)\right]+\OH(1)

\displaystyle\overset{(\ddagger)}{=}\sum_{z\in\bigcup\mathfrak{Z}_{0}}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\mathbf{P}\left(C(M)\lVert\hat{M}_{t_{k(j)}}(z)-M(z)\rVert\geq{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}(T),\atop N_{t_{k(j)}}(z)>\alpha\sqrt{j}\right)+\OH(1)

\displaystyle\overset{(\lx@sectionsign)}{\leq}\sum_{z\in\bigcup\mathfrak{Z}_{0}}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\min\left\{1,\exp\left(-\frac{2\alpha{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}(T)^{2}\sqrt{j}}{\kappa(M)^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}\log\left(1+\alpha\sqrt{j}\right)+(\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert+1)\log(2)\right)\right\}+\OH(1)

where

(\dagger)
follows from (
S
TEP
1
);

(\ddagger)
converts
\lVert-\rVert^{*}
to
\lVert-\rVert
using
Lemma

C.25
; and

(\lx@sectionsign)
follows by
Corollary

C.24
.
Setting
\lambda=2{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}(T)^{2}\kappa(M)^{-2}
, we have to upper-bound a term of the form

B:=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\min\left\{1,\left(1+\alpha\sqrt{n}\right)\exp\left(-\lambda\sqrt{n}\right)\right\}

We have

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\alpha}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\min\left\{\sqrt{n}\exp\left(-\lambda\sqrt{n}\right),1\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\alpha}\left(\left\lceil\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\right\rceil+\sum_{n>\lceil\lambda^{-2}\rceil}\sqrt{n}\exp\left(-\lambda\sqrt{n}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\ddagger)}{\leq}\frac{2}{\alpha}\left(\left\lceil\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\right\rceil+\int_{0}^{\infty}\sqrt{x}\exp\left(-\lambda\sqrt{x}\right)\mathrm{d}x\right)=\frac{2}{\alpha}\left(\left\lceil\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\right\rceil+\frac{4}{\lambda^{3}}\right)=\OH\left(\frac{1}{{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}(T)^{6}}\right)

where
(\dagger)
follows by a sum-integral comparison, using that
f(x):=\sqrt{x}\exp(-\lambda\sqrt{x})
is decreasing on
[\lambda^{-2},\infty)
.
This concludes the proof.

\blacksquare

C.2.6
Wrong exploitation with wrong empirical kernel supports

The proof of
Lemma

C.7
can be generalized to non-closed structures.
For non-closed structures however, we can provide a better bound than
{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}(T)^{-6}
and show that such structures are all rejected in finite time.
The idea is simple: If
\mathfrak{Z}_{0}
is not closed in
M
, it has a non-closed component
\mathcal{Z}_{0}
and every time the algorithm exploits this component, it has positive probability to escape from it and to panic accordingly—and thanks to the co-exploration mechanism, (
Lemma

C.6
), this component is visited about as many times as the algorithm exploits with co-exploration structure
\mathfrak{Z}_{0}
.
See
Lemma

C.8
below.

Lemma C.8
.

Let
z_{0}\notin\mathcal{Z}^{**}(M)
.
Let
\mathcal{E}_{t},\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{t}
be the good events as given by (
C.3
).
For all non-closed co-exploration structure
\mathfrak{Z}_{0}
, we have

\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}\left(z_{0}\in\mathcal{Z}^{**}_{\epsilon\mathrm{lvl}(t)}(\hat{M}_{t}),\atop t\in\mathcal{T}^{+}_{0}(\mathfrak{Z}_{0}),(\mathcal{E}_{t}^{\prime})^{c}\right)\right]<\infty.

Proof.

Fix
\mathfrak{Z}_{0}\equiv(\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{1},\ldots,\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{c})
a
non
closed exploration structure, meaning that there is
\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{u}\in\mathfrak{Z}_{0}
together with some
z\in\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{u}
such that
\support(p(z))\not\subseteq\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{u})
, i.e., playing
z\in\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{u}
makes one exit the states spawned by
\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{u}
with positive probability.
Let
\mathcal{T}_{0}^{+}(\mathfrak{Z}_{0}):=\mathcal{T}^{+}(\mathfrak{Z}_{0})\cap\mathcal{T}_{0}^{+}
be the initial exploitation times with co-exploration structure
\mathfrak{Z}_{0}
.
By definition, see (
C.5
),
\mathcal{T}^{+}(\mathfrak{Z}_{0})
is a union of phases
\{t_{k},\ldots,t_{k+1}-1\}
and we denote
k(i)
the
i
-th phase for which
t_{k(i)}\in\mathcal{T}^{+}(\mathfrak{Z}_{0})
.
We have

\mathcal{T}^{+}_{0}(\mathfrak{Z}_{0})=\{t_{k(i)}:i\geq 1\}\text{\hskip 10.00002ptand \hskip 10.00002pt}\mathcal{T}^{+}(\mathfrak{Z}_{0})=\bigcup_{i=1}^{\infty}\{t_{k(i)},\ldots,t_{k(i)+1}-1\}.

Given a component
\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{u}
of
\mathfrak{Z}_{0}
, we write
\mathcal{I}^{u}:=\{i:S_{t_{k(i)}}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{u})\}
the indices of initial exploitation times starting in the component
u=1,\ldots,c
.

(
S
TEP
1
)

Let
\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{u}\in\mathfrak{Z}_{0}
be a component that is not closed.
There exists
C>0
such that, for all
x\geq 0
, we have

\mathbf{P}\left(\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}^{u}}\left(t_{k(i)+1}-t_{k(i)}\right)\geq x\right)\leq\exp\left(-Cx+\frac{1}{C}\right)

(C.17)

Proof.

Let
T_{i}^{u}:=\sum_{j\in\mathcal{I}^{u}\cap[i]}(t_{k(j)+1}-t_{k(j)})
the time spent on component
\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{u}
.
Consider the reward function
f(s,a):=\mathbf{1}(s\notin\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{u}))
and let
g^{f}
,
b^{f}
be the gain and bias functions for ther reward function
f
obtained by iterating the uniform policy
\pi_{u}
on
\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{u}
extended to the uniform policy on
\mathcal{A}
outside of
\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{u})
.
As usual, observe that
\mathrm{sp}(g^{f})=0
and that
0<\alpha:=\min(g^{f}),\beta:=\mathrm{sp}(b^{f})<\infty
.
By design of panic times, we cannot have
f(Z_{t})=1
for
t=t_{k(i)},\ldots,t_{k(i)+1}-1
if
i\in\mathcal{I}^{u}
.
We deduce that

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in\mathcal{I}^{u}\cap[i]}\sum_{t=t_{k(j)}}^{t_{k(j)+1}-1}f(Z_{t})

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{=}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{I}^{u}\cap[i]}\sum_{t=t_{k(j)}}^{t_{k(j)+1}-1}\left(g^{f}(S_{t})+\left(e_{S_{t}}-p^{\pi_{u}}(S_{t})\right)b^{f}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\ddagger)}{\geq}\alpha T_{i}^{u}-\beta+\sum_{j\in\mathcal{I}^{u}\cap[i]}\sum_{t=t_{k(j)}}^{t_{k(j)+1}-1}\left(e_{S_{t+1}}-p^{\pi_{u}}(S_{t})\right)b^{f}\overset{(\lx@sectionsign)}{\geq}\alpha T_{i}^{u}-\beta\left(1+\sqrt{T_{i}^{u}\log\left(\frac{1+T_{i}^{u}}{\delta}\right)}\right)

where

(\dagger)
follows by the Poisson equation
g^{f}(s)+b^{f}(s)=f^{\pi_{u}}(s)+p^{\pi_{u}}(s)b^{f}
;

(\ddagger)
uses that by design of panic times, at most one episodes
k(j)
isn’t regenerative;
and
(\lx@sectionsign)
bounds the RHS martingale with a time-uniform Azuma-Hoeffding’s inequality (
Lemma

C.26
) and holds with probability
1-\delta
.
By letting
i
go to infinity, we see that
T^{u}:=\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}^{u}}(t_{k(i)+1}-t_{k(i)})
satisfies

\forall\delta\geq 0,\hskip 10.00002pt\mathbf{P}\left(T^{u}\leq\frac{\alpha}{\beta}\left(1+\sqrt{T^{u}\log\left(\frac{1+T^{u}}{\delta}\right)}\right)\right)\leq\delta

and we conclude with straight-forward algebra that
T^{u}
has sub-exponential tails.

\blacksquare

We now prove
Lemma

C.8
.
Let
u\in\{1,\ldots,c\}
be such that
\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{u}
is not closed.
We have

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}(t\in\mathcal{T}^{+}_{0}(\mathfrak{Z}_{0}))\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}(t_{k(j)}<\infty)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{=}\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}\left(t_{k(j)}<\infty,\left(\forall z\in{\textstyle\bigcup\mathfrak{Z}_{0}},N_{t_{k(j)}}(z)\geq\alpha\sqrt{j}\right)\right)\right]+\OH(1)

\displaystyle\overset{(\ddagger)}{\leq}\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}\left(T^{u}\geq\alpha\sqrt{j}\right)\right]+\OH(1)

\displaystyle\overset{(\lx@sectionsign)}{\leq}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha}{C}\sqrt{j}+\frac{1}{C}\right)+\OH(1)<\infty

where

(\dagger)
follows by
Lemma

C.6
;

(\ddagger)
introduces
T^{u}:=\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}^{u}}(t_{k(i)}-t_{k(i)})
; and

(\lx@sectionsign)
follows from (
S
TEP
1
);
This concludes the proof.

\blacksquare

C.2.7
Correctness of empirical data on the skeleton

In this paragraph, we bound the last term in (
C.4
), accounting for wrong empirical data on the skeleton.
The idea of the argument is that since a pair belongs to the skeleton if, and only if
N_{t}(z)\geq\log^{2}(t)
, the associated empirical data concentrates very fast.

Lemma C.9
.

Let
\mathcal{E}_{t}
be the good event as given by (
C.3
).
We have

\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}(\mathcal{E}_{t}^{c})\right]<\infty.

Proof.

Note that by
Lemma

C.25
,
\lVert M^{\prime}(z)-M(z)\rVert^{*}=\lVert M^{\prime}(z)-M(z)\rVert
if
\lVert M^{\prime}(z)-M(z)\rVert<\mathrm{dmin}(M)
.
Therefore, the event
\mathcal{E}_{t}
holds if for all
z\in\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{skel}}_{t}
, we have

\lVert\hat{M}_{t}(z)-M(z)\rVert<\frac{{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}(t)}{\kappa(M)}\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002pt{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}(t)<\min\left\{\frac{\Delta^{g}(M)}{3},\kappa(M)\mathrm{dmin}(M)\right\}.

(C.18)

By
6
(
A0
), we have
{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}(t)\to 0
as
t\to\infty
, so the second requirement of (
C.18
) is satisfied as soon as
t
is large enough, say
t\geq\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}
for some
\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}\in\mathbf{N}
.
So,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}(\mathcal{E}_{t}^{c})\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{\leq}\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}+\sum_{t=\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}}^{\infty}\mathbf{P}\left(\exists z\in\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{skel}}_{t},\lVert\hat{M}_{t}(z)-M(z)\rVert\leq\frac{{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}(t)}{\kappa(M)}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\ddagger)}{=}\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}+\sum_{t=\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}}^{\infty}\mathbf{P}\left(\exists z\in\mathcal{Z},\lVert\hat{M}_{t}(z)-M(z)\rVert\leq\frac{{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}(t)}{\kappa(M)}\text{\penalty\ and\penalty\ }N_{t}(z)\geq\log^{2}(t)\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\lx@sectionsign)}{\leq}\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}+\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\sum_{t=\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}}^{\infty}\exp\left(-\frac{2\log^{2}(t){\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}(t)^{2}}{\kappa(M)^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}\log\left(1+\log^{2}(t)\right)+\log\left(2^{\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert+1}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\$)}{\leq}\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}+\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\sum_{t=\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}}^{\infty}\exp\left(-\log^{\frac{3}{2}}(t)+\oh\left(\log^{\frac{3}{2}}(t)\right)\right)=\OH(1)

where

(\dagger)
follows by definition of
\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}
;

(\ddagger)
follows by definition of the skeleton
\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{skel}}_{t}:=\{z\in\mathcal{Z}:N_{t}(z)\geq\log^{2}(t)\}
;

(\lx@sectionsign)
follows from concentration results (
Corollary

C.24
); and

(\$)
follows from
6
(
A2
), that guarantees that
2\log^{2}(t){\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}(t)^{2}\kappa(M)^{-2}\geq\log^{\frac{3}{2}}(t)
for
t
large enough.
This concludes the proof.

\blacksquare

C.2.8
Proof of
Lemma

C.2
: Putting everything together

We finally prove
Lemma

C.2
.
We have

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\mathbf{1}(Z_{t}=z_{0},t\in\mathcal{T}^{+})\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{=}\OH\left(\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\mathbf{1}\left(z_{0}\in\mathcal{Z}^{**}_{\epsilon\mathrm{lvl}}(\hat{M}_{t})\atop t\in\mathcal{T}_{0}^{+}\right)\right]\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\ddagger)}{\leq}\OH\left(\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\mathbf{1}\left(z_{0}\in\mathcal{Z}^{**}_{\epsilon\mathrm{lvl}}(\hat{M}_{t})\atop t\in\mathcal{T}_{0}^{+},\mathcal{E}_{t},\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{t}\right)\right]+\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\mathbf{1}\left(z_{0}\in\mathcal{Z}^{**}_{\epsilon\mathrm{lvl}}(\hat{M}_{t})\atop t\in\mathcal{T}_{0}^{+},(\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{t})^{c}\right)\right]+\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\mathbf{1}(\mathcal{E}_{t}^{c})\right]\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\lx@sectionsign)}{=}\OH\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\left(\frac{1}{t}\right)^{1+\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{\mathrm{tst}}(t)}\right)+\OH\left(\sum_{\mathfrak{Z}_{0}}\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\mathbf{1}\left(z_{0}\in\mathcal{Z}^{**}_{\epsilon\mathrm{lvl}}(\hat{M}_{t})\atop t\in\mathcal{T}_{0}^{+}(\mathfrak{Z}_{0}),(\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{t})^{c}\right)\right]\right)+\OH(1)

\displaystyle\overset{(\$)}{=}\OH\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\left(\frac{1}{t}\right)^{1+\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{\mathrm{tst}}(t)}\right)+\OH\left(\frac{1}{{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}(T)^{6}}\right)+\OH(1)

where

(\dagger)
follows from
Lemma

C.3
;

(\ddagger)
invokes the decomposition from (
C.4
);

(\lx@sectionsign)
bounds the first term using
Lemma

C.5
, the second is decomposed along the finitely many possible co-exploration structures
\mathfrak{Z}_{0}
and the third using
Lemma

C.9
; and

(\$)
bounds the last term using
Lemma

C.7
and
Lemma

C.8
depending on whether
\mathfrak{Z}_{0}
is closed or not.

\blacksquare

C.3
Amount of exploration

Let
\mu^{*}
be the central optimal exploration probability measure of
M
(
Definition

B.19
), that is the limit point of optimal
{\bar{\epsilon}}
-regularized exploration measures
\mu^{*}_{\bar{\epsilon}}
of
M
as
{\bar{\epsilon}}\to 0
in the sense of
Proposition

B.18
.
Recall that
C(\mu^{*},M)
denotes the information value of
\mu^{*}
in
M
(
Definition

B.3
).
The whole section is dedicated to the proof
Lemma

C.10
below, that bounds the number of times a pair is being pulled during exploration.

Lemma C.10
.

Let
z\in\mathcal{Z}
be an arbitrary state-action pair.
As
T\to\infty
, we have

\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\mathbf{1}(Z_{t}=z,t\in\mathcal{T}^{-})\right]\leq\frac{\mu^{*}(z)\log(T)}{C(\mu^{*},M)}+\oh(\log(T)).

(C.19)

C.3.1
Idea of the proof

In
Figure

6
, we provide a high level representation of how exploration is decomposed and analyzed.
Overall, the set of exploration times
\{t\in\mathcal{T}^{-}:t\leq T\}
is split into five periods: The “burn-in” period, the dominant “near-optimal exploration” period, the “co-exploration travels” period, a “first order failure” period and a “second order failure” period.

\lvert\mathcal{T}^{-}\rvert
0
\displaystyle\epsilon_{0}\log(T)\atop\displaystyle\epsilon_{0}\ll 1
C(\mu^{*},M)^{-1}\log(T)
\log^{3}(T)
Burn-In
Near Optimal Exploration
Co-Exploration Travels
Most likely value
1st failure
\mathbf{P}=\oh\left(e^{-\sqrt{\log(T)}}\right)
2nd failure
\mathbf{P}=\oh\left(T^{-2}\right)

Figure 6:

Global overview of the different exploration periods as
\lvert\mathcal{T}^{-}\rvert
increases.

We fix
\epsilon_{0}>0
a precision parameter.
Then, we begin by discarding what happens prior to time
\approx\epsilon_{0}\log(T)
, during the
Burn-In
period.
Past the burn-in period, we show that the empirical data is quite likely to concentrate well-enough so that the algorithm approximates the exploration measure and information values correctly.
This phenomenon is called the
trigger effect
(
Section

C.3.4
).
It is made possible by the uniform exploration guarantees of
ECoE*
allowed by the
\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}
-regularization of its exploration policies, that makes sure that all pairs are covered uniformly fast during exploration (
Section

C.3.3
).
Following the burn-in period,
ECoE*
enters the
Near Optimal Exploration
period during which it explores near-optimally (
Section

C.3.5
).
During this period and because of the trigger effect,
ECoE*
further estimates the information values well-enough so that it does not overshot the duration of exploration.
After about
C(\mu^{*},M)^{-1}\log(T)
exploration times, GLR exploration tests (
16
) do not provoke exploration phases anymore and
ECoE*
enters the
Co-Exploration travels
period.
During this period, exploration phases can only be triggered by the co-exploration rule, for a total number of exploration phases that is negligible in front of
\log(T)
(
Section

C.3.9
).
Combining everything so far, we deduce that the number of exploration phases is quite likely to remain below
C(\mu^{*},M)^{-1}\log(T)
(
Sections

C.3.6
and
C.3.9
).

If
\lvert\mathcal{T}^{-}\rvert
exceeds
C(\mu^{*},M)^{-1}\log(T)
significatively, it essentially means that the trigger effect failed to trigger after the burn-in period.
This is the
First Order Failure
period, that
ECoE*
only enters with probability
\oh(\exp(-\sqrt{\log(T)}))
.
We show that at horizon
\OH(\log^{3}(T))
and thanks to uniform exploration again, all pairs enter the skeleton
\{z\in\mathcal{Z}:N_{t}(z)\geq\log^{2}(t)\}
and
ECoE*
naturally stops to explore by design.
Because
\log^{3}(T)\exp(-\sqrt{\log(T)})\to 0
when
T\to\infty
, the number of exploration times induced by the first order failure period is negligible in expectation (
Section

C.3.7
).
This is the
skeleton barrier
.
Beyond that barrier, the algorithm only continues to explore if pairs failed to entered the skeleton.
This is the
Second Order Failure
period, that has so little probability to happen that its is completely negligible in expectation.

Everything is compiled in
Section

C.3.8
for a proof of
Lemma

C.10
.

C.3.2
Preliminaries: Exploration visit counts and truncated regularization

The
exploration visit count
of
z
at time
t\geq 1
is given by

N_{t}^{-}(z):=\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}\mathbf{1}(Z_{i}=z,i\in\mathcal{T}^{-}).

(C.20)

We write
(\tau_{\ell})
the stopping-time enumeration of exploration times
\mathcal{T}^{-}
, i.e.,
\tau_{1}:=\inf\mathcal{T}^{-}
and
\tau_{\ell+1}:=\inf\{t>\tau_{\ell}:t\in\mathcal{T}^{-}\}
.
Note that
N_{\tau_{\ell}}^{-}(z)=\sum_{i=1}^{\ell-1}\mathbf{1}(Z_{\tau_{i}}=z)
for
\ell\geq 1
.
Given
T\geq 1
, we denote
{\bar{\epsilon}}^{\prime\prime}
the
T
-truncated
regularization parameter, given by

\begin{gathered}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}^{\prime\prime}(t):=\max\left\{{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}^{\prime}(t),{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}^{\prime}(T)\right\},\qquad\qquad\epsilon_{\mathrm{tst}}^{\prime\prime}(t):=\epsilon_{\mathrm{tst}}^{\prime}(t),\\
\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}^{\prime\prime}(m):=\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}^{\prime}(m),\qquad\qquad\epsilon_{\mathrm{conv}}^{\prime\prime}(m):=\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}^{\prime}(m).\end{gathered}

(C.21)

That is,
{\bar{\epsilon}}^{\prime\prime}
and
{\bar{\epsilon}}^{\prime}
only differ at
{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}
.

C.3.3
Part I: Uniform exploration guarantees

We begin this adventure by establishing the uniform exploration guarantees of
ECoE*
, that are crucial to prove lower bound the likeliness of the trigger effect in
Section

C.3.4
and control the skeleton barrier in
Section

C.3.7
.
In
Lemma

C.11
below, we provide a high probability bound of the uniform visit rates of
ECoE*
during exploration, as a function of the uniformization regularizer
\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}
.

Lemma C.11
.

Let
(\tau_{\ell})
be the stopping-time enumeration of
\mathcal{T}^{-}
, i.e.,
\tau_{1}:=\inf\mathcal{T}^{-}
and
\tau_{\ell+1}:=\inf\{t>\tau_{\ell}:t\in\mathcal{T}^{-}\}
.
If the regularizer
{\bar{\epsilon}}
satisfies
6
, there exist constants
\alpha,\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}>0
and a polynomial
\mathrm{poly}(-)
of degree
4\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert
such that

\mathbf{P}\left(\exists z\in\mathcal{Z},\exists\ell\geq m,\penalty\ N_{\tau_{\ell}}(z)<\alpha\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(\ell)^{\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\ell\right)\leq\exp\left(-\mathrm{poly}(\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m))m+\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}\right).

Proof.

Fix
\ell\geq m
and denote
\eta\equiv\eta(\ell):=\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(\ell)
for short.
We consider the Markov decision process
M_{\eta}:=(\mathcal{Z},\nu,p_{\eta})
with kernel given by

p_{\eta}(s,a):=\left(1-\eta\lvert\mathcal{A}(s)\rvert\right)p(s,a)+\eta\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}(s)}p(s,a^{\prime}).

The model
M_{\eta}
is communicating, because the execution of the fully uniform policy
\pi_{u}
on
M_{\eta}
is equivalent to the execution of the uniform policy on
M
, in the sense that
\mathbf{E}^{M,\pi_{u}}[-]=\mathbf{E}^{M_{\eta},\pi_{u}}[-]
.
By construction, the execution of any
\eta
-uniform policy
\pi
on
M
can be seen as the execution of a randomized policy
\pi_{\eta}
on
M_{\eta}
, with
\mathbf{E}^{\pi_{\eta},M}[-]=\mathbf{E}^{\pi,M_{\eta}}[-]
.

Fix
z_{0}\in\mathcal{Z}
and introduce the reward function
f(z):=\mathbf{1}(z=z_{0})
.
Consider the model
M_{\eta}^{-f}=(\mathcal{Z},-f,p_{\eta})
with kernel
p_{\eta}
and deterministic reward function
-f
.
Introduce
g^{-f}_{\eta}
,
b^{-f}_{\eta}
and
\Delta^{-f}_{\eta}
the associated optimal gain, bias an gap functions of
M_{\eta}^{-f}
.
Because
M_{\eta}^{-f}
is communicating, we have
\mathrm{sp}(g^{-f}_{\eta})=0
.
Let
\pi^{*}_{\eta}
be a deterministic bias optimal policy of
M_{\eta}^{-f}
.
By construction,
\pi^{*}_{\eta}
corresponds to an
\eta
-uniform policy of
M
under which all pairs are recurrent.
By
Lemma

A.12
, we have
\min(g^{-f}_{\eta})=\max(g^{-f}_{\eta})\leq-\alpha(\ell):=-\mathrm{dmin}(p)^{\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\eta^{\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}
.
Consider the gaps in
M
, given by

\Delta^{-f}(s,a):=g^{-f}_{\eta}(s)+b^{-f}_{\eta}(s)+f(s,a)-p(s,a)b^{-f}_{\eta}.

Following from the Bellman equations of
M_{\eta}^{-f}
, we check that every
\eta
-uniform policy
\pi
satisfies

\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}(s)}\pi_{\eta}(a|s)\Delta^{-f}_{\eta}(s,a)=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}(s)}\pi(a|s)\Delta^{-f}(s,a)\geq 0

(C.22)

where
\pi_{\eta}
is the randomized policy induced by
\pi
in
M_{\eta}
.
Introduce the function
\beta(\ell):=1+2\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert\mathrm{dmin}(p)^{-\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(\ell)^{-\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}
.
By
Lemma

A.12
, we have
\beta(\ell)\geq\max\{\mathrm{sp}(b^{-f}_{\eta}),\lVert\Delta^{-f}\rVert_{\infty}\}
.
We have

\displaystyle N_{\tau_{\ell}}(z_{0})=\sum_{t=1}^{\tau_{\ell}-1}\mathbf{1}(Z_{t}=z_{0})

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i=1}^{\ell-1}\mathbf{1}(Z_{\tau_{i}}=z_{0})

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{=}\sum_{i=1}^{\ell-1}\left(-g^{-f}_{\eta}(S_{\tau_{i}})+\left(p(Z_{\tau_{i}})-e_{S_{\tau_{i}}}\right)b^{-f}_{\eta}+\Delta^{-f}(Z_{\tau_{i}})\right)

\displaystyle=(\ell-1)\alpha(\ell)+\underbrace{\sum_{i=1}^{\ell-1}\left(p(Z_{\tau_{i}})-e_{S_{\tau_{i}}}\right)b^{-f}_{\eta}}_{\mathrm{A}_{1}}+\underbrace{\sum_{i=1}^{\ell-1}\Delta^{-f}(Z_{\tau_{i}})}_{\mathrm{A}_{2}}

where
(\dagger)
unfolds the definition of
\Delta^{-f}
.
We control the error terms as follows, starting with
\mathrm{A}_{1}
.
We have

\displaystyle\mathrm{A}_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{\ell-1}\left(b^{-f}_{\eta}(S_{\tau_{i}+1})-b^{-f}_{\eta}(S_{\tau_{i}})\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{\ell-1}\left(p(Z_{\tau_{i}})-e_{S_{\tau_{i}+1}}\right)b^{-f}_{\eta}

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{\geq}-\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert\beta(\ell)+\sum_{i=1}^{\ell-1}\left(p(Z_{\tau_{i}})-e_{S_{\tau_{i}+1}}\right)b^{-f}_{\eta}

where

(\dagger)
follows from the observation that, between exploration times, the algorithm is exploiting.
When it does, it does so until regeneration, hence coming back to the state where exploration was suspended; At the exception of phases killed by panicking, but there are at most
\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert
of them—one for each transition to be discovered, see
Lemma

C.22
.
The remaining right-hand term is a martingale that we leave for later.

We continue with
\mathrm{A}_{2}
.
We have

\displaystyle\mathrm{A}_{2}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{i=1}^{\ell-1}\Delta^{-f}(Z_{\tau_{i}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{\ell-1}\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}(S_{\tau_{i}})}\pi^{-}_{\tau_{i}}(a|S_{\tau_{i}})\Delta^{-f}(S_{\tau_{i}},a)+\sum_{i=1}^{\ell-1}\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}(S_{\tau_{i}})}\left(\mathbf{1}(A_{\tau_{i}}=a)-\pi^{-}_{\tau_{i}}(a|S_{\tau_{i}})\right)\Delta^{-f}(S_{\tau_{i}},a)

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{\geq}\sum_{i=1}^{\ell-1}\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}(S_{\tau_{i}})}\left(\mathbf{1}(A_{\tau_{i}}=a)-\pi^{-}_{\tau_{i}}(a|S_{\tau_{i}})\right)\Delta^{-f}(S_{\tau_{i}},a)

where

(\dagger)
follows from the observation that
\pi_{\tau_{i}}^{-}
is
\eta
-randomized (since
i\leq\ell
), that
\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}
is non-increasing by
6
(
A1
) combined with (
C.22
).
We conclude that
\mathrm{A}_{1}
and
\mathrm{A}_{2}
both involve martingales obtained as the sum of
\ell-1
martingale differences whose terms have span at most
\beta(\ell)
.
So, by a time-uniform Azuma-Hoeffing’s inequality (
Lemma

C.26
), we find that with probability
1-\delta
and uniformly for
\ell\geq m
, we have

N_{\tau_{\ell}}(z_{0})\geq\alpha(\ell-1)-\beta(\ell)\left(\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert+2\sqrt{(\ell-1)\log\left(\frac{1+\ell}{\delta}\right)}\right).

(C.23)

We conclude by solving
m\alpha(m)>4\beta(m)\sqrt{m\log((1+m)/\delta)}
in
\delta
.
We decouple the inequality by simplifying it to the pair of conditions (C1)
m\alpha(m)>4\beta(m)\sqrt{m\log(1+m)}
and (C2)
m\alpha(m)>4\beta(m)\sqrt{m\log(1/\delta)}
.
We know that
\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(\ell)=\omega(\log(\ell)^{-1})
by assumption, so that the condition (C1) is satisfied for
m
large enough, say
m\geq C_{\bar{\epsilon}}
.
As for the condition (C2), solving in
\delta
provides the condition

\delta\geq\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha(m)^{2}m}{\beta(m)^{2}}\right).

By assumption on
\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}
again, see
6
(
A6
), the function
m\mapsto\alpha(m)^{2}\beta(m)^{-2}m
is increasing provided that
m
is large enough, so provided that
m\geq C_{\bar{\epsilon}}
up to increasing
C_{\bar{\epsilon}}
.
In the end, we set

\delta:=\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha(m)^{2}m}{\beta(m)^{2}}+\mathbf{1}(m<C_{\epsilon})\right)\leq\exp\left(-\mathrm{poly}(\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m))m+C_{\bar{\epsilon}}\right)

where
\mathrm{poly}(-)
is of degree
4\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert
.

\blacksquare

C.3.4
Part II: Post burn-in properties & the trigger effect

In this section, we show that after a few exploration phases called the
burn-in
exploration phases, empirical data starts to concentrate in probability so that the algorithm explores near optimally: The exploration policy is near optimal and GLR exploration tests do not overshoot the required amount of information.

We refer this phenomenon to as the
trigger effect
.

Consider the function
\beta(\ell):=1+2\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert\mathrm{dmin}(p)^{-\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}^{\prime}(\ell)^{-\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}
and let
\delta(\ell):=\alpha\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(\ell)^{\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}
where
\alpha>0
is an alias for constant given by
Lemma

C.11
.
Recall that
\mathcal{P}_{\delta(\ell)}(\mathcal{Z})
is the set of probability distributions
\mu
over
\mathcal{Z}
such that
\min(\mu)\geq\delta(\ell)
.
Given a burn-in threshold
\epsilon_{0}>0
, we introduce the
trigger event
as

\mathcal{E}\equiv\mathcal{E}_{\epsilon_{0},T}:=\left(\forall\ell\geq\frac{\epsilon_{0}\log(T)}{\beta(\epsilon_{0}\log(T))}:\hskip-5.0pt\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{Z}^{**}_{\epsilon\mathrm{lvl}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell})}(\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}})=\mathcal{Z}^{**}(M)\mathrm{\penalty\ and\penalty\ }\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}}\sim M\mathrm{\penalty\ and\penalty\ }\\
\lVert\mu_{{\bar{\epsilon}}^{\prime\prime}}^{*}(\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}})-\mu^{*}_{{\bar{\epsilon}}^{\prime\prime}}(M)\rVert_{\infty}<\epsilon_{0}\beta(\ell)^{-2}\mathrm{\penalty\ and\penalty\ }\\
\sup_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{\delta(\ell)}(\mathcal{Z})}\left\lvert C_{{\bar{\epsilon}}^{\prime\prime}}(\mu,\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}})-C_{{\bar{\epsilon}}^{\prime\prime}}(\mu,M)\right\rvert<\epsilon_{0}\end{array}\hskip-5.0pt\right)

(C.24)

where
{\bar{\epsilon}}^{\prime\prime}
is the
T
-truncated regularization hyperparameter, see
Section

C.3.2
and (
C.21
).
It is to be noted that for
\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(\ell)=\omega(\log^{-1}(\ell))
, we have
\beta(\ell)\geq 1
and
\beta(\ell)=\oh(\log(\ell)^{\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert})
when
\ell\to\infty
, so that
\beta(\ell)
is negligible in front of any positive power of
\ell
.

In
Lemma

C.12
below, we bound the probability of error of the trigger event
\mathcal{E}
when the exploration threshold
\epsilon_{0}\log(T)/\beta(\epsilon_{0}\log(T))
is replaced by a general
m/\beta(m)
for
m\geq 1
, see (
C.25
) below.
For
m\geq 1
, let

\mathcal{E}_{m}\equiv\mathcal{E}_{\epsilon_{0},T,m}:=\left(\forall\ell\geq\frac{m}{\beta(m)}:\hskip-5.0pt\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{Z}^{**}_{\epsilon\mathrm{lvl}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell})}(\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}})=\mathcal{Z}^{**}(M)\mathrm{\penalty\ and\penalty\ }\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}}\sim M\mathrm{\penalty\ and\penalty\ }\\
\lVert\mu_{{\bar{\epsilon}}^{\prime\prime}}^{*}(\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}})-\mu^{*}_{{\bar{\epsilon}}^{\prime\prime}}(M)\rVert_{\infty}<\epsilon_{0}\beta(\ell)^{-2}\mathrm{\penalty\ and\penalty\ }\\
\sup_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{\delta(\ell)}(\mathcal{Z})}\left\lvert C_{{\bar{\epsilon}}^{\prime\prime}}(\mu,\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}})-C_{{\bar{\epsilon}}^{\prime\prime}}(\mu,M)\right\rvert<\epsilon_{0}\end{array}\hskip-5.0pt\right).

(C.25)

In
Corollary

C.13
, we instantiate
m=\epsilon_{0}\log(T)
to obtain a final bound on
\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}^{c})
.

Lemma C.12
.

Let
(\tau_{\ell})
be the stopping-time enumeration of
\mathcal{T}^{-}
, i.e.,
\tau_{1}:=\inf\mathcal{T}^{-}
and
\tau_{\ell+1}:=\inf\{t>\tau_{\ell}:t\in\mathcal{T}^{-}\}
.
For all error level
\epsilon_{0}>0
, there exists a constant
\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}>0
such that for all
T\geq\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}
, the event
\mathcal{E}_{m}
as given by (
C.25
) satisfies

\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{m}^{c})\leq\exp\left(-\mathrm{poly}({\bar{\epsilon}}^{\prime\prime})\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m)^{\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}}\right)m+\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}\right)

for all
m\geq 1
, where
\mathrm{poly}({\bar{\epsilon}}^{\prime\prime}):=\alpha^{\prime}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}(T)^{2}\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m)^{11\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\epsilon_{\mathrm{conv}}(m)^{2}
is polynomial and
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\in\mathbf{R}_{+}^{*}
are constants that depend on
M
.

Proof.

Introduce the event
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{m}:=(\forall z\in\mathcal{Z},\forall\ell\geq m,N_{\tau_{\ell}}(z)\geq\alpha_{1}\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(\ell)^{\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\ell)
where
\alpha_{1}>0
is a constant given by
Lemma

C.11
.
We have

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\left(\mathcal{E}_{m}^{c}\right)\leq\mathbf{P}\left(\mathcal{E}_{m}^{c}\cap\mathcal{E}_{m}^{\prime}\right)+\mathbf{P}\left((\mathcal{E}_{m}^{\prime})^{c}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{\leq}\mathbf{P}\left(\mathcal{E}_{m}^{c}\cap\mathcal{E}_{m}^{\prime}\right)+\exp\left(-\mathrm{poly}(\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m))m+\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}\right)

where
(\dagger)
follows by
Lemma

C.11
again, and
\mathrm{poly}(-)
is a polynomial of degree
4\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert
and
\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}<\infty
is a constant.

Now, we look at conditions on
\lVert\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}}-M\rVert
and
{\bar{\epsilon}}^{\prime}
under which the conditions of
\mathcal{E}_{m}
are met, then we will conclude by combining
\mathcal{E}_{m}^{\prime}
with concentration bounds (
Lemma

C.23
).
First, by
Lemma

C.25
, the absolute continuity condition “
\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}}\sim M
”, which is equivalent to “
\lVert\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}}-M\rVert^{*}=\lVert\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}}-M\rVert
”, is met as soon as

\lVert\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}}-M\rVert<\mathrm{dmin}(M).

(C.26)

By
Proposition

12
, the condition “
\mathcal{Z}^{**}_{\epsilon\mathrm{lvl}^{\prime}}(\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}})=\mathcal{Z}^{**}(M)
” is met as soon as

\alpha_{2}\lVert\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}}-M\rVert<\frac{1}{3}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell})\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002pt{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell})\leq\frac{1}{3}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}^{\prime\prime}(\ell)<\Delta^{g}(M)

(C.27)

for some model dependent constant
\alpha_{2}>0
.
Note that the second condition of (
C.27
) is satisfied as soon as
\ell,T
are large enough, say
T,\ell\geq\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}
up to increasing
\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}
.

For the condition “
\lVert\mu^{*}_{{\bar{\epsilon}}^{\prime\prime}}(\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}})-\mu^{*}_{{\bar{\epsilon}}^{\prime\prime}}(M)\rVert_{\infty}<\epsilon_{0}\beta(\ell)^{-2}
,” we rely on
Proposition

B.21
.
Because tht error function
\psi_{\mu}
of
Proposition

B.21
satisfies
\psi_{\mu}(x)=\OH(x)
when
x\to 0
, there exists constants
\alpha_{3},\alpha_{4}\in\mathbf{R}_{+}^{*}
such that
\psi_{\mu}(x)\leq\alpha_{3}x
for
x\leq\alpha_{4}
.
Following
Proposition

B.21
, we get the condition

\sqrt{\frac{\lVert\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}}-M\rVert}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}^{\prime}(\ell)^{3\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\epsilon_{\mathrm{conv}}^{\prime}(\ell)}}+\mathbf{1}\left(\lVert\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}}-M\rVert<\alpha_{5}\min\left\{{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}^{\prime\prime}(\ell),\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha_{6}}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}^{\prime}(\ell)^{\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}}\right)\right\}\right)\leq\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{\alpha_{3}\beta(\ell)^{2}}

(C.28)

where
\alpha_{5},\alpha_{6}
are other model dependent constants.
Note that the indicator in (
C.28
) can be rewritten as the pair of conditions

\lVert\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}}-M\rVert\leq\alpha_{5}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell})\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\lVert\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}}-M\rVert\leq\alpha_{5}\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha_{6}}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}^{\prime}(\ell)^{\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}}\right).

(C.29)

Note that the first condition of (
C.29
) is essentially the same as the first condition of (
C.27
) up to tuning constants.

To obtain the last condition “
\sup_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{\delta(\ell)}(\mathcal{Z})}\lvert C_{{\bar{\epsilon}}^{\prime\prime}}(\mu,\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}})-C_{{\bar{\epsilon}}^{\prime\prime}}(\mu,M)\rvert<\epsilon_{0}
”, we rely on
Lemma

B.13
.
Using that, for all
\alpha>0
,
\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m)^{-\alpha}=\oh(\exp(1/\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m)))
when
m\to\infty
and that
\delta(\ell)=\Omega(\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}^{\prime}(\ell)^{\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert})
, we obtain a pair of conditions where the first one is similar to (
C.29
) and the second is

\lVert\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}}-M\rVert<\alpha_{7}\epsilon_{0}.

(C.30)

Since
\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}^{\prime}(m)\to 0
as
m\to\infty
by
6
(
A0
), the condition (
C.30
) is already satisfied through (
C.28
) provided that
\ell
is large enough, say
\ell>\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}
up to increasing
\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}
.
Using that
\beta(\ell)=\OH(\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}^{\prime}(\ell)^{-\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert})
and that
\min\{a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}\}\geq\prod_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}
for
a_{i}\in[0,1]
, we combine (
C.26
) to (
C.30
) into the single condition

\lVert\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}}-M\rVert\leq\alpha_{8}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell})\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}^{\prime}(\ell)^{7\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\epsilon_{\mathrm{conv}}^{\prime}(\ell)\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha_{6}}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}^{\prime}(\ell)^{\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}}\right)\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\ell\geq\beta_{\epsilon}

where
\alpha_{8}
is some model dependent constant.
Using that for all
\ell\geq 1
, we have
{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell})\geq{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}(T)
,
\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}^{\prime}(\ell)\geq\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(\ell)
and
\epsilon_{\mathrm{conv}}^{\prime}(\ell)\geq\epsilon_{\mathrm{conv}}(\ell)
, we simplify the above into

\lVert\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}}-M\rVert\leq\underbrace{\alpha_{8}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}(T)\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(\ell)^{7\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\epsilon_{\mathrm{conv}}(\ell)}_{\varphi({\bar{\epsilon}}(\ell))}\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha_{6}}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(\ell)^{\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}}\right)\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\ell\geq\beta_{\epsilon}.

(C.31)

We conclude using concentration results (
Lemma

C.23
).
For
m\geq\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{m}^{c}\cap\mathcal{E}_{m}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{\leq}\mathbf{P}\left(\left(\exists\ell\geq m,\lVert\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}}-M\rVert\geq\varphi({\bar{\epsilon}}(\ell))\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha_{6}}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(\ell)^{\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}}\right)\right),\mathcal{E}_{m}^{\prime},\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\left(\left(\exists\ell\geq m,\exists z\in\mathcal{Z},\lVert(\hat{r}_{\tau_{\ell}}(z),\hat{p}_{\tau_{\ell}}(z))-(r(z),p(z))\rVert_{1}\geq\varphi({\bar{\epsilon}}(\ell))\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha_{6}}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(\ell)^{\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}}\right)\right),\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{m}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\ddagger)}{\leq}\sup_{\ell\geq m}\exp\left(-2\varphi({\bar{\epsilon}}(\ell))^{2}\alpha_{1}\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(\ell)^{\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\exp\left(-\tfrac{2\alpha_{6}}{\epsilon\mathrm{unif}(\ell)^{\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}}\right)\ell+\tfrac{1}{2}\log\left(1+\alpha_{1}\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(\ell)^{2\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\ell\right)+\alpha_{8}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\lx@sectionsign)}{=}\exp\left(-2\varphi({\bar{\epsilon}}(m))^{2}\alpha_{1}\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m)^{\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\exp\left(-\tfrac{2\alpha_{6}}{\epsilon\mathrm{unif}(m)^{\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}}\right)m+\tfrac{1}{2}\log\left(1+\alpha_{1}\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m)^{2\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}m\right)+\alpha_{8}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\$)}{\leq}\exp\left(-\varphi({\bar{\epsilon}}(m))^{2}\alpha_{1}\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m)^{\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\exp\left(-\tfrac{2\alpha_{6}}{\epsilon\mathrm{unif}(m)^{\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}}\right)m+\alpha_{8}\right)

where

(\dagger)
follows by (
C.31
);

(\ddagger)
follows from concentration results (
Lemma

C.23
);

(\lx@sectionsign)
uses that the inner function in the exponential is decreasing for
\ell
large enough by
6
(
A6
), so holds for
m\geq\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}
up to increasing
\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}
; and

(\$)
holds for
m
large enough again, say
m\geq\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}
up to increasing
\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}
again.
We conclude accordingly.

\blacksquare

Corollary C.13

(Trigger effect)
.

Let
\mathcal{E}_{\epsilon_{0},T}
be the trigger event as given by (
C.24
).
For all
\alpha>0
and
\epsilon_{0}>0
, we have

\mathbf{P}\left((\mathcal{E}_{\epsilon_{0},T})^{c}\right)=\oh\left(\exp\left(-\log^{1-\alpha}(T)\right)\right)\hskip 10.00002pt\text{when}\hskip 10.00002ptT\to\infty.

Proof.

Let
m_{T}:=\epsilon_{0}\log(T)
and fix
\alpha>0
.
By (
A2
-
4
) from
6
, we find
\mathrm{poly}({\bar{\epsilon}}(m_{T}))\geq\log^{-\alpha}(T)
when
T\to\infty
and by (
A4
), it follows that
\exp(-\beta\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m_{T})^{-\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert})\geq\exp(-\alpha\beta\log\log(T))
when
T\to\infty
.
All together, we find that when
T\to\infty
,

\mathbf{P}\left(\mathcal{E}_{m_{T}}^{c}\right)\leq\exp\left(-\Omega\left(\log(T)^{-\alpha}\log(T)^{-\alpha\beta}\cdot\epsilon_{0}\log(T)\right)\right)=\exp\left(-\Omega\left(\log(T)^{1-\alpha(1+\beta)}\right)\right).

As this holds for arbitrary
\alpha>0
, we conclude accordingly.

\blacksquare

C.3.5
Part III: Post burn-in quality of exploration

In this section, we show that under the trigger event (
C.24
), explorative visit counts concentrate around
N^{-}_{\tau_{\ell}}(z)\approx\ell\mu^{*}(z)
in high probability after a burn-in time of
\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}}\log(T)
exploration phases, see
Lemma

C.14
below.
Roughly speaking, it means that once the trigger event holds, exploration is near optimal.

Lemma C.14
.

Let
(\tau_{\ell})
be the stopping-time enumeration of
\mathcal{T}^{-}
, i.e.,
\tau_{1}:=\inf\mathcal{T}^{-}
and
\tau_{\ell+1}:=\inf\{t>\tau_{\ell}:t\in\mathcal{T}^{-}\}
.
Let
\mathcal{E}\equiv\mathcal{E}_{\epsilon_{0},T}
be the trigger event, see (
C.24
).
For all
\epsilon_{0}\in(0,1]
, there exists a constant
\alpha_{\bar{\epsilon}}(\epsilon_{0})<\infty
such that, for all
T\geq 1
, all
m\geq\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}}\log(T)+\alpha_{\bar{\epsilon}}(\epsilon_{0})
and all
z\in\mathcal{Z}
, we have

\mathbf{P}\left(\exists\ell\geq m:\tau_{\ell}\leq T\penalty\ \mathrm{and}\penalty\ \left\lvert\frac{1}{\ell}N_{\tau_{\ell}}^{-}(z)-\mu^{*}(z)\right\rvert\geq 8\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}},\mathcal{E}\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert^{2}\epsilon_{0}m}{\beta(m)^{2}}\right)

(C.32)

where
\beta(m):=1+2\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert\mathrm{dmin}(p)^{-\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m)^{-\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}
.

We start by showing the alternative statement of
Lemma

C.14
given by
Lemma

C.15
below.
Then
Lemma

C.15
is reworked into
Lemma

C.14
to better fit our needs.

Lemma C.15
.

Let
(\tau_{\ell})
be the stopping-time enumeration of
\mathcal{T}^{-}
, i.e.,
\tau_{1}:=\inf\mathcal{T}^{-}
and
\tau_{\ell+1}:=\inf\{t>\tau_{\ell}:t\in\mathcal{T}^{-}\}
.
Let
\mathcal{E}\equiv\mathcal{E}_{\epsilon_{0},T}
be the trigger event, see (
C.24
).
There exists a function
\gamma_{\bar{\epsilon}}(m)=\OH\big(\sum_{n=1}^{m}(\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(n)/\epsilon_{\mathrm{conv}}(n)+\epsilon_{\mathrm{conv}}(n))^{1/2}\big)=\oh(m)
when
m\to\infty
such that, for all
\delta>0
, we have

\mathbf{P}\left(\exists\ell\geq 1:\tau_{\ell}\leq T\penalty\ \mathrm{and}\penalty\ \left\lvert N_{\tau_{\ell}}^{-}(z)-\ell\mu^{*}(z)\right\rvert>\begin{pmatrix}\epsilon_{0}\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\left(\log(T)+4\ell\right)\\
+\beta(\ell)\sqrt{\ell\log\left(\frac{1+\ell}{\delta}\right)}+\gamma_{\bar{\epsilon}}(\ell)\end{pmatrix}\hskip-3.30002pt,\mathcal{E}\right)\leq\delta

(C.33)

where
\beta(m):=1+2\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert\mathrm{dmin}(p)^{-\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m)^{-\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}
.

Discussion of
Lemma

C.15
.

Said roughly, (
C.33
) states that on the good event
\mathcal{E}
, we have
N_{\tau_{\ell}}^{-}(z)\approx\ell\mu^{*}(z)
up to a few error terms.
The first term
\epsilon_{0}\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\log(T)
is due to the initial burn-in phase, when the number of exploration times is less than
\epsilon_{0}\log(T)
and that the algorithm explores very badly, due to potentially wrong empirical data.
The second term
4\epsilon_{0}\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\ell
comes from the algorithm navigating following a noisy version of the optimal exploration policy.
The third term
\beta(\ell)\sqrt{\ell\log((1+\ell)/\delta)}
comes from the stochasticity of the navigation, namely, from the aggregate difference between
e_{S_{t+1}}
and
p(Z_{t})
suffered during exploration.
The last term
\gamma_{\bar{\epsilon}}(\ell)
encapsulates the cost of changing of exploration epochs and the cost on the quality of the exploration policy due to the regularization
{\bar{\epsilon}}
.

Proof of
Lemma

C.15
.

We begin by introducing a few notations.
Because the regularization is floored as in (
17
), the uniformization
\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell})
and the convexification
\epsilon_{\mathrm{conv}}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell})
are constant over ranges of exploration times the form
\{\tau_{2^{m}},\ldots,\tau_{2^{m+1}-1}\}
.
Such ranges are referred to as
exploration epochs
and exploration will be decomposed along them.
Further note that for
\tau_{\ell}\leq T
, the floored and
T
-truncated regularizers (
C.21
) are equal with
{\bar{\epsilon}}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell})={\bar{\epsilon}}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell})
and in particular,
{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell})\geq{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}^{\prime\prime}(T)
.
For an exploration epoch
m\geq 0
, let

\mu_{m}\in\mathrm{Inv}(M)\cap\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{Z})

(C.34)

the central optimal exploration measure (
Definition

B.19
) of
M
under regularization
{\bar{\epsilon}}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{2^{m}})
.
Let
\pi^{*}_{m}
the fully randomized policy induced by
\mu_{m}
(see
Proposition

A.10
).
For
z\in\mathcal{Z}
, let
b_{m}^{z}
and
\Delta_{m}^{z}
the bias and gap functions of
\pi_{m}
in
M
under the reward function
f(z^{\prime}):=\mathbf{1}(z^{\prime}=z)
.

Fix
\ell_{0}\geq 1
and assume that
\tau_{2^{m}}\leq T
for
m=\lfloor\log_{2}(\ell_{0})\rfloor
.
We write

\displaystyle N_{\tau_{\ell_{0}}}(z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell=2^{m}}^{2^{m+1}-1}\mathbf{1}(\tau_{\ell}<\tau_{\ell_{0}})\mathbf{1}(Z_{\ell}=z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell=2^{m}}^{2^{m+1}-1}\mathbf{1}(\tau_{\ell}<\tau_{\ell_{0}})\left(\mu_{m}(z)+\left(e_{S_{\tau_{\ell}}}-p(Z_{\tau_{\ell}})\right)b_{m}^{z}-\Delta_{m}^{z}(Z_{\tau_{\ell}})\right).

(C.35)

By
Lemma

A.12
, we have the two bounds
\beta(x)\geq\max_{m\leq\log_{2}(x)}\max\{\mathrm{sp}(b_{m}^{z}),\lVert\Delta_{m}^{z}\rVert_{\infty}\}
and
\beta(x)^{-1}\leq\min_{m\leq\log_{2}(x)}\min(\mu_{m})
.
We continue by bounding the middle term in (
C.35
).
We have

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell=2^{m}}^{2^{m+1}-1}\mathbf{1}(\tau_{\ell}<\tau_{\ell_{0}})\left(e_{S_{\tau_{\ell}}}-p(Z_{\tau_{\ell}})\right)b_{m}^{z}

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{=}\pm\log_{2}(\ell_{0})\beta(\ell_{0})+\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell=2^{m}}^{2^{m+1}-1}\mathbf{1}(\tau_{\ell}<\tau_{\ell_{0}})\left(\left(e_{S_{\tau_{\ell+1}}}-e_{S_{\tau_{\ell}+1}}\right)+\left(e_{S_{\tau_{\ell}+1}}-p(Z_{\tau_{\ell}})\right)\right)b_{m}^{z}

\displaystyle\overset{(\ddagger)}{=}\pm\beta(\ell_{0})\left(\log_{2}(\ell_{0})+\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert\right)+\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell=2^{m}}^{2^{m+1}-1}\mathbf{1}(\tau_{\ell}<\tau_{\ell_{0}})\left(e_{S_{\tau_{\ell}+1}}-p(Z_{\tau_{\ell}})\right)b_{m}^{z}

(C.36)

where

(\dagger)
introduces the notation
x=\pm\alpha+y
as a shorthand for
\lvert y-x\rvert\leq\alpha
; and

(\ddagger)
follows from the observation that
S_{\tau_{\ell+1}}\neq S_{\tau_{\ell}+1}
can only hold when a panic happens, and the algorithm panics at most
\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert
times, see
Lemma

C.22
.
The remaining term is a martingale that will be dealt with in time.

Moving on to the bound of the last term in (
C.35
), we write

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell=2^{m}}^{2^{m+1}-1}\mathbf{1}(\tau_{\ell}<\tau_{\ell_{0}})\Delta_{m}^{z}(Z_{\tau_{\ell}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell=2^{m}}^{2^{m+1}-1}\mathbf{1}(\tau_{\ell}<\tau_{\ell_{0}})\left(\pi^{*}_{m}(S_{\tau_{\ell}})+\left(\pi^{*}_{m}(S_{\tau_{\ell}})-\pi^{-}_{\tau_{\ell}}(S_{\tau_{\ell}})\right)+\left(\pi^{-}_{\tau_{\ell}}(S_{\tau_{\ell}})-e_{A_{\tau_{\ell}}}\right)\right)\cdot\Delta_{m}^{z}

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{=}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell=2^{m}}^{2^{m+1}-1}\mathbf{1}(\tau_{\ell}<\tau_{\ell_{0}})\left(\pi^{*}_{m}(S_{\tau_{\ell}})-\pi^{-}_{\tau_{\ell}}(S_{\tau_{\ell}})\right)\cdot\Delta_{m}^{z}\hskip 10.00002pt\Big\}=:\mathrm{B_{1}}

(C.37)

\displaystyle\phantom{{}\overset{(\dagger)}{=}{}}+\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell=2^{m}}^{2^{m+1}-1}\mathbf{1}(\tau_{\ell}<\tau_{\ell_{0}})\left(\pi^{-}_{\tau_{\ell}}(S_{\tau_{\ell}})-e_{A_{\tau_{\ell}}}\right)\cdot\Delta_{m}^{z}

where
(\dagger)
follows from the Poisson equation of
\pi_{m}^{*}
, stating that
\pi_{m}^{*}(s)\cdot\Delta_{m}^{z}=0
for all
s\in\mathcal{S}
.

Equation

C.37
is the sum of a martingale and of an error term denoted
\mathrm{B}_{1}
.
We focus on that error term.
On the trigger event
\mathcal{E}
defined in (
C.24
) and combined with
Lemma

C.16
, we see that for
\ell\in\{2^{m},\ldots,2^{m+1}-1\}
, we have
\lVert\pi^{-}_{\tau_{\ell}}-\pi^{*}_{m}\rVert_{\infty}\leq 4\epsilon_{0}\beta(m)^{-2}\min(\mu_{m})^{-1}
provided that
m
is large enough.
Indeed, for
\ell\geq\epsilon_{0}\log(T)/\beta(\epsilon_{0}\log(T))
and on the trigger event
\mathcal{E}
, we have

\lVert\mu^{*}_{{\bar{\epsilon}}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell})}(\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}})-\mu^{*}_{{\bar{\epsilon}}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell})}(M)\rVert_{\infty}\leq\epsilon_{0}\beta(\ell)^{-2}

(C.38)

by definition.
Recall that
{\bar{\epsilon}}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell})={\bar{\epsilon}}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell})
for
\tau_{\ell}\leq T
.
Accordingly,
{\bar{\epsilon}}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell})
can be changed to
{\bar{\epsilon}}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell})
for all
\ell\leq\ell_{0}
since
\tau_{\ell_{0}}\leq T
.
By invoking
Lemma

C.16
, we conclude that for all
2^{m}\geq\epsilon_{0}\log(T)/\beta(\epsilon_{0}\log(T))
and
\ell\in\{2^{m},\ldots,2^{m+1}\}
with
\tau_{\ell}\leq\tau_{\ell_{0}}
, we have

\lVert\pi^{-}_{\tau_{\ell}}-\pi^{*}_{m}\rVert_{\infty}\leq 4\epsilon_{0}\beta(m)^{-2}\min(\mu_{m})^{-1}

(C.39)

So, on
\mathcal{E}
, we have

\displaystyle\lvert\mathrm{B}_{1}\rvert

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{\leq}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell=2^{m}}^{2^{m+1}-1}\mathbf{1}(\tau_{\ell}<\tau_{\ell_{0}})\lVert\pi^{-}_{\tau_{\ell}}-\pi^{*}_{m}\rVert_{\infty}\lVert\Delta_{m}^{z}\rVert_{1}

\displaystyle\overset{(\ddagger)}{\leq}\frac{\epsilon_{0}\log(T)\sup_{m\leq\log_{2}(\epsilon_{0}\log(T))}\lVert\Delta_{m}^{z}\rVert_{1}}{\beta(\epsilon_{0}\log(T))}+\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell=2^{m}}^{2^{m+1}-1}\mathbf{1}(\tau_{\ell}<\tau_{\ell_{0}})\frac{4\epsilon_{0}\lVert\Delta_{m}^{z}\rVert_{1}}{\beta(2^{m})^{2}\min(\mu_{m})}

\displaystyle\overset{(\lx@sectionsign)}{\leq}\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\left(\frac{\epsilon_{0}\log(T)\sup_{m\leq\log_{2}(\epsilon_{0}\log(T))}\lVert\Delta_{m}^{z}\rVert_{\infty}}{\beta(\epsilon_{0}\log(T))}+\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell=2^{m}}^{2^{m+1}-1}\mathbf{1}(\tau_{\ell}<\tau_{\ell_{0}})\frac{4\epsilon_{0}\lVert\Delta_{m}^{z}\rVert_{\infty}}{\beta(2^{m})^{2}\min(\mu_{m})}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\$)}{\leq}\epsilon_{0}\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\left(\log(T)+4\ell_{0}\right)

(C.40)

where

(\dagger)
follows from Hölder’s inequality;

(\ddagger)
bounds the first
\epsilon_{0}\log(T)/\beta(\epsilon_{0}\log(T))
terms by
\lVert\Delta_{m}^{z}\rVert_{1}
and invoke (
C.39
) for the others;

(\lx@sectionsign)
uses that
\lVert\Delta_{m}^{z}\rVert_{1}\leq\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\lVert\Delta_{m}^{z}\rVert_{\infty}
;

(\lx@sectionsign)
follows by bounding the first term using that
\beta(\ell)\leq\beta(\epsilon_{0}\log(T))
for
\ell\leq\epsilon_{0}\log(T)
, then using
Lemma

A.12
to obtain that
\lVert\Delta_{m}^{z}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\beta(\epsilon_{0}\log(T))
;
and
(\lx@sectionsign)
bounds the second term with
Lemma

A.12
for the bound
\lVert\Delta_{m}^{z}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\min(\mu_{m})\beta(2^{m})^{2}
;
and
(\$)
follows from straight-forward algebra.

Lastly, we relate
\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell=2^{m}}^{2^{m+1}-1}\mu_{m}(z)\mathbf{1}(\tau_{\ell}<\tau_{\ell_{0}})
to
\ell_{0}\mu^{*}(z)
.
Let
\psi
be the error function given by
Proposition

B.18
, see (
B.25
).
For all
m\geq 0
, we have

\lVert\mu_{m}-\mu^{*}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert^{\frac{3}{2}}\lVert\mu_{m}-\mu^{*}\rVert_{2}\overset{(\dagger)}{\leq}\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert^{\frac{3}{2}}\sqrt{\psi\left(\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(2^{m})}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{conv}}(2^{m})}+\epsilon_{\mathrm{conv}}(2^{m})\right)}

where
(\dagger)
follows by definition of
\mu_{m}
and
Proposition

B.18
.
Note that
\psi(x)=\OH(x)
when
x\to 0
, see
Proposition

B.18
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell=2^{m}}^{2^{m+1}-1}\mathbf{1}(\tau_{\ell}<\tau_{\ell_{0}})\mu_{m}(z)

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{=}\ell_{0}\mu^{*}(z)\pm\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert^{\frac{3}{2}}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell=2^{m}}^{2^{m+1}-1}\mathbf{1}(\tau_{\ell}<\tau_{\ell_{0}})\sqrt{\psi\left(\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(2^{m})}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{conv}}(2^{m})}+\epsilon_{\mathrm{conv}}(2^{m})\right)}

\displaystyle=\ell_{0}\mu^{*}(z)\pm\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert^{\frac{3}{2}}\sum_{m=0}^{\lfloor\log_{2}(\ell_{0})\rfloor}2^{m}\sqrt{\psi\left(\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(2^{m})}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{conv}}(2^{m})}+\epsilon_{\mathrm{conv}}(2^{m})\right)}=:\ell_{0}\mu^{*}(z)\pm\eta_{\bar{\epsilon}}(\ell_{0})

(C.41)

where
(\dagger)
introduces the short-hand
x=y\pm\alpha
for
\lvert x-y\rvert\leq\alpha
.

We have
\psi(x)=\OH(x)
when
x\to 0
and
\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(2^{m})/\epsilon_{\mathrm{conv}}(2^{m})+\epsilon_{\mathrm{conv}}(2^{m})\to 0
as
m\to 0
by
6
(
A5
).
Now, note that if a non-negative sequence
(u_{n})\in\mathbf{R}_{+}^{\mathbf{N}}
satisfies
u_{n}\to 0
, then we have
\sum_{m=0}^{n}2^{m}u_{2^{m}}=\oh(2^{n})
.
So, the quantity
\eta_{\bar{\epsilon}}(\ell_{0})
defined in (
C.41
) satisfies

\eta_{\bar{\epsilon}}(\ell_{0})=\OH\left(\sum_{n=1}^{\ell_{0}}\sqrt{\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(n)}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{conv}}(n)}+\epsilon_{\mathrm{conv}}(n)}\right)=\oh(\ell_{0}).

(C.42)

We finish the proof by merging everything.
Combining (
C.35
), (
C.36
), (
C.37
), (
C.40
) and (
C.41
), we find that on the trigger event
\mathcal{E}
and for
\tau_{\ell_{0}}\leq T
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert N^{-}_{\tau_{\ell_{0}}}(z)-\ell_{0}\mu^{*}(z)\right\rvert\leq\eta_{\bar{\epsilon}}(\ell_{0})+\beta_{\ell_{0}}\left(\log_{2}(\ell_{0})+\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert\right)+\epsilon_{0}\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\left(\log(T)+4\ell_{0}\right)+\mathrm{MDS}

where MDS is the martingale difference sequence given by

\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell=2^{m}}^{2^{m+1}-1}\mathbf{1}(\tau_{\ell}<t)\left(\left(e_{S_{\tau_{\ell}+1}}-p(Z_{\tau_{\ell}})\right)b_{m}^{z}+\left(\pi^{-}_{\tau_{\ell}}(S_{\tau_{\ell}})-e_{A_{\tau_{\ell}}}\right)\cdot\Delta_{m}^{z}\right),

which is the sum of
\ell_{0}
terms that are almost surely bounded by
2\beta(\ell_{0})
because
\beta(-)
is non-decreasing (see
6
(
A1
)) and
\mathrm{sp}(b_{m}^{z})\leq D(\pi_{m}^{*};M)\leq\beta(2^{m})
by
Lemmas

B.23
and
A.12
.
Therefore, using a time-uniform Azuma-Hoeffding inequality (
Lemma

C.26
), it is bounded by
2\beta(\ell_{0})\sqrt{\ell_{0}\log((1+\ell_{0})/\delta)}
with probability
1-\delta
.
To conclude the proof, we set
\gamma_{\bar{\epsilon}}(\ell):=\eta_{\bar{\epsilon}}(\ell)+\beta(\ell)(\log_{2}(\ell)+\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert)
.
Note that the definition of
\gamma_{\bar{\epsilon}}
does not depend on
\epsilon_{0}
.
Because
\beta(\ell)=\mathrm{poly}(1/\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(\ell))
and
\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(\ell)^{-\alpha}=\oh(\ell)
for all
\alpha>0
by
6
(
A4
), we conclude that
\gamma_{\bar{\epsilon}}(\ell)=\oh(\ell)
.

\blacksquare

The proof of
Lemma

C.15
used the result below (
Lemma

C.16
), relating the distances between two induced policies
\pi(\mu)
and
\pi(\mu^{\prime})
when
\mu,\mu^{\prime}
are two fully supported measures that are close to one another.

Lemma C.16
.

Let
\mu,\mu^{\prime}\in(\mathbf{R}_{+}^{*})^{\mathcal{Z}}
be two fully supported measures on
\mathcal{Z}
and denote
\pi(\mu),\pi(\mu^{\prime})
their respective induced policies, see
Proposition

A.10
.
If
\lVert\mu^{\prime}-\mu\rVert_{\infty}\leq\epsilon\min(\mu)
for
\epsilon<(2\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert)^{-1}
, then

\lVert\pi(\mu^{\prime})-\pi(\mu)\rVert_{\infty}:=\max_{(s,a)\in\mathcal{Z}}\left\lvert\pi(\mu^{\prime})(a|s)-\pi(\mu)(a|s)\right\rvert\leq 4\epsilon.

Proof.

This is straight-forward algebra.
For short, we write
\mu(s):=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}(s)}\mu(s,a)
.
Recall that by definition, the policy induced by
\mu
is
\pi(\mu)(a|s):=\mu(s,a)\mu(s)^{-1}
.
For
(s,a)\in\mathcal{Z}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\pi(\mu^{\prime})(a|s)-\pi(\mu)(a|s)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lvert\frac{\mu(s,a)-\mu^{\prime}(s,a)}{\mu(s)}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\mu^{\prime}(s,a)\left(\frac{1}{\mu^{\prime}(s)}-\frac{1}{\mu(s)}\right)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon+\frac{\mu^{\prime}(s,a)}{\mu(s)}\left\lvert\frac{1}{1+\frac{\mu^{\prime}(s)-\mu(s)}{\mu(s)}}-1\right\rvert

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{\leq}\epsilon+\frac{2\mu^{\prime}(s,a)\epsilon}{\mu(s)}\leq\epsilon\left(1+\frac{2(\mu(s,a)+\epsilon\min(\mu))}{\mu(s)}\right)\leq 4\epsilon

where
(\dagger)
uses that
\lvert\frac{1}{1+x}-1\rvert\leq 2\lvert x\rvert
for
x\leq\frac{1}{2}
.

\blacksquare

We derive
Lemma

C.14
by reworking (
C.33
) from
Lemma

C.15
into a more convenient form.

Proof of
Lemma

C.14
.

Borrowing the notations from
Lemma

C.15
, it holds that with probability
1-\delta
and on the trigger event
\mathcal{E}
, that

\displaystyle N_{\tau_{\ell}}(z)

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{\geq}\ell\mu^{*}(z)-\beta(\ell)\sqrt{\ell\log\left(\frac{1+\ell}{\delta}\right)}-\epsilon_{0}\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\left(\log(T)+4\ell\right)-\gamma_{\bar{\epsilon}}(\ell)

\displaystyle\geq\ell\left(\mu^{*}(z)-{\beta(\ell)}\sqrt{\frac{\log((1+\ell)/\delta)}{\ell}}-{\epsilon_{0}\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert}\left(\frac{\log(T)}{\ell}+4\right)-\frac{\gamma_{\bar{\epsilon}}(\ell)}{\ell}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\ell\left(\mu^{*}(z)-\frac{\beta(\ell)\big(\sqrt{\log(1+\ell)}+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}\big)}{\sqrt{\ell}}-{\epsilon_{0}\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert}\left(\frac{\log(T)}{\ell}+4\right)-\frac{\gamma_{\bar{\epsilon}}(\ell)}{\ell}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\ddagger)}{\geq}\ell\left(\mu^{*}(z)-{8\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}}\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert}\right)

where
(\dagger)
readily follows from
Lemma

C.15
, see (
C.33
); and

(\ddagger)
holds provided that the following equations hold:

\begin{gathered}{\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}}\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert}\overset{(\dagger)}{\geq}{\beta(\ell)}\sqrt{\frac{\log(1+\ell)}{\ell}},\qquad{\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}}\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert}\overset{(\ddagger)}{\geq}{\beta(\ell)}\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{\ell}}\\
{\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}}\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert}\overset{(\lx@sectionsign)}{\geq}\frac{\epsilon_{0}\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\log(T)}{\ell},\qquad{\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}}\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert}\overset{(\$)}{\geq}{\epsilon_{0}\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert}\quad\text{and}\quad{\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}}\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert}\overset{(\#)}{\geq}\frac{\gamma_{\bar{\epsilon}}(\ell)}{\ell}.\end{gathered}

In the above,

(\dagger)
is of the form
\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}}\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\geq C\,\mathrm{poly}(\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(\ell))\sqrt{\log(1+\ell)/\ell}
, so using that
\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(\ell)=\omega(\log^{-1}(\ell))
by
6
(
A4
), so we see that
(\dagger)
holds provided that
\ell
is large enough with respect to
\epsilon_{0}
, say
\ell\geq\alpha^{\dagger}(\epsilon_{0})
;

(\lx@sectionsign)
is equivalent to
\ell>\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}}\log(T)
;

(\$)
holds provided that
\epsilon_{0}\leq 1
;

(\#)
holds provided that
\gamma_{\bar{\epsilon}}(\ell)\leq\ell\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}}\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert
so since
\gamma_{\bar{\epsilon}}(\ell)=\oh(\ell)
, see
Lemma

C.15
, so we see that
(\#)
holds provided that
\ell
is large enough with respect to
\epsilon_{0}
, say
\ell\geq\alpha^{\#}(\epsilon_{0})
; and

(\ddagger)
can be solved in
\delta>0
, to find the condition
\delta\geq\exp(-\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert^{2}\epsilon_{0}\ell\beta(\ell)^{-2})
.
Setting
\alpha(\epsilon_{0}):=\max\{\alpha^{\dagger}(\epsilon_{0}),\alpha^{\#}(\epsilon_{0})\}
, we finally find the conditions

.\ell\geq\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}}\log(T)+\alpha(\epsilon_{0})\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\delta\geq\exp\left(-\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert^{2}\epsilon_{0}\cdot\frac{\ell}{\beta(\ell)^{2}}\right).

(C.43)

Note that the condition on
\delta
is monotone in
\ell
when
\ell
is large enough, see
6
(
A6
).
So, up to increasing
\alpha(\epsilon_{0})
, if (
C.43
) holds for some
\ell\geq 1
, then it holds for all
\ell^{\prime}\geq\ell
.
The upper-bound is obtained similarly.

\blacksquare

C.3.6
Part IV: The high probability exploration barrier

Following (
C.32
) from
Lemma

C.14
, we have
N_{\tau_{\ell}}^{-}(z)\approx\ell\mu^{*}(z)
with high probability on the trigger event
\mathcal{E}
.
In other words, exploration is near optimal.
Still under the trigger event, we know that the information value (
Definition

B.3
) of exploration measures is well estimated by definition, see (
C.24
).
Following this, we show that the number of exploration phases is quite unlikely to exceed the
exploration threshold
given by

m_{0}\equiv m_{\epsilon_{0},T}:=\frac{(1+\epsilon_{\mathrm{tst}}(T))\log(T)}{\left(1-\frac{8\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}}}{\mathrm{dmin}(\mu^{*})}\right)\left(C(\mu^{*},M)-\epsilon_{0}\right)}

(C.44)

where
\epsilon_{0}>0
quantifies the desired precision of the trigger event, see
Lemma

C.17
.

Lemma C.17
.

Let
T^{-}:=\sup\{\ell\geq 1:\tau_{\ell}\leq T-1\}
be the (random) number of exploration times prior to
T\geq 1
.
Let
m_{\epsilon_{0},T}
be as given by (
C.44
).
There exists a constant
\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
and a function
\psi\equiv\psi_{\epsilon_{0},{\bar{\epsilon}}}:\mathbf{N}\to\mathbf{R}_{+}
such that, for all
T\geq 1
, we have

\mathbf{P}\left(T^{-}\geq m_{\epsilon_{0},T}+\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}\log^{\frac{3}{4}}(T),\mathcal{E}\right)\leq\exp\left(-\psi(T)\right)

(C.45)

with
\psi(T)=\omega(\log(T)^{1/2-\alpha})
as
T\to\infty
for all
\alpha>0
.

To establish
Lemma

C.17
, we show first that after the exploration threshold
m_{0}
, exploration GLR tests (
16
) do not provoke exploration phases, see
Lemma

C.18
below.

Lemma C.18
.

Let
T^{-}:=\sup\{\ell\geq 1:\tau_{\ell}\leq T-1\}
be the (random) number of exploration times prior to
T\geq 1
.
Let
m_{0}\equiv m_{\epsilon_{0},T}
be as given by (
C.44
).
There is a polynomial
\mathrm{poly}(-)
of degree
4\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert
and some constant
\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
such that, for
m_{0}\equiv m_{\epsilon_{0},T}\geq\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}}\log(T)+\alpha_{\bar{\epsilon}}(\epsilon_{0})
, we have

\mathbf{P}\left(\sum_{\ell=m_{0}+1}^{T^{-}}\mathbf{1}\left(S_{\tau_{\ell}}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{Z}^{**}(M))\atop\tau_{\ell}\notin\mathcal{T}^{\pm}\right)>0,\mathcal{E}\right)\leq\exp\left(-\mathrm{poly}(\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m_{\epsilon_{0},T}))m_{\epsilon_{0},T}\atop+\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}\right).

(C.46)

Proof of
Lemma

C.18
.

Let
\beta(\ell):=1+2\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert\mathrm{dmin}(p)^{-\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(\ell)^{-\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}
.
Note that on
\mathcal{E}
and for
\ell\geq\epsilon_{0}\log(T)/\beta(\epsilon_{0}\log(T))
, we have
\mathcal{Z}^{**}_{\epsilon\mathrm{lvl}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell})}(\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}})=\mathcal{Z}^{**}(M)
where
{\bar{\epsilon}}^{\prime\prime}
is the
T
-truncated regularizer, see (
C.21
).
We find condition on
\ell\geq 1
such that the GLR exploration test (
16
) is guaranteed to fail at time
t=\tau_{\ell}
.
Let
M^{\dagger}\in\mathrm{Cnf}_{\bar{\epsilon}}(\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}})
.
We have

\displaystyle\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}N_{\tau_{\ell}}(z)\mathrm{KL}(\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}}(z)||M^{\dagger}(z))

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{\geq}(1-\lambda)\ell\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}\max\left\{\mu^{*}(z),\alpha\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(\ell)^{\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\right\}\mathrm{KL}(\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}}(z)||M^{\dagger}(z))

\displaystyle\overset{(\ddagger)}{\geq}(1-\lambda)\ell\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}\left((1-\delta_{\ell})\mu^{*}(z)+\delta_{\ell}\right)\mathrm{KL}(\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}}(z)||M^{\dagger}(z))

\displaystyle\overset{(\lx@sectionsign)}{\geq}(1-\lambda)\ell\penalty\ C_{\bar{\epsilon}}\left((1-\delta)_{\ell}\mu^{*}+\delta_{\ell}e,\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\$)}{\geq}(1-\lambda)\ell\cdot\Big(C_{\bar{\epsilon}}\left((1-\delta_{\ell})\mu^{*}+\delta_{\ell}e,M\right)-\epsilon_{0}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq(1-\lambda)\ell\cdot\Big(C_{\bar{\epsilon}}\left((1-\delta_{\ell})\mu^{*},M\right)-\epsilon_{0}\Big)

\displaystyle=(1-\lambda)\ell\cdot\Big((1-\delta_{\ell})C_{\bar{\epsilon}}\left(\mu^{*},M\right)-\epsilon_{0}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{(\#)}{\geq}(1-\lambda)\ell\cdot\Big((1-\delta_{\ell})C_{0}\left(\mu^{*},M\right)-\epsilon_{0}\Big)

where

(\dagger)
introduces
\lambda:=8\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}}\mathrm{dmin}(\mu^{*})^{-1}
and holds, on
\mathcal{E}
, uniformly over
\ell
with probability
1-\exp(-\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert^{2}\epsilon_{0}\cdot\beta(\ell)^{-2}\ell)-\exp(-\mathrm{poly}(\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(\ell))\ell+\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}})
, using a combination of (
C.32
) from
Lemma

C.14
to claim that
N_{\tau_{\ell}}(z)\gtrsim\ell\mu^{*}(z)
and of the uniform exploration result of
Lemma

C.11
to claim that
N_{\tau_{\ell}}(z)\gtrsim\alpha\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(\ell)^{\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}
for some
\alpha>0
;

(\ddagger)
introduces
\delta(\ell):=\alpha\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(\ell)^{\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}
;

(\lx@sectionsign)
holds by
M^{\dagger}\in\mathrm{Cnf}_{\bar{\epsilon}}(\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}})
and by definition of
C_{\bar{\epsilon}}
(
Definition

B.3
);

(\$)
holds because
(1-\delta(\ell))\mu^{*}+\delta(\ell)e
is obviously
\delta(\ell)
-uniform and by definition of
\mathcal{E}
, see (
C.24
); and

(\#)
holds when
{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}(\tau_{\ell})
is small enough so that
\mathrm{Cnf}_{\epsilon\mathrm{lvl}}(M)=\mathrm{Cnf}_{0}(M)
, i.e., when
{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}(\tau_{\ell})<\Delta^{g}(M)
, see
Proposition

12
and
Definition

14
.

Solving
(\#)\geq(1+\epsilon_{\mathrm{tst}}(T))\log(T)
, we derive the definition of
m_{0}\equiv m_{\epsilon_{0},T}
in (
C.44
).
We conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\left(\sum_{\ell=m_{0}+1}^{T^{-}}\mathbf{1}\left(S_{\tau_{\ell}}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{Z}^{**}(M))\atop\tau_{\ell}\notin\mathcal{T}^{\pm}\right)>0,\mathcal{E}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\frac{\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert^{2}\epsilon_{0}m_{0}}{\beta(m_{0})^{2}}\right)+\exp\left(-\mathrm{poly}(\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m_{0}))m_{0}+\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\min\left\{\frac{\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert^{2}\epsilon_{0}m_{0}}{\beta(m_{0})^{2}},\mathrm{poly}(\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m_{0}))m_{0}\right\}+\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}+\log(2)\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{\leq}\exp\left(-\mathrm{poly}(\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m_{0}))m_{0}+\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}+\beta^{\prime}+\log(2)\right)

where
(\dagger)
follows from the observation that
\beta(\ell)^{-1}=\mathrm{poly}(\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(\ell))
is a polynomial of degree
\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert
while
\mathrm{poly}(-)
introduced by
Lemma

C.11
is of degree
4\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert
, so that
\mathrm{poly}(\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m_{0}))m_{0}\leq\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert^{2}\epsilon_{0}\cdot\beta(m_{0})^{-2}m_{0}
for
m_{0}>\kappa^{\prime}
where
\kappa^{\prime}\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
is some constant.
This proves the claim.

\blacksquare

Following (
C.46
) from
Lemma

C.18
, we know that exploration GLR tests (
16
) do not provoke exploration phases after
m_{\epsilon_{0},T}
exploration phases—under the good event
