\mathcal{E}
given in (
C.24
) at least.
Beyond that threshold, exploration is mostly due to co-exploration and the number of induced phases can be controlled using
Lemma

C.21
, concluding the proof of
Lemma

C.17
.

Proof of
Lemma

C.17
.

Let
m\geq m_{0}\equiv m_{\epsilon_{0},T}
be as given in (
C.44
), and assume that
T\geq 1
is large enough so that
m_{\epsilon_{0},T}\geq\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}}\log(T)+\alpha_{\bar{\epsilon}}(\epsilon_{0})
where
\alpha_{\bar{\epsilon}}(\epsilon_{0})
is given by
Lemma

C.14
.
Let
\alpha>0
the constant given by
Lemma

C.21
.
We have

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\left(T^{-}\geq m+\alpha\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(T^{-})^{-2\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\log\log(T),\mathcal{E}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{\leq}\mathbf{P}\left(T^{-}\geq m+\alpha\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(T^{-})^{-2\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\left(\log\log(T)+\sum_{\ell=m_{0}+1}^{T^{-}}\mathbf{1}\left(S_{\tau_{\ell}}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{Z}^{**}(M))\atop\tau_{\ell}\notin\mathcal{T}^{\pm}\right)\right),\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle\phantom{{}\leq{}}+\mathbf{P}\left(\sum_{\ell=m_{0}+1}^{T^{-}}\mathbf{1}\left(S_{\tau_{\ell}}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{Z}^{**}(M))\atop\tau_{\ell}\notin\mathcal{T}^{\pm}\right)>0,\mathcal{E}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\ddagger)}{\leq}\exp\left(-\beta_{1}\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m)^{4\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}(m-m_{0})\atop+\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}\right)+\exp\left(-\mathrm{poly}(\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m_{\epsilon_{0},T}))m_{\epsilon_{0},T}\atop+\kappa^{\prime}_{\bar{\epsilon}}\right)

(C.47)

where

(\dagger)
introduces
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}:=(\forall\ell\geq m_{0},\mathcal{Z}^{**}_{\epsilon\mathrm{lvl}^{\prime\prime}}(\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}})=\mathcal{Z}^{**}(M))
where
{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}^{\prime\prime}
is the
T
-truncated leveling regularizer (
C.21
), and
(\dagger)
uses that
\mathcal{E}\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
; and

(\ddagger)
bounds the first term of
(\dagger)
using the lemma on co-exploration travels (
Lemma

C.21
) and the second term using (
C.46
) from
Lemma

C.18
, where
\mathrm{poly}(-)
is a polynomial of degree
4\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert
.
In the following, we we assume that
\mathrm{poly}(x)\leq\beta_{1}x^{4\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}
up to decreasing
\mathrm{poly}(-)
.

We set
m_{T}:=(1+\log(T)^{-1/2})m_{\epsilon_{0},T}
, so that
m_{T}-m_{\epsilon_{0},T}=\log(T)^{-1/2}m_{\epsilon_{0},T}
.

Setting
m\equiv m_{T}
in (
C.47
), we find

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\left(T^{-}\geq m_{\epsilon_{0},T}+\frac{m_{\epsilon_{0},T}}{\sqrt{\log(T)}}+\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(T^{-})^{-2\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\log\log(T),\mathcal{E}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\mathrm{poly}(\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m_{T}))(m-m_{\epsilon_{0},T})\atop+\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}\right)+\exp\left(-\mathrm{poly}(\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m_{\epsilon_{0},T}))m_{\epsilon_{0},T}\atop+\kappa^{\prime}_{\bar{\epsilon}}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{\leq}\exp\left(-\omega\left(\log\log(T)^{-4\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\sqrt{\log(T)}\right)\right)+\exp\left(-\omega\left(\log\log(T)^{-4\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\log(T)\right)\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\ddagger)}{\leq}\exp\left(-\omega\left(\log(T)^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}\right)\right)

where

(\dagger)
uses that
m_{\epsilon_{0},T}=\Theta(\log(T))
, that
m_{T}-m_{\epsilon_{0},T}=\Theta(\sqrt{\log(T)})
and that
\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m)=\omega(\log(m)^{-1})
, see
6
(
A4
); and

(\ddagger)
holds when
T\to\infty
for all (fixed)
\alpha>0
.

We conclude by simplifying the bound on
T^{-}
.
Assume that
T^{-}\leq(1+\log(T)^{-1/2})m_{\epsilon_{0},T}+\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(T^{-})^{-2\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\log\log(T)
.
Because
\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m)^{-2\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}=\oh(\log(m))
by
6
(
A4
), we have
\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(T^{-})^{-2\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\leq\log(T^{-})
provided that
T^{-}
is large enough, say
T^{-}\geq\kappa^{\prime\prime}_{{\bar{\epsilon}}}
.
It follows that

\displaystyle T^{-}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\log(T)}}\right)m_{\epsilon_{0},T}+\log(T^{-})\log\log(T)+\kappa^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{\epsilon}}

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{\leq}\inf_{\delta\in(0,1)}\left\{\frac{1}{1-\delta}\left(\left(1+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\log(T)}}\right)m_{\epsilon_{0},T}+\kappa^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{\epsilon}}\right)+\left(\frac{\log\log(T)}{\delta}\right)^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\overset{(\ddagger)}{\leq}\inf_{\delta\in(0,\frac{1}{2})}\left\{(1+2\delta)\left(\left(1+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\log(T)}}\right)m_{\epsilon_{0},T}+\kappa^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{\epsilon}}\right)+\left(\frac{\log\log(T)}{\delta}\right)^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\overset{(\lx@sectionsign)}{\leq}\left(1+\frac{2}{\log(T)^{1/4}}\right)\left(1+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\log(T)}}\right)m_{\epsilon_{0},T}+\log\log(T)^{2}\sqrt{\log(T)}+2\kappa^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{\epsilon}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\frac{5}{\log(T)^{1/4}}\right)m_{\epsilon_{0},T}+\log\log(T)^{2}\sqrt{\log(T)}+2\kappa^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{\epsilon}}

where

(\dagger)
uses that
\log(T^{-})\log\log(T)/T^{-}\leq\delta
for
T^{-}\geq(\log\log(T)/\delta)^{2}
;

(\ddagger)
follows from
\frac{1}{1-\delta}\leq 1+2\delta
for
\delta<\frac{1}{2}
; and

(\lx@sectionsign)
sets
\delta=\log(T)^{-1/4}
.
This proves the claim.

\blacksquare

C.3.7
Part V: First order failure and the skeleton exploration barrier

If either the trigger event
\mathcal{E}
fails, or if
N^{-}_{\tau_{\ell}}(z)
drastically deviates from its expected value
\ell\mu^{*}(z)
, the number of exploration phases can be as big as
T
in the worst scenario.
Combining
Lemmas

C.12
,
C.14
and
C.17
, this can happen with probability of order
\exp(-\log(T)^{1-\alpha})
for some
\alpha>0
.
In expectation, the associated error becomes
T\cdot\exp(-\log(T)^{\alpha})
, that is
\Omega(T^{1-\beta})
for all
\beta>0
.
One cannot afford an error term that large—bounding the number of exploration phases by
T
when our good events fail is too rough.

That is why, in this paragraph, we show that with
very
high probability, exploration cannot go beyond a threshold of order
\log(T)^{3}
(
Lemma

C.19
and
Corollary

C.20
): This is the
heavy
exploration barrier motivated earlier.
We start with a complete (but heavy) statement in
Lemma

C.19
that we simplify in
Corollary

C.20
under assumptions on the hyperparameter
{\bar{\epsilon}}
.
The idea behind the exploration barrier (
Lemma

C.19
) is that after enough exploration times, all pairs must enter the skeleton because exploration is slightly uniformized.
Once that all pairs are in the skeleton, the GLR exploration test (
16
) never triggers exploration phases, so that all further exploration times are exclusively produced by the co-exploration mechanism.
Because all the empirical data concentrates quickly on the skeleton, all the conditions are realized so that co-exploration induces very few exploration times, see
Lemma

C.21
.

Therefore, the exploration barrier consists in showing that exploration naturally stops for good once all pairs enter in the skeleton, and quantifying how quickly this happens.

Lemma C.19
.

Let
T^{-}:=\sup\{\ell\geq 1:\tau_{\ell}\leq T-1\}
the (random) number of exploration times prior to
T\geq 1
.
There exist constants
\alpha,\beta>0
and a function
\psi\equiv\psi_{\bar{\epsilon}}:\mathbf{N}\to\mathbf{R}_{+}
such that

\mathbf{P}\left(T^{-}\geq 2\log^{3}(T)+\alpha\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(T^{-})^{-2\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\log\log(T)\right)\leq\exp\left(-\psi(T)\right)

where
\psi(T)\sim-\beta\min\left\{\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(4\log^{3}(T))^{4\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\log^{3}(T),{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}(T)^{2}\log^{2}(T)\right\}
when
T\to\infty
.

Under
6
, the dominant term in the condition on
T^{-}
is
T^{-}\geq 2\log^{3}(T)
and the dominant term in the error probability is
\log^{2}(T){\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}(T)^{-2}
.
This provides the alternative version below (
Corollary

C.20
) that is easier to carry out.

Corollary C.20

(Skeleton barrier)
.

Under
6
, there exists a constant
\alpha>0
such that for all
\delta>0
, we have

\mathbf{P}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\mathbf{1}(t\in\mathcal{T}^{-})\geq\alpha\log^{3}(T)\right)\leq\oh\left(\exp\left(-\log^{2-\delta}(T)\right)\right)=\oh(T^{-2})\text{\hskip 10.22217ptwhen $T\to\infty$}.

(C.48)

Proof of
Lemma

C.19
.

Let
(\tau_{\ell})
be the stopping-time enumeration of
\mathcal{T}^{-}
, i.e.,
\tau_{1}:=\inf\mathcal{T}^{-}
and
\tau_{\ell+1}:=\inf\{t>\tau_{\ell}:t\in\mathcal{T}^{-}\}
.
Recall that
{\bar{\epsilon}}^{\prime}
denotes the floored regularizer (
17
) and that
{\bar{\epsilon}}^{\prime\prime}
denotes the
T
-truncated regularizer (
C.21
).
Recall that
\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}^{\prime\prime}(\ell)=\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}^{\prime}(\ell)\geq\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(\ell)
,
\epsilon_{\mathrm{conv}}^{\prime\prime}(\ell)=\epsilon_{\mathrm{conv}}^{\prime}(\ell)\geq\epsilon_{\mathrm{conv}}(\ell)
and
\epsilon_{\mathrm{tst}}^{\prime\prime}(t)=\epsilon_{\mathrm{tst}}^{\prime}(t)=\epsilon_{\mathrm{tst}}(t)
.
Let
m_{0}\geq 1
to be tuned later and introduce the events

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{m_{0}}

\displaystyle:=\left(\forall\ell\geq m_{0},\mathcal{Z}^{**}_{\epsilon\mathrm{lvl}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell})}(\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}})\neq\mathcal{Z}^{**}(M)\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{m_{0}}

\displaystyle:=\left(\forall\ell\geq m_{0},\forall z\in\mathcal{Z},N_{\tau_{\ell}}(z)\geq\log^{2}(T)\right)

and introduce the short-hand random variables

\displaystyle T^{-}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\mathbf{1}(t\in\mathcal{T}^{-})=\sup\{\ell\geq 1:\tau_{\ell}\leq T-1\},

\displaystyle\psi_{1}(m_{0},T)

\displaystyle:=2m_{0}+\alpha_{1}\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(T^{-})^{-2\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\log\log(T),

\displaystyle\psi_{2}(m_{0},T)

\displaystyle:=2m_{0}+\alpha_{1}\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(T^{-})^{-2\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\left(\log\log(T)+\sum_{\ell=2m_{0}+1}^{T^{-}}\mathbf{1}\left(S_{\tau_{\ell}}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{Z}^{**}(M))\atop\tau_{\ell}\notin\mathcal{T}^{\pm}\right)\right)

where
\alpha_{1}
is the alias for the constant
\alpha
that appears in the co-exploration travel lemma (
Lemma

C.21
).
We have

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\left(T^{-}\geq 2m_{0}+\psi_{1}(m_{0},T)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{P}\left(T^{-}\geq 2m_{0}+\psi_{1}(m_{0},T),\atop\mathcal{E}_{m_{0}},\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{m_{0}}\right)+\mathbf{P}\left(\left(\mathcal{E}_{m_{0}}\right)^{c},\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{m_{0}}\right)+\mathbf{P}\left(\left(\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{m_{0}}\right)^{c}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{\leq}\mathbf{P}\left(T^{-}\geq 2m_{0}+\psi_{1}(m_{0},T),\atop\mathcal{E}_{m_{0}},\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{m_{0}}\right)+\mathbf{P}\left(\left(\mathcal{E}_{m_{0}}\right)^{c},\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{m_{0}}\right)+\mathbf{P}\left(\left(\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{m_{0}}\right)^{c}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\ddagger)}{\leq}\exp\left(-\beta_{1}\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(2m_{0})^{4\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}m_{0}\atop+2\log(m_{0})+\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}\right)+\mathbf{P}\left(\left(\mathcal{E}_{m_{0}}\right)^{c},\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{m_{0}}\right)+\mathbf{P}\left(\left(\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{m_{0}}\right)^{c}\right)

(C.49)

where

(\dagger)
follows from the observation that under
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{m_{0}}
and for
t\leq T-1
, all pairs are within the skeleton hence no GLR exploration test (
16
) may trigger an exploration phase, so that
\psi_{1}(m_{0},T)=\psi_{2}(m_{0},T)
; and

(\ddagger)
follows by the co-exploration lemma (
Lemma

C.21
) and
\beta_{1},\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}
are aliases for the constants
\beta,\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}
that appear in that result.

This leaves to bound
\mathbf{P}((\mathcal{E}_{m_{0}})^{c},\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{m_{0}})
and
\mathbf{P}((\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{m_{0}})^{c})
.

Starting with
\mathbf{P}((\mathcal{E}_{m_{0}})^{c},\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{m_{0}})
, we could use the trigger effect (
Lemma

C.12
) but this is a bit of an overshoot—instead, we specialize the argument of the proof of
Lemma

C.12
on the fly.
By
Proposition

12
, the condition “
\mathcal{Z}^{**}_{\epsilon\mathrm{lvl}^{\prime\prime}}(\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}})=\mathcal{Z}^{**}(M)
” is met as soon as

\alpha_{2}\lVert\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}}-M\rVert^{*}<\frac{1}{3}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell})\leq\frac{1}{3}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}^{\prime\prime}(\ell)<\Delta^{g}(M)

(C.50)

where
\alpha_{2}
is an alias for the constant
C(M)
that appears in
Proposition

12
.
To convert
\lVert\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}}-M\rVert^{*}
into
\lVert\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}}-M\rVert
, we rely on
Lemma

C.25
, that provides the additional condition “
\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}}\sim M
” that is met as soon as

\lVert\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}}-M\rVert<\mathrm{dmin}(M)=:\alpha_{3}.

(C.51)

Now, the equations (
C.50
) and (
C.51
) can be combined into a single one, of the form
\alpha_{4}\lVert\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}}-M\rVert<\frac{1}{3}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell})
together with
{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}^{\prime}(\ell)<\Delta^{g}(M)
.
The second condition is about
\ell
and
T
being large enough because
{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}(\ell)\to 0
when
\ell\to\infty
.
All together, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\left(\left(\mathcal{E}_{m_{0}}\right)^{c},\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{m_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{\leq}\mathbf{P}\left(\exists\ell\geq m_{0}:\lVert\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}}-M\rVert\geq\frac{1}{3\alpha_{4}}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell})\text{\penalty\ and\penalty\ }\forall z\in\mathcal{Z},N_{\tau_{\ell}}(z)\geq\log^{2}(T)\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\ddagger)}{\leq}\mathbf{P}\left(\exists\ell\geq m_{0}:\lVert\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}}-M\rVert\geq\frac{1}{3\alpha_{4}}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}(T)\text{\penalty\ and\penalty\ }\forall z\in\mathcal{Z},N_{\tau_{\ell}}(z)\geq\log^{2}(T)\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\lx@sectionsign)}{\leq}2\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\exp\left(-{18\alpha_{4}^{2}{{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}(T)^{2}}\log^{2}(T)}+\frac{1}{2}\log\left(1+\log^{2}(T)\right)+\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert\log(2)\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-{18\alpha_{4}^{2}{{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}(T)^{2}}\log^{2}(T)}+\OH(\log\log(T))\right)

(C.52)

where

(\dagger)
holds when
{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}(m_{0})<\Delta^{g}(M)
and
T\geq m_{0}
, i.e., when
T
is large enough by
6
(
A0
);

(\ddagger)
holds because
{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}
is decreasing; and

(\lx@sectionsign)
follows from concentration results (
Corollary

C.24
).

We continue with
\mathbf{P}((\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{m_{0}})^{c})
.
Assume that
m_{0}
is large enough so that, for all
\ell\geq m_{0}
, we have
\alpha_{5}\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(\ell)^{\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\ell\geq\log^{2}(T)
where
\alpha_{5}
is an alias for the constant
\alpha
appearing in
Lemma

C.11
.
Because
\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(\ell)^{\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\ell=\omega(\ell^{1-\eta})
for all
\eta>0
when
\ell\to\infty
by
6
(
A4
), we see that
m_{0}\asymp\alpha_{4}^{-1}\log^{2}(T)
is asymptotically the right threshold.
In the sequel, set

m_{0}:=\log^{3}(T)

and consider that
T\to\infty
.
For
T
large, we have
\alpha_{5}\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(\ell)^{\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\ell\geq\log^{2}(T)
for all
\ell\geq m_{0}\equiv\log^{3}(T)
.
So, by
Lemma

C.11
, we find

\mathbf{P}((\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{m_{0}})^{c})\leq\exp\left(-\mathrm{poly}(\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m_{0}))m_{0}\atop+\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}\right)=\exp\left(-\mathrm{poly}(\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(\log^{3}(T)))\log^{3}(T)\atop+\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}\right)

(C.53)

where
\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}
is the constant
\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}
appearing in
Lemma

C.11
and
\mathrm{poly}(-)
is a polynomial of degree
4\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert
.
So, setting
m_{0}=\log^{3}(T)
, we conclude by combining (
C.49
), (
C.52
) and (
C.53
).

\blacksquare

C.3.8
Part VI: Putting everything together and proving
Lemma

C.10

We can finally conclude the proof.
Let
m_{\epsilon_{0},T}
the exploration threshold as given by (
C.44
).
Introduce

\beta_{1}(T):=m_{\epsilon_{0},T}+\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}\log^{\frac{3}{4}}(T)\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\beta_{2}(T):=\alpha\log^{3}(T)

(C.54)

where

\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}
is the constant that appears in the high probability exploration barrier (
Lemma

C.17
) and

\alpha
is the constant that appears in the skeleton exploration barrier (first order failure, see
Corollary

C.20
).
Introduce

\begin{gathered}\mathcal{E}_{0}:=\left(\forall\ell\geq\beta_{1}(T),\forall z\in\mathcal{Z},N_{\tau_{\ell}}^{-}(z)\leq\left(\mu^{*}(z)+8\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}}\right)\ell\right),\\
\mathcal{E}_{1}:=\left(T^{-}<\beta_{1}(T)\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{E}_{2}:=\left(T^{-}<\beta_{2}(T)\right).\end{gathered}

(C.55)

The event
\mathcal{E}_{1}^{c}
corresponds to the first order failure and
\mathcal{E}_{2}^{c}
corresponds to the second order failure in
Figure

6
.
Recall that
\mathcal{E}\equiv\mathcal{E}_{\epsilon_{0},T}
is the trigger event, see (
C.24
).
We have

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[N_{T}^{-}(z)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{\leq}\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\mu^{*}(z)+8\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}}\right)\beta_{1}(T)\cdot\mathbf{1}(\mathcal{E}_{0}\cap\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E})+\beta_{2}(T)\cdot\mathbf{1}(\mathcal{E}_{0}^{c}\cup\mathcal{E}_{1}^{c}\cup\mathcal{E}^{c})\mathbf{1}(\mathcal{E}_{2})+T\cdot\mathbf{1}(\mathcal{E}_{2}^{c})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\mu^{*}(z)+8\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}}\right)\beta_{1}(T)+\beta_{2}(T)\left(\mathbf{P}\left(\mathcal{E}_{0}^{c},\mathcal{E}\right)+\mathbf{P}\left(\mathcal{E}_{1}^{c},\mathcal{E}\right)+\mathbf{P}\left(\mathcal{E}^{c}\right)\right)+T\mathbf{P}\left(\mathcal{E}_{2}^{c}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\ddagger)}{=}\left(\mu^{*}(z)+8\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}}\right)\beta_{1}(T)+\OH\left(\log^{3}(T)e^{-\Omega\left(\sqrt{\log(T)}\right)}\right)+\OH\left(\log^{3}(T)e^{-\Omega\left(\log(T)^{1/4}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle\phantom{{}\overset{(\ddagger)}{=}\left(\mu^{*}(z)+8\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}}\right)\beta_{1}(T)}+\OH\left(\log^{3}(T)e^{-\Omega\left(\sqrt{\log(T)}\right)}\right)+\OH\left(Te^{-\Omega\left(\log(T)^{3/2}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\mu^{*}(z)+8\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}}\right)\beta_{1}(T)+\oh(1)

where

(\dagger)
follows from the observation that
N_{T}^{-}(z)=N^{-}_{\tau_{T^{-}}}(z)
and
N^{-}_{T}(z)\leq T^{-}
a.s.; and

(\dagger)
bounds the first error using (
C.32
) from
Lemma

C.14
, the second error using (
C.45
) from
Lemma

C.17
(exploration barrier), the third error using
Corollary

C.13
(trigger effect), and the fourth error using
Corollary

C.20
(skeleton exploration barrier).
Unfolding the definition of
m_{\epsilon_{0},T}
, see (
C.44
), we obtain

\mathbf{E}\left[N_{T}^{-}(z)\right]\leq\frac{\left(\mu^{*}(z)+8\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}}\right)(1+\epsilon_{\mathrm{tst}}(T))\log(T)}{\left(1-\frac{8\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}}}{\mathrm{dmin}(\mu^{*})}\right)\left(C(\mu^{*},M)-\epsilon_{0}\right)}+\OH\left(\log^{\frac{3}{4}}(T)\right)\hskip 10.00002pt\text{when}\hskip 10.00002ptT\to\infty,

where
\mu^{*}
is the central optimal probability exploration measure of
M
(
Definition

B.19
).
Letting
T\to\infty
then taking
\epsilon_{0}
vanishing, we conclude that

\limsup_{T\to\infty}\frac{\mathbf{E}\left[N_{T}^{-}(z)\right]}{\log(T)}\leq\frac{\mu^{*}(z)}{C(\mu^{*},M)}.

This concludes the proof of
Lemma

C.10
.

C.3.9
Auxiliary: Co-exploration travels

In this section, we provide an instrumentary result (
Lemma

C.21
) to bound the number of exploration phases triggered by co-exploration.
In spirit,
Lemma

C.21
states that the number of exploration phases triggered by co-exploration is of order
\log\log(T)
.

Recall that
{\bar{\epsilon}}^{\prime\prime}
denotes the
T
-truncated regularizer, see (
C.21
).

Lemma C.21
.

Let
(\tau_{\ell})
be the stopping-time enumeration of
\mathcal{T}^{-}
, i.e.,
\tau_{1}:=\inf\mathcal{T}^{-}
and
\tau_{\ell+1}:=\inf\{t>\tau_{\ell}:t\in\mathcal{T}^{-}\}
.
Let
T^{-}:=\sup\{\ell\geq 1:\tau_{\ell}\leq T-1\}
be the number of exploration times prior to
T\geq 1
.
There exist constants
\alpha,\beta,\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}>0
such that, for all
m_{0}
and
T\geq 1
, introducing the event
\mathcal{E}\equiv\mathcal{E}_{m_{0}}:=(\forall\ell\geq m_{0},\mathcal{Z}^{**}_{\epsilon\mathrm{lvl}^{\prime\prime}}(\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}})=\mathcal{Z}^{**}(M))
, for all
m\geq m_{0}
, we have

\begin{gathered}\mathbf{P}\left(T^{-}\geq m+\alpha\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(T^{-})^{-2\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\left(\log\log(T)+\sum_{\ell=m_{0}+1}^{T^{-}}\mathbf{1}\left(S_{\tau_{\ell}}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{Z}^{**}(M))\atop\tau_{\ell}\notin\mathcal{T}^{\pm}\right)\right),\mathcal{E}\right)\\
\leq\exp\left(-\beta\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m)^{4\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}(m-m_{0})+\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}\right)\quad\text{when $m\to\infty$.}\end{gathered}

(C.56)

Interpretation of
Lemma

C.21
.

The statement of
Lemma

C.21
reads as follows:
\mathcal{E}
is a good event, stating that beyond the exploration threshold
m_{0}
, the co-exploration structure is correct, with
\mathcal{Z}^{**}_{\epsilon\mathrm{lvl}^{\prime\prime}}(\hat{M})=\mathcal{Z}^{**}(M)
.
Then,
Equation

C.56
states that beyond the threshold
m_{0}
and under
\mathcal{E}
, exploration times are due to
(I) co-exploration times for a total of
\OH(\log\log(T))
induced exploration times; and
(II) exploration GLR tests (
16
) that explicitely cause exploration phases (exploration times for which
\tau_{\ell}\notin\mathcal{T}^{\pm}
while
S_{\tau_{\ell}}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{Z}^{**}(M))
).

Lemma

C.21
is to be applied when (II) is unlikely to happen beyond
m_{0}
, i.e., when the exploration GLR test is very likely to claim that enough information has been gathered beyond the exploration threshold
m_{0}
.
In such circumstances,
Equation

C.56
essentially says that there are
\OH(\log\log(T))
exploration times beyond
m_{0}
under the good event
\mathcal{E}
.

Proof.

Fix
m_{0}\geq 1
.
Let
\mathcal{Z}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{Z}_{c_{0}}
be the communicating components of
\mathcal{Z}^{**}(M)
, that the algorithm guesses correctly on
\mathcal{E}
by assumption.
Let
c^{-}(t):=\min\{c:\min\{N_{t}(z):z\in\mathcal{Z}_{c}\}=\min\{N_{t}(z):z\in\mathcal{Z}^{**}(M)\}\}
be the least visited component at time
t\geq 1
.
We decompose
\{\tau_{m_{0}},\tau_{m_{0}+1},\ldots\}
into segments alternating between least visited components.
Specifically, let
(j(i))_{i\geq 1}
be the sequence given by

\displaystyle j(1)

\displaystyle:=m_{0},

(C.57)

\displaystyle j(i+1)

\displaystyle:=\inf\left\{\ell>j(i):S_{\tau_{\ell}}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{Z}_{c^{-}(\tau_{\ell})})\right\}.

We relate the number of exploration times in
\{\tau_{m_{0}},\tau_{m_{0}+1},\ldots\}
to
j(-)
as follows.

(
S
TEP
1
)

Denote
I(m):=\inf\{i:j(i)<m\}
the pseudo-inverse of
j(-)
.
For
m\geq 1
, let
\alpha_{m}:=\mathrm{dmin}(p)^{\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m)^{\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}
and
\beta_{m}:=1+2\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert\mathrm{dmin}(p)^{-\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m)^{-\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}
.
For all
\delta\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\left(\exists m\geq m_{0},(1+\beta_{m})I(m)\geq\alpha_{m}(m-m_{0})-\beta_{m}\left(\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert+2\sqrt{\textstyle(m-m_{0})\log\left(\frac{1+m-m_{0}}{\delta}\right)}\right),\mathcal{E}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\delta.

Proof.

We use a technique that is similar to the proof of the uniform exploration lemma (
Lemma

C.11
).
Given
\eta>0
, we consider the Markov decision process
M_{\eta}:=(\mathcal{Z},r,p_{\eta})
with kernel given by

p_{\eta}(s,a):=\left(1-\eta\lvert\mathcal{A}(s)\rvert\right)p(s,a)+\eta\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}(s)}p(s,a^{\prime}).

The model
M_{\eta}
is communicating and by construction, the execution of any
\eta
-uniform policy
\pi
on
M
can be seen as the execution of a randomized policy
\pi_{\eta}
on
M_{\eta}
, in the sense that
\mathbf{E}^{M_{\eta},\pi}[-]=\mathbf{E}^{M,\pi_{\eta}}[-]
.

Given
\mathcal{Z}^{i}\equiv\mathcal{Z}_{c^{-}(\tau_{j(i)})}
, introduce the reward function
f_{i}(z):=\mathbf{1}(z\in\mathcal{Z}^{i})
.
Consider the model
M_{\eta}^{-f_{i}}=(\mathcal{Z},-f_{i},p_{\eta})
with kernel
p_{\eta}
and deterministic reward function
-f_{i}
and introduce
g^{-f_{i}}_{\eta}
,
b^{-f_{i}}_{\eta}
and
\Delta^{-f_{i}}_{\eta}
the associated optimal gain, bias an gap functions of
M_{\eta}^{-f_{i}}
.
Because
M_{\eta}^{-f_{i}}
is communicating, we have
\mathrm{sp}(g^{-f_{i}}_{\eta})=0
.
Let
\pi^{*}_{\eta}
be a deterministic bias optimal policy of
M_{\eta}^{-f_{i}}
.
By construction,
\pi^{*}_{\eta}
corresponds to an
\eta
-uniform policy of
M
for which all pairs are recurrent.
By
Lemma

A.12
, we have
\min(g^{-f_{n}}_{\eta})=\max(g^{-f_{i}}_{\eta})\leq-\mathrm{dmin}(p)^{\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\eta^{\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}
.
Consider the gaps in
M
, given by

\Delta^{-f_{i}}(s,a):=g^{-f_{i}}_{\eta}(s)+b^{-f_{i}}_{\eta}(s)+f(s,a)-p(s,a)b^{-f_{i}}_{\eta}.

(C.58)

Note that we also have
\max\{\mathrm{sp}(b^{-f_{i}}_{\eta}),\lVert\Delta^{-f_{i}}_{\eta}\rVert_{\infty}\}\leq\beta_{\eta}:=1+2\,\mathrm{dmin}(p)^{-\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\eta^{-\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}
by
Lemma

A.12
again.
Following from the Bellman equations of
M_{\eta}^{-f_{i}}
, check that every
\eta
-uniform policy
\pi
satisfies

\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}(s)}\pi_{\eta}(a|s)\Delta^{-f_{i}}_{\eta}(s,a)=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}(s)}\pi(a|s)\Delta^{-f_{i}}(s,a)\geq 0

(C.59)

where
\pi_{\eta}
is the randomized policy induced by
\pi
(i.e., bisimulating
\pi
) in
M_{\eta}
.

Let
m\geq m_{0}
and set
\eta:=\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m)
.
For conciseness, let
j^{\prime}(i):=\min\{j(i),m\}
.
We have

\displaystyle I(m)=\sum_{i=1}^{I(m)}1

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{=}\sum_{i=1}^{I(m)}\sum_{\ell=j(i)}^{j^{\prime}(i+1)-1}\mathbf{1}(S_{\tau_{\ell}}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{Z}_{c^{-}(\tau_{j(i)})}))

\displaystyle\overset{(\ddagger)}{=}\sum_{i=1}^{I(m)}\sum_{\ell=j(i)}^{j^{\prime}(i+1)-1}\left(-g^{-f_{i}}_{\eta}(S_{\tau_{\ell}})+\left(p(Z_{\tau_{\ell}})-e_{S_{\tau_{\ell}}}\right)b^{-f_{i}}_{\eta}+\Delta^{-f_{i}}(Z_{\tau_{\ell}})\right)

\displaystyle\geq\alpha_{m}(m-m_{0})+\underbrace{\sum_{i=1}^{I(m)}\sum_{\ell=j(i)}^{j^{\prime}(i+1)-1}\left(p(Z_{\tau_{\ell}})-e_{S_{\tau_{\ell}}}\right)b^{-f_{i}}_{\eta}}_{\mathrm{A}_{1}}+\underbrace{\sum_{i=1}^{I(m)}\sum_{\ell=j(i)}^{j^{\prime}(i+1)-1}\Delta^{-f_{i}}(Z_{\tau_{\ell}})}_{\mathrm{A}_{2}}

where

(\dagger)
follows by definition of
j(i+1)
, see (
C.57
); and

(\ddagger)
follows by definition of
\Delta^{-f_{i}}
, see (
C.58
).
We control the error terms as follows, starting with
\mathrm{A}_{1}
.
We have

\displaystyle\mathrm{A}_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{I(m)}\sum_{\ell=j(i)}^{j^{\prime}(i+1)-1}\left(e_{S_{\tau_{\ell}+1}}-e_{S_{\tau_{\ell}}}\right)b^{-f_{i}}_{\eta}+\sum_{i=1}^{I(m)}\sum_{\ell=j(i)}^{j^{\prime}(i+1)-1}\left(p(Z_{\tau_{\ell}+1})-e_{S_{\tau_{\ell}+1}}\right)b^{-f_{i}}_{\eta}

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{\geq}-\beta_{m}\left(I(m)+\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{I(m)}\sum_{\ell=j(i)}^{j^{\prime}(i+1)-1}\left(p(Z_{\tau_{\ell}+1})-e_{S_{\tau_{\ell}+1}}\right)b^{-f_{i}}_{\eta}

where

(\dagger)
follows from the observation that for
\ell\in\{j(i),\ldots,j^{\prime}(i+1)-1\}
, we have
\tau_{\ell+1}=\tau_{\ell}+1
unless
\ell\in\mathcal{T}^{!}
is a panic time—and by bounding the number of panic times by
\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert
, the maximum number of times a new transition is discovered, see
Lemma

C.22
.
The remaining right-hand term is a martingale that we leave for later.

We continue with
\mathrm{A}_{2}
.
We have

\displaystyle\mathrm{A}_{2}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{i=1}^{I(m)}\sum_{\ell=j(i)}^{j^{\prime}(i+1)-1}\Delta^{-f_{i}}(Z_{\tau_{\ell}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{I(m)}\sum_{\ell=j(i)}^{j^{\prime}(i+1)-1}\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}(S_{\tau_{\ell}})}\left(\pi_{\tau_{\ell}}^{-}(a|S_{\tau_{\ell}})\Delta^{-f_{i}}(S_{\tau_{\ell}},a)+\left(\mathbf{1}(A_{\tau_{\ell}}=a)-\pi_{\tau_{\ell}}^{-}(a|S_{\tau_{\ell}})\right)\Delta^{-f_{i}}(S_{\tau_{\ell}},a)\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{\geq}\sum_{i=1}^{I(m)}\sum_{\ell=j(i)}^{j^{\prime}(i+1)-1}\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}(S_{\tau_{\ell}})}\left(\mathbf{1}(A_{\tau_{\ell}}=a)-\pi_{\tau_{\ell}}^{-}(a|S_{\tau_{\ell}})\right)\Delta^{-f_{i}}(S_{\tau_{\ell}},a)

where

(\dagger)
follows from the observation that
\pi_{\tau_{\ell}}^{-}
is
\eta
-randomized (since
\ell\leq m
) and from (
C.59
).
We conclude that
\mathrm{A}_{1}
and
\mathrm{A}_{2}
both involve martingales obtained as the sum of
m-m_{0}
martingale differences whose terms hav e span at most
\beta_{m}
.
So, by a time-uniform Azuma-Hoeffding’s inequality (
Lemma

C.26
), we find that with probability
1-\delta
and uniformly for
m\geq m_{0}
, we have

(1+\beta_{m})I(m)\geq\alpha_{m}(m-m_{0})-\beta_{m}\left(\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert+2\sqrt{(m-m_{0})\log\left(\frac{1+m-m_{0}}{\delta}\right)}\right).

This proves the claim.

\blacksquare

(
S
TEP
2
)

For short, denote
I(m):=\inf\{i:j(i)<m\}
the pseudo-inverse of
j(-)
.
There exist constants
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime},\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}>0
such that, for all
m_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\left(\exists m^{\prime}\geq m,I(m^{\prime})>\alpha\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m^{\prime})^{2\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}(m^{\prime}-m_{0}),\mathcal{E}\right)

(C.60)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\alpha^{\prime}\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m)^{4\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}(m-m_{0})+2\log(m_{0})+\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}\right).

Proof.

We continue from the result of (
S
TEP
1
).
Recall that
\alpha_{m}:=\mathrm{dmin}(p)^{\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m)^{\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}
and
\beta_{m}:=1+2\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert\mathrm{dmin}(p)^{-\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m)^{-\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}
.
We are to solve the equation

\alpha_{m}\cdot(m-m_{0})>4\beta_{m}\sqrt{(m-m_{0})\log\left(\frac{1+m-m_{0}}{\delta}\right)}

(C.61)

in
\delta
.
We first decouple (
C.61
) as the pair of sufficient conditions

(
i
)

\displaystyle\alpha_{m}\cdot(m-m_{0})>8\beta_{m}\sqrt{(m-m_{0})\log(1+m-m_{0})},\text{and}

(
ii
)

\displaystyle\alpha_{m}\cdot(m-m_{0})>8\beta_{m}\sqrt{(m-m_{0})\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta}\right)}.

The first condition (
i
) is equivalent to

\frac{m-m_{0}}{\log(1+m-m_{0})}\geq 64\mathrm{dmin}(p)^{-2\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m)^{-2\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\left(1+2\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert\mathrm{dmin}(p)^{-\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m)^{-\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\right)^{2}

(C.62)

and using
\log(1+x)\leq\sqrt{x}
, we deduce that there is some
c>0
such that (
C.62
) holds if
m-m_{0}\geq c\penalty\ \epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m)^{-8\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}
.
As
\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m)^{-8\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}=\oh(\log(m))
when
m\to\infty
by
6
(
A4
), we have
\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m)^{-8\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\leq\log(m)/c
provided that
m
is large enough, say
m\geq\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}
.
We obtain the sufficient condition
m-m_{0}\geq\log(m)
that is again satisfied provided that

m-m_{0}\geq 2\log(m_{0})\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002ptm-m_{0}\geq 2\log\left(1+\frac{m-m_{0}}{m_{0}}\right).

The second is always satisfied provided that
m
is large enough.
We conclude that there is some
\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
such that (
C.62
) is satisfied as soon as
m\geq 2\log(m_{0})+\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}
.

The second condition (
ii
) solves as
\delta\geq\exp(-c(m-m_{0})\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m)^{4\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert})
for some constant
c>0
.
We conclude by using that
\psi_{m}(x)=(x-m_{0})\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(x)^{4\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}
is asymptotically increasing when
x\to\infty
, see
6
(
A6
):
There exist constants
\alpha^{\prime}\in(0,1]
and
\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}\in\mathbf{R}_{+}^{*}
such that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\left(\exists m^{\prime}\geq m,I(m^{\prime})\leq\alpha\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m^{\prime})^{2\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}(m^{\prime}-m_{0})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\mathbf{1}(m\geq 2\log(m_{0})+\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}})\cdot\alpha^{\prime}\penalty\ \epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m)^{4\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}(m-m_{0})\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{\leq}\exp\left(-\alpha^{\prime}\penalty\ \epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m)^{4\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}(m-m_{0})+2\log(m_{0})+\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}\right)

where
(\dagger)
follows from
\alpha^{\prime}\penalty\ \epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m)^{4\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}\leq 1
.
This proves the claim.

\blacksquare

(
S
TEP
3
)

Denote
I^{\pm}(m):=\lvert\{i\leq I(m):\tau_{j(i)}\in\mathcal{T}^{\pm}\}\rvert
.
On
\mathcal{E}
, we have

I^{\pm}(m)\leq c_{0}+\frac{\log\log(T)}{\log(2)}\leq\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert+2\log\log(T).

(C.63)

Proof.

Let
c_{t}
be the component of
S_{t}
at time
t
, if any (if it exists, it has to be unique).
For
c=1,\ldots,c_{0}
, further introduce

L(t;c):=\log\min\left\{N_{t}(z):z\in\mathcal{Z}_{c}\right\}

the minimal visit count of component
c
at time
t
.
Note that under the event
\mathcal{E}:=(\forall\ell\geq m,\mathcal{Z}^{**}_{\epsilon\mathrm{lvl}^{\prime\prime}}(\hat{M}_{\tau_{\ell}})=\mathcal{Z}^{**}(M))
and by definition of
j(-)
, see (
C.57
),
c_{\tau_{j(i)}}
is well-defined for
i\geq 2
and
c\equiv c_{\tau_{j(i+1)}}
is a least visited component of time
\tau_{j(i)}
.
Under
\mathcal{E}\cap(\tau_{j(i+1)}\in\mathcal{T}^{\pm})
, all the time instants
t\in\{\tau_{j(i+1)-1}+1,\tau_{j(i+1)-1}+2,\ldots,\tau_{j(i+1)-1}\}
are exploitation times that play the uniform policy on
\mathcal{Z}_{c}
, and
\tau_{j(i+1)}\in\mathcal{T}^{\pm}
means that
\mathcal{Z}_{c}
is over-visited compared to other components of
\mathcal{Z}^{**}(M)
.
Moreover, under
\mathcal{E}
, the algorithm cannot panic by running such a policy on
\mathcal{Z}_{c}
because no transition discovery may ever lead to a state outside of
\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{Z}_{c})
.
In other words, under
\mathcal{E}\cap(\tau_{j(i+1)}\in\mathcal{T}^{\pm})
, we have

L(\tau_{j(i+1)};c)>2\min_{c^{\prime}\in\{1,\ldots,c_{0}\}}L(\tau_{j(i+1)};c^{\prime})\geq 2\min_{c^{\prime}\in\{1,\ldots,c_{0}\}}L(\tau_{j(i)};c^{\prime})=2L(\tau_{j(i)};c).

(C.64)

From (
C.64
), we conclude that under
\mathcal{E}
, every time
\tau_{j(i+1)}\in\mathcal{T}^{\pm}
, the least visited component of time
\tau_{j(i)}
has doubled its visited count by time
\tau_{j(i+1)}
.
On
\mathcal{E}
, we have
\min_{c}L(\tau_{m};c)\geq 2^{\lfloor I^{\pm}(m)/c_{0}\rfloor-1}
.
Moreover, for
\tau_{m}\leq T
, we have
L(\tau_{m};c)\leq\log(T)
for all
c=1,\ldots,c_{0}
.
Solving in
I^{\pm}(m)
, we obtain the claim.

\blacksquare

Now, we conclude the proof of
Lemma

C.21
by combining (
C.63
) from (
S
TEP
3
) together with (
C.60
) from (
S
TEP
2
).

For
m\geq m_{0}
and as
m\to\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\beta\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m)^{4\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}(m-m_{0})+2\log(m_{0})+\kappa_{\bar{\epsilon}}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{\geq}\mathbf{P}\left(\exists m^{\prime}\geq m,m^{\prime}\geq m_{0}+\frac{1}{\alpha}\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(m^{\prime})^{-2\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}I(m^{\prime})\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\ddagger)}{\geq}\mathbf{P}\left(T^{-}\geq m+\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(T^{-})^{-2\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}}{\alpha}I(T^{-})\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbf{P}\left(T^{-}\geq m+\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(T^{-})^{-2\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}}{\alpha}\sum_{i=1}^{I(T^{-})}\left(\mathbf{1}\left(\tau_{j(i)}\in\mathcal{T}^{\pm}\right)+\mathbf{1}\left(\tau_{j(i)}\notin\mathcal{T}^{\pm}\right)\right),\mathcal{E}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\lx@sectionsign)}{\geq}\mathbf{P}\left(T^{-}\geq m+\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(T^{-})^{-2\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}}{\alpha}\left(\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert+2\log\log(T)+\sum_{i=1}^{I(T^{-})}\mathbf{1}\left(\tau_{j(i)}\notin\mathcal{T}^{\pm}\right)\right),\mathcal{E}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\$)}{\geq}\mathbf{P}\left(T^{-}\geq m+\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{unif}}(T^{-})^{-2\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert}}{\alpha/2\cdot\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert^{-1}}\left(\log\log(T)+\sum_{\ell=1}^{T^{-}}\mathbf{1}\left(S_{\tau_{\ell}}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{Z}^{**}(M))\atop\tau_{\ell}\notin\mathcal{T}^{\pm}\right)\right),\mathcal{E}\right)

where

(\dagger)
follows from (
S
TEP
2
);

(\ddagger)
instantiates
m^{\prime}=T^{\prime}
and simplifies the expression with straight-forward algebra;

(\lx@sectionsign)
unfolds
I^{\pm}(m)=\sum_{j=1}^{I(m)}\mathbf{1}(\tau_{j(i)}\in\mathcal{T}^{\pm})
then invokes (
C.63
) from (
S
TEP
3
); and

(\$)
uses that by definition of
j(-)
, see (
C.57
), we have
S_{\tau_{j(i)}}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{Z}^{**}(M))
almost surely.
This proves
Lemma

C.21
.

\blacksquare

C.4
Amount of panic times

Bounding the amount of panic times is much easier.

Lemma C.22
.

We have
\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\mathbf{1}(t\in\mathcal{T}^{!})\leq\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert
almost surely.

Proof.

By construction of the
ECoE*
,
Algorithm

2
can only panic if it has discovered a transition
(S_{t},A_{t},S_{t+1})
that it has never seen before.
As
(S_{t},A_{t},S_{t+1})
lives in the space
\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{S}
, we deduce that there is a injection
\mathcal{T}^{!}\to\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{S}
.
So
\lvert\mathcal{T}^{!}\rvert\leq\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert
.

\blacksquare

C.5
Combining everything together and proving
Theorem

C.1

Now,
Theorem

C.1
is a direct consequence of
Lemmas

C.2
,
C.10
and
C.22
.
Indeed, for
z\notin\mathcal{Z}^{**}(M)
and applying
Lemmas

C.2
,
C.10
and
C.22
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[N_{T}(z)\right]=\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\mathbf{1}\left(Z_{t}=z\atop t\in\mathcal{T}^{+}\right)\right]+\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\mathbf{1}\left(Z_{t}=z\atop t\in\mathcal{T}^{-}\right)\right]+\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\mathbf{1}\left(Z_{t}=z\atop t\in\mathcal{T}^{!}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mu^{*}(z)\log(T)}{C(\mu^{*},M)}+\OH\left(\frac{1}{{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lvl}}}(T)^{6}}+\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\exp\left(-\left(1+\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{\mathrm{tst}}(t)\right)\log(t)\right)\right)+\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert+\oh(\log(T)).

This proves
Theorem

C.1
.

C.6
Auxiliary: Adaptations of standard concentration results

In this paragraph, we adapt a few classical probability results (see
Section

C.7
) into the versions that are used all along the proof of
Theorem

C.1
.

Lemma C.23
.

Let
m\geq 1
and
d\geq 2
.
Fix
q
a distribution on
\{1,\ldots,d\}
and denote
q^{n}
the empirical distribution on
\{1,\ldots,d\}
obtained after
n
i.i.d. samples of
q
.
For every sequence
(\epsilon_{n})\in(\mathbf{R}_{+}^{*})^{\mathbf{N}}
, we have

\mathbf{P}\left(\exists n\geq m:\lVert q^{n}-q\rVert_{1}>\epsilon_{n}\right)\leq\sup_{n\geq m}\exp\left(-2n\epsilon_{n}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\log(1+n)+d\log(2)\right).

Proof.

By
Lemma

C.27
, we have

\displaystyle\delta

\displaystyle\geq\mathbf{P}\left(\exists n\geq m,\left\lVert n(q^{n}-q)\right\rVert_{1}^{2}\geq\tfrac{1}{4}n\left(1+\tfrac{1}{n}\right)\log\left(\tfrac{2^{d}\sqrt{1+n}}{\delta}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbf{P}\left(\exists n\geq m,\left\lVert q^{n}-q\right\rVert_{1}^{2}\geq\tfrac{1}{2n}\log\left(\tfrac{2^{d}\sqrt{1+n}}{\delta}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{\geq}\mathbf{P}\left(\exists n\geq m,\left\lVert q^{n}-q\right\rVert_{1}^{2}\geq\epsilon_{n}^{2}\right)

where
(\dagger)
holds if
\frac{1}{2n}\log(2^{d}\sqrt{1+n}/\delta)\leq\epsilon^{2}_{n}
for all
n\geq m
.
We conclude by solving in
\delta
.

\blacksquare

Corollary C.24

(Threshold concentration)
.

Let
m\geq 1
and
d\geq 2
.
Fix
q
a distribution on
\{1,\ldots,d\}
and denote
q^{n}
the empirical distribution on
\{1,\ldots,d\}
obtained after
n
i.i.d. samples of
q
.
For all
\epsilon>0
, we have

\mathbf{P}\left(\exists n\geq m:\lVert q^{n}-q\rVert_{1}>\epsilon\right)\leq\exp\left(-2m\epsilon^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\log(1+m)+d\log(2)\right).

Proof.

Starting from
Lemma

C.23
, we introduce the function

\varphi(n):=\exp\left(-2n\epsilon^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\log(1+n)+d\log(2)\right).

The function
\varphi
is decreasing in
[\frac{1}{4\epsilon^{2}}-1,\infty)
and satisfies
\varphi\geq 4
on
[0,\frac{1}{4\epsilon^{2}}-1]
, so that we may set
\inf_{n\geq m}\varphi(n)=\varphi(m)\equiv\exp(-2m\epsilon^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\log(1+m)+d\log(2))
.

\blacksquare

Lemma C.25

(Converting
\lVert-\rVert^{*}
to
\lVert-\rVert
)
.

Let
M\equiv(\mathcal{Z},p,r)
and
M^{\prime}\equiv(\mathcal{Z},p^{\prime},r^{\prime})
be two Markov decision processes with
M^{\prime}\ll M
.
For all
z\in\mathcal{Z}
, if
\lVert M^{\prime}(z)-M(z)\rVert^{*}<\mathrm{dmin}(M)
, then
\lVert M^{\prime}(z)-M(z)\rVert^{*}=\lVert M^{\prime}(z)-M(z)\rVert
.

Proof.

By definition, we have

\displaystyle\lVert M^{\prime}(z)-M(z)\rVert^{*}

\displaystyle=\lVert r^{\prime}(z)-r(z)\rVert^{*}_{\infty}+\lVert p^{\prime}(z)-p(z)\rVert^{*}_{1},\text{\penalty\ and}

\displaystyle\lVert M^{\prime}(z)-M(z)\rVert

\displaystyle=\lVert r^{\prime}(z)-r(z)\rVert_{\infty}+\lVert p^{\prime}(z)-p(z)\rVert_{1}.

By assumption,
M^{\prime}(z)\ll M(z)
so we just have to show that
M(z)\ll M^{\prime}(z)
.
Assume that
\lVert M^{\prime}(z)-M(z)\rVert<\mathrm{dmin}(M)
.
For kernels, we have

\displaystyle\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\lvert p^{\prime}(s|z)-p(s|z)\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\lVert p^{\prime}(z)-p(z)\rVert_{1}<\mathrm{dmin}(M)\leq\mathrm{dmin}(p(z))

so
p^{\prime}(s|z)>0
for all
s\in\support(p(z))
.
So
p^{\prime}(z)\gg p(z)
.
With the same rationale, we show that
r^{\prime}(z)\gg r(z)
and conclude that
M^{\prime}(z)\gg M(z)
.

\blacksquare

C.7
Auxiliary: Standard probability results

In this paragraph, we recall a few classical probability results.

Lemma C.26

(Time-uniform Azuma-Hoeffding,
Bourel et al. 2020
)
.

Let
(U_{t})
be a martingale difference sequence sequence with
\sigma
-subgaussian increments, i.e., for all
\lambda\in\mathbf{R}
, we have
\mathbf{E}[\exp(\lambda U_{n})|U_{1},\ldots,U_{n-1}]\leq\exp(\frac{\lambda^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2})
.
Then, for all
\delta>0
, we have

\mathbf{P}\left(\exists n\geq 1,\hskip 10.00002pt\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n}U_{k}\right)^{2}\geq n\sigma^{2}\left(1+\frac{1}{n}\right)\log\left(\frac{\sqrt{1+n}}{\delta}\right)\right)\leq\delta.

Lemma C.27

(Time-uniform Weissman,
Boone and Gaujal 2025
)
.

Let
d\geq 2
and
q
be a distribution over
\{1,\ldots,d\}
.
Let
(U_{t})
be a sequence of i.i.d. random variables of distribution
q
.
Then, for all
\delta>0
, we have

\mathbf{P}\left(\exists n\geq 1,\hskip 10.00002pt\left\lVert\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(e_{U_{k}}-q\right)\right\rVert_{1}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{4}n\left(1+\frac{1}{n}\right)\log\left(\frac{2^{d}\sqrt{1+n}}{\delta}\right)\right)\leq\delta.

Proof.

Note that

\left\lVert\sum_{k=1}^{n}(e_{U_{k}}-q)\right\rVert_{1}=\max_{v\in\{-1,1\}^{d}}\left\{\sum_{k=1}^{n}(e_{U_{k}}-q)\cdot v\right\}.

Let
W_{k}^{v}:=(e_{U_{k}}-q)\cdot v
.
Then, for each
v\in\{-1,1\}^{d}
,
(W_{k}^{v})
is a family of i.i.d. random variables with
\mathbf{E}[W_{k}^{v}]=0
and
-qv\leq W_{k}^{v}\leq 1-qv
a.s., so
\mathbf{E}[\exp(\lambda W_{k}^{v})]\leq\exp(\lambda^{2}/8)
by Hoeffding’s Lemma.
So, by a time-uniform Azuma-Hoeffding inequality (
Lemma

C.26
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\left(\exists n\geq 1,\hskip 10.00002pt\left\lVert\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(e_{U_{k}}-q\right)\right\rVert_{1}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{4}n\left(1+\frac{1}{n}\right)\log\left(\frac{2^{d}\sqrt{1+n}}{\delta}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\left(\exists v\in\{-1,1\}^{d},\exists n\geq 1,\hskip 10.00002pt\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n}W_{k}^{v}\right)^{2}\geq\frac{1}{4}n\left(1+\frac{1}{n}\right)\log\left(\frac{2^{d}\sqrt{1+n}}{\delta}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{v\in\{-1,1\}^{d}}\mathbf{P}\left(\exists n\geq 1,\hskip 10.00002pt\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n}W_{k}^{v}\right)^{2}\geq\frac{1}{4}n\left(1+\frac{1}{n}\right)\log\left(\frac{2^{d}\sqrt{1+n}}{\delta}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{v\in\{-1,1\}^{d}}\mathbf{P}\left(\exists n\geq 1,\hskip 10.00002pt\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n}W_{k}^{v}\right)^{2}\geq\frac{1}{4}n\left(1+\frac{1}{n}\right)\log\left(\frac{\sqrt{1+n}}{2^{-d}\delta}\right)\right)\leq 2^{d}\cdot 2^{-d}\delta=\delta.

This concludes the proof.

\blacksquare

Lemma C.28

(All-time Sanov)
.

Let
M\equiv(\mathcal{Z},\nu,p)
be a Markov decision process with Bernoulli rewards.
Fix
n\in(\mathbf{N}^{*})^{\mathcal{Z}}
and denote
\hat{M}_{(n)}
the empirical model obtained obtained by sampling independently
n(z)
times from
\nu(z)\otimes p(z)
.
Let
\mathcal{M}^{(n)}
be the discrete space of all possible values of
\hat{M}_{(n)}
.
For all
M^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}^{(n)}
, we have

\mathbf{P}\left(\hat{M}_{(n)}=M^{\prime}\right)\leq\exp\left(-\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}\mathrm{KL}(M^{\prime}(z)||M(z))\right)

where
\mathrm{KL}(M^{\prime}(z)||M(z)):=\mathrm{KL}(\nu^{\prime}(z)||\nu(z))+\mathrm{KL}(p^{\prime}(z)||p(z))
.

Proof.

This result is conveniently obtained with the method of types, see
Csiszar 2006
for example.
For
m\geq 1
, write
r_{(m)}(z)
and
p_{(m)}(z)
the empirically observed reward and kernel after
m
independent samples of
\nu(z)\otimes p(z)
.
We have

\mathbf{P}\left(\hat{M}_{(n)}=M^{\prime}\right)=\prod_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}\mathbf{P}\left(\hat{r}_{(n(z))}(z)=r^{\prime}(z)\right)\mathbf{P}\left(\hat{p}_{(n(z))}(z)=p^{\prime}(z)\right).

(C.65)

Let
z\in\mathcal{Z}
.
By definition,
r^{\prime}(z)
is of the form
\mathrm{Ber}(k(z)/n(z))
for
k\in\{0,\ldots,n(z)\}
.
So,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\left(\hat{r}_{(n(z))}(z)=r^{\prime}(z)\right)

\displaystyle=\binom{n(z)}{k(z)}r(z)^{k(z)}(1-r(z))^{n(z)-k(z)}

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-n\mathrm{KL}(\nu^{\prime}(z)||\nu(z))\right)\cdot\binom{n(z)}{k(z)}\exp\left(-n(z)\mathrm{Ent}(r^{\prime}(z))\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{\leq}\exp\left(-n(z)\mathrm{KL}(\nu^{\prime}(z)||\nu(z))\right)

(C.66)

where
(\dagger)
follows from the classical inequality
\binom{a}{b}\leq\exp(a\mathrm{Ent}(\frac{b}{a}))
where
\mathrm{Ent}(-)
is the entropy in base
e
.
With the same computation for kernels, we find that

\mathbf{P}\left(\hat{p}_{(n(z))}(z)=p^{\prime}(z)\right)\leq\exp\left(-n(z)\mathrm{KL}(p^{\prime}(z)||p(z))\right)

(C.67)

for all
z\in\mathcal{Z}
.
Combining (
C.65
), (
C.66
) and (
C.67
), we conclude accordingly.

\blacksquare

Lemma C.29

(Combinatorics, folklore)
.

Let
k\geq 1
and denote
\mathcal{P}_{n}[k]
the set of probability distributions over
\{1,\ldots,k\}
of the form
(\frac{n_{1}}{n},\ldots,\frac{n_{k}}{n})
for
n_{i}\in\mathbf{N}
.
We have
\lvert\mathcal{P}_{n}[k]\rvert\leq(n+1)^{k}
.

Appendix D
Deviation bounds on MDP specific quantities

The goal of this section is to provide a complete machinery to bound the variations of various policy related quantities, such as the gain, the bias, the diameter, the invariant measure and the reaching probabilities to components of recurrent sets.
One important consequence of the developed results is
Proposition

12
, consisting in that if
M,M^{\prime}
are two Markov decision processes, then

\mathcal{Z}^{**}_{\epsilon}(M^{\prime})=\mathcal{Z}^{**}(M)\hskip 10.00002pt\text{when}\hskip 10.00002ptC(M)\lVert M^{\prime}-M\rVert^{*}\ll\epsilon

(D.1)

where
C(M)
is directly related to
D^{*}(M)
, that is a notion of
worst diameter
(see
Section

4.2
and
Proposition

12
), and
\epsilon>0
is smaller than the gain gap of
M
.

All bounds are developed using a martingale technique coupled with a Poisson equation, sometimes under a transform of the initial kernel and reward function.
To provide a general flavor of the technique, consider two Markov reward processes
(r_{1},p_{1})
and
(r_{2},p_{2})
, and denote
g_{j},b_{j}
their respective gain and bias functions.
We have the Poisson equation
g_{j}(s)+b_{j}(s)=r_{j}(s)+p_{j}(s)b_{j}
.
We write
S_{t}
the random state at time
t
, and
\mathbf{P}_{s}^{p_{j}}(-),\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{j}}[-]
the probability and expectation of the trajectory governed by
p_{j}
initialized at
S_{1}=s
.
Consider a stopping time
\tau
such that
\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}[\tau]<\infty
.
The heart of the technique lies in the following computation:

\begin{split}&\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{\tau-1}r_{2}(S_{t})\right]\\
&=\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{\tau-1}\left(r_{2}(S_{t})-r_{1}(S_{t})\right)\right]+\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{\tau-1}r_{1}(S_{t})\right]\\
&\overset{(\dagger)}{=}\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{\tau-1}\left(r_{2}(S_{t})-r_{1}(S_{t})\right)\right]+\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{\tau-1}\left(g_{1}(S_{t})+\left(\mathbf{e}_{S_{t}}-p_{1}(S_{t})\right)b_{1}\right)\right]\\
&=\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{\tau-1}g_{1}(S_{t})\right]+\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{\tau-1}\left(r_{2}(S_{t})-r_{1}(S_{t})\right)\right]+\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{\tau-1}\left(p_{2}(S_{t})-p_{1}(S_{t})\right)b_{1}\right]\\
&\phantom{{}=\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{\tau-1}g_{1}(S_{t})\right]}+\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}\left[b_{1}(s)-b_{1}(S_{\tau})\right]\\
&\overset{(\ddagger)}{\leq}\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{\tau-1}g_{1}(S_{t})\right]+\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}\left[b_{1}(s)-b_{1}(S_{\tau})\right]+\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}\left[\tau\right]\left(\lVert r_{2}-r_{1}\rVert_{\infty}+\tfrac{1}{2}\mathrm{sp}(b_{1})\lVert p_{2}-p_{1}\rVert_{\infty}\right)\end{split}

where
(\dagger)
follows from the Poisson equation of
(r_{1},p_{1})
and
(\ddagger)
from standard norm bounds.
Coupled with model transformation and careful choices of stopping times, this technique provides tight bounds for the variations of the gain, bias, diameter, reaching time and invariant measures.
The obtained bounds on the variations of gain for instance (see
Lemma

D.9
and
Lemma

D.14
) are much better than those obtained using algebraic approaches involving the Drazin inverse with very little structural assumptions, going way beyond the scope of ergodic Markov reward processes.

D.1
A multichain-friendly diameter notion for Markov chains

The diameter provided in the definition is below generalizes the well-known notion of diameter in the communicating/recurrent setting.
It is tuned to provide a simple bound on the span of the bias function.

Definition D.0

(Diameter of a policy/Markov chain)
.

Let
p
be the kernel of a Markov chain with recurrent components
\mathcal{S}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{S}_{k}
.
The
(policy) diameter
of
p
is given by

D(p):=\sup_{(s_{i})\in\prod_{i}\mathcal{S}_{i}}\sup_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p}[\tau_{\{s_{1},\ldots,s_{k}\}}]<\infty.

(D.2)

For Markov decision processes, the policy diameter of
\pi\in\Pi
in
M
is also denoted
D(\pi;M)\equiv D(p_{\pi})
, where
p_{\pi}(s^{\prime}|s):=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}(s)}p(s^{\prime}|s,a)\pi(a|s)
is the transition kernel of
\pi
.

When the underlying chain is irreducible (i.e., recurrent), this diameter is related to return times hence providing lower bounds on invariant measures.

Lemma D.2

(Diameter and invariant measures)
.

The unique probability invariant measure
\mu
of a irreducible Markov chain with kernel
p
satisfies
\min(\mu)\geq D(p)^{-1}
.

Proof.

Let
\tau_{s}^{+}:=\inf\{t\geq 2:S_{t}=s\}
the first return time to
s\in\mathcal{S}
.
It is known, see for e.g.
(
Levin and Peres 2017
, Proposition 1.19)
, that
\mu(s)=(\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p}[\tau_{s}^{+}]-1)^{-1}
.
Now, note that

\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p}[\tau_{s}^{+}]\leq 1+\max_{s^{\prime}\neq s}\mathbf{E}_{s^{\prime}}^{p}[\inf\{t\geq 1:S_{t}=s\}]\leq 1+D(p)

and conclude accordingly.

\blacksquare

D.1.1
Policy diameter and diameters of Markov decision processes

The policy diameter is related to the standard notion of diameter in Markov decision processes.
Recall that the
diameter
of a Markov decision process
M
is given by
D(M):=\max_{s\neq s^{\prime}}\min_{\pi\in\Pi}\mathbf{E}_{s}^{\pi,M}[\inf\{t\geq 1:S_{t}=s^{\prime}\}]
and is finite if, and only if
M
is communicating.
Note that, for fixed
s\neq s^{\prime}
, the inner minimum can be taken over the set of unichain policies, i.e., policies with a unique recurrent component on
M
; Indeed, by taking a policy
\pi
achieving the minimum, we can modify
\pi
into
\pi^{\prime}
by setting
\pi^{\prime}(s^{\prime\prime})=\mathrm{Uniform}(\mathcal{A}(s^{\prime\prime}))
for every
s^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{S}
from which
s^{\prime}
is not reachable, i.e.,
\mathbf{P}_{s^{\prime\prime}}^{\pi,M}(\inf\{t\geq 2:S_{t}=s^{\prime}\})=0
, and check that the reaching time to
s^{\prime}
from
s
has to be the same under
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}
.
Then, note that if
s^{\prime}
is recurrent under a unichain policy
\pi\in\Pi
, then
\mathbf{E}_{s}^{\pi,M}[\inf\{t\geq 1:S_{t}=s^{\prime}\}]\leq D(\pi;M)
.
This shows that the diameter of
M
is upper-bounded relatively to the policy diameters of its unichain policies as given by

D(M)\leq\max_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\min\Big\{D(\pi;M):\pi\text{ unichain and $s^{\prime}$ recurrent under $\pi$}\Big\}.

(D.3)

In particular, we have the following result.

Proposition D.3

(Diameter and worst diameter)
.

In communicating Markov decision processes, the diameter is bounded by the worst diameter, i.e.,
D(M)\leq D^{*}(M)
.

The inequality in (
D.3
) is strict in general; Take for instance the deterministic transition Markov decision process induced by the complete graph with 3 vertices (the undirected
3
-cycle).
However, if we denote
M_{s}
the copy of
M
obtained by making
s
absorbing, then it can be shown that

D(M)=\max_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\min\Big\{D(\pi;M_{s^{\prime}}):\pi\text{ unichain and $s^{\prime}$ recurrent under $\pi$ in $M$}\Big\}.

(D.4)

Indeed, regarding (
D.2
), the set of recurrent states is reduced to
\{s^{\prime}\}
because if
s^{\prime}
is recurrent under some unichain policy
\pi
, then it is the only recurrent state of
\pi
in
M_{s^{\prime}}
.

D.1.2
Policy diameter and bias function

Following these observations, we can generalize the links between the bias function and the diameter to the policy diameter as follows.

Lemma D.4

(Policy bias and diameter)
.

Let
(r,p)
be a Markov reward process and denote
g,b
its gain and bias functions.
Then
\mathrm{sp}(b)\leq 2\penalty\ \mathrm{sp}(r)D(p)
.

Proof.

For
(s_{i})\in\prod_{i=1}^{k}\mathcal{S}_{i}
a covering of the recurrent components, we denote
\tau\equiv\tau_{\{s_{1},\ldots,s_{k}\}}
for short.
We have
\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p}[\tau]\leq D(p)<\infty
for all
s\in\mathcal{S}
by construction.
Because
\mu b=0
for every invariant measure of
p
, we see that for all
i=1,\ldots,k
, there must be
s_{i}\in\mathcal{S}_{i}
such that
b(s_{i})\geq 0
, and another
s_{i}\in\mathcal{S}_{i}
such that
b(s_{i})\leq 0
.
Assume that
b(s_{i})\leq 0
for all
i=1,\ldots,k
.
Then

\begin{split}\max(r-g)D(p)&\geq\mathbf{E}^{p}_{s}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{\tau-1}(r(S_{t})-g(S_{t}))\right]\\
&\overset{(\dagger)}{=}\mathbf{E}^{p}_{s}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{\tau-1}\left(\mathbf{e}_{S_{t}}-p(S_{t})\right)b\right]\\
&=b(s)-\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p}\left[b(S_{\tau})\right]=b(s)-\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathbf{P}^{p}_{s}\left(\tau_{\mathcal{S}_{i}}<\infty\right)b(s_{i})\overset{(\ddagger)}{\geq}b(s).\end{split}

(D.5)

where
(\dagger)
follows from the Poisson equation
g(s)+b(s)=r(s)+p(s)b
and
(\ddagger)
by
b(s_{i})\leq 0
.
With similar computations, and picking
s_{i}
such that
b(s_{i})\geq 0
for
i=1,\ldots,k
, we have

\min(r-g)D(p)\leq b(s)-\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathbf{P}^{p}_{s}\left(\tau_{\mathcal{S}_{i}}<\infty\right)b(s_{i})\leq b(s).

(D.6)

Combining (
D.5
) and (
D.6
), we obtain

\max(b)-\min(b)\leq D(p)\left(\max(r-g)-\min(r-g)\right)\leq 2\penalty\ \mathrm{sp}(r)D(p)

(D.7)

where the second inequality uses that
\min(r)\leq g(s)\leq\max(r)
for all
s
.

\blacksquare

D.1.3
Sensitivity of the policy diameter to perturbations

In this paragraph, we quantify the sensitivity of hitting times and of the policy diameter to perturbations of the transition kernel (
Lemma

D.5
).
For the general setting, we make a absolute continuity assumption.
This assumption can be dropped completely under an ergodic assumption (
Lemma

D.6
), and partially under a unichain assumption (
Corollary

D.7
).

Lemma D.5

(Variations of policy diameter)
.

Let
p_{1}\sim p_{2}
be two equivalent Markov chains with (common) recurrent components
\mathcal{S}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{S}_{k}
.
For all
(s_{i})\in\prod_{i=1}^{k}\mathcal{S}_{i}
covering of the recurrent components, the variations of the hitting time
\tau\equiv\tau_{\{s_{1},\ldots,s_{k}\}}
are bounded as:

\left\lvert\sup_{s}\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}[\tau]-\sup_{s}\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{1}}[\tau]\right\rvert\leq\frac{1}{2}\sup_{s}\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}[\tau]\sup_{s}\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{1}}[\tau]\lVert p_{2}-p_{1}\rVert_{\infty}.

(D.8)

In particular
D(p_{2})\leq D(p_{1})+\frac{1}{2}D(p_{1})D(p_{2})\lVert p_{2}-p_{1}\rVert_{\infty}
.

Proof.

Let
(s_{i})\in\prod_{i=1}^{k}\mathcal{S}_{i}
be a covering of the recurrent components and denote
\tau\equiv\tau_{\{s_{1},\ldots,s_{k}\}}
for short.
Consider the kernel
p^{\prime}_{j}
obtained by making every
s_{i}
absorbing and consider the reward function
r^{\prime}(s):=\mathbf{1}(s\notin\{s_{1},\ldots,s_{k}\})
.
The gain and bias functions of the Markov reward process
(r^{\prime},p^{\prime}_{j})
are denoted
g^{\prime}_{j}
and
b^{\prime}_{j}
for
j=1,2
.
Remark that
g^{\prime}_{j}=0
and that the bias satisfies

b^{\prime}_{j}(s)=\Clim_{T\to\infty}\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p^{\prime}_{j}}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{\tau\wedge T-1}\left(r^{\prime}(S_{t})-g^{\prime}_{j}(S_{t})\right)\right]=\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{j}}[\tau]

(D.9)

hence
b^{\prime}_{j}
are the reaching times of which we want to bound the variations.
We have

\begin{split}b^{\prime}_{2}(s)=\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{\tau-1}r^{\prime}(S_{t})\right]&=b^{\prime}_{1}(s)+\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{\tau-1}\left(p_{2}(S_{t})-p_{1}(S_{t})\right)b^{\prime}_{1}\right].\end{split}

The error term is bounded by
\lvert\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}[\sum_{t=1}^{\tau-1}(p_{2}(S_{t})-p_{1}(S_{t}))b^{\prime}_{1}]\rvert\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\mathrm{sp}(b_{1}^{\prime})\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}[\tau]\lVert p_{2}-p_{1}\rVert_{\infty}
.
By (
D.9
), we clearly have
\mathrm{sp}(b_{1}^{\prime})=\max(b^{\prime}_{1})=\sup_{s}\mathbf{E}^{p_{1}}_{s}[\tau]
.
Accordingly, we have obtained the self-bound:

\left\lvert\sup_{s}\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}[\tau]-\sup_{s}\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{1}}[\tau]\right\rvert\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\sup_{s}\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}[\tau]\sup_{s}\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{1}}[\tau]\lVert p_{2}-p_{1}\rVert_{\infty}.

(D.10)

This concludes the proof.

\blacksquare

The assumption
p_{1}\sim p_{2}
is not always necessary and can be dropped under a recurrent assumption.
In turn, this allows to control the deviations of reaching times to recurrent states of unichain Markov chains.

Lemma D.6

(Variations of policy diameter, recurrent case)
.

Let
p_{1},p_{2}
be two recurrent Markov chains.
We have

\left\lvert D(p_{1})-D(p_{2})\right\rvert\leq\frac{1}{2}D(p_{1})D(p_{2})\lVert p_{2}-p_{1}\rVert_{\infty}.

(D.11)

Proof.

Same proof as
Lemma

D.5
.

\blacksquare

Corollary D.7

(Variations of reaching times, unichain case)
.

Let
p_{1},p_{2}
be two unichain Markov chains that share one recurrent state
s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}
.
Then

\left\lvert\sup_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}[\tau_{s^{\prime}}]-\sup_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{1}}[\tau_{s^{\prime}}]\right\rvert\leq\frac{1}{2}\sup_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{1}}[\tau_{s^{\prime}}]\sup_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}[\tau_{s^{\prime}}]\lVert p_{2}-p_{1}\rVert_{\infty}.

Proof.

For
i=1,2
, introduce
p^{\prime}_{i}
the copy of
p_{i}
where
s^{\prime}
is made absorbing, and apply
Lemma

D.5
to
p^{\prime}_{1},p^{\prime}_{2}
.

\blacksquare

In
Lemma

D.6
, the inequality
D(p_{2})\leq D(p_{1})+\frac{1}{2}D(p_{1})D(p_{2})\lVert p_{2}-p_{1}\rVert_{\infty}
is a self-bound for
D(p_{2})
.
It can be decoupled when
\lVert p_{2}-p_{1}\rVert_{\infty}
is small.
If
\lVert p_{2}-p_{1}\rVert_{\infty}<\tfrac{2}{D(p_{1})}
, we obtain

D(p_{2})\leq\frac{1}{1-\tfrac{1}{2}D(p_{1})\lVert p_{2}-p_{1}\rVert_{\infty}}D(p_{1}).

(D.12)

For instance, if
\lVert p_{2}-p_{1}\rVert_{\infty}\leq{D(p_{1})}^{-1}
, then
D(p_{2})\leq 2D(p_{1})
.
The same technique can be used to untangle the inequalities of
Lemmas

D.5
and
D.7
.

D.1.4
Sensitivity of the diameter of MDPs to perturbations

Using the links between the diameter of Markov decision processes and the diameter of unichain policies, see (
D.3
) and (
D.4
),
Lemma

D.5
allows to bound the variations of the diameter in a neighborhood of
M
.

Lemma D.8

(Variations of diameter)
.

Let
M\equiv(\mathcal{Z},p,r)
be a communicating Markov decision process.
For
M^{\prime}\equiv(\mathcal{Z},p^{\prime},r^{\prime})
such that
p^{\prime}\sim p
, we have

\lvert D(M^{\prime})-D(M)\rvert\leq\frac{1}{2}D(M^{\prime})D(M)\lVert p^{\prime}-p\rVert_{\infty}.

(D.13)

In particular, if
\lVert p^{\prime}-p\rVert_{\infty}\leq 2D(M)^{-1}
, then
D(M^{\prime})\leq(1-\frac{1}{2}D(M)\lVert p^{\prime}-p\rVert_{\infty})^{-1}D(M)
.

Proof.

Following (
D.4
), we introduce
\pi_{s^{\prime}}
the policy minimizing
D(\pi;M_{s^{\prime}})
over the set of unichain policies under which
s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}
is recurrent in
M
, where
M_{s^{\prime}}\equiv(\mathcal{Z},p_{s^{\prime}},r)
is the copy of
M
obtained by making
s^{\prime}
absorbing.
By
Lemma

D.5
, we have

\lvert D(\pi_{s^{\prime}};M^{\prime}_{s^{\prime}})-D(\pi_{s^{\prime}};M_{s^{\prime}})\rvert\leq\frac{1}{2}D(\pi_{s^{\prime}};M^{\prime}_{s^{\prime}})D(\pi_{s^{\prime}};M_{s^{\prime}})\lVert p^{\prime}_{s^{\prime}}-p_{s^{\prime}}\rVert_{\infty}

for
s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}
.
By construction,
\lVert p^{\prime}_{s^{\prime}}-p_{s^{\prime}}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\lVert p^{\prime}-p\rVert_{\infty}
.
Conclude by (
D.4
).

\blacksquare

In particular, if
\lVert M^{\prime}-M\rVert^{*}\leq{D(M)}^{-1}
, then
D(M^{\prime})\leq 2D(M)
.

D.2
Results for unichain Markov reward processes

The first lemma is the conclusion of the tutorial computations that start
Appendix

D
.

Lemma D.9

(Unichain gain variations)
.

Let
(r_{1},p_{1})
and
(r_{2},p_{2})
be two Markov reward processes.
Denote
g_{j},b_{j}
the gain and bias functions of
(r_{j},p_{j})
for
j=1,2
.
Assume that
\mathrm{sp}(g_{1})=0
.
Then

\left\lVert g_{2}-g_{1}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\lVert r_{2}-r_{1}\rVert_{\infty}+\tfrac{1}{2}\mathrm{sp}(b_{1})\lVert p_{2}-p_{1}\rVert_{\infty}.

If in addition,
p_{1}\sim p_{2}
, then
\mathrm{sp}(b_{1})
can be changed to
\mathrm{sp}(b_{1}|_{\mathcal{S}_{1}})
where
\mathcal{S}_{1}
is the collection of recurrent states under
p_{1}
.

Proof.

This is direct adaptation of tutorial computations.

\blacksquare

Lemma D.10

(Unichain invariant measure variations)
.

Let
p_{1}\sim p_{2}
be two Markov chains and assume that
p_{1}
is unichain.
4
4

4

Hence
p_{2}
is equally unichain with the same recurrent class.

Denote
\mu_{j}
the (unique) probability invariant measure under
p_{j}
.
We have

\lVert\mu_{2}-\mu_{1}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\min_{j\in\{1,2\}}D(p_{j})\penalty\ \left\lVert p_{2}-p_{1}\right\rVert_{\infty}.

Proof.

Fix
s_{0}\in\mathcal{S}
a recurrent state.
Consider the reward function
r(s)=\mathbf{1}(s=s_{0})
, and denote
g_{j},b_{j}
the gain and bias functions of the Markov reward process
(r,b_{j})
.
Remark that
g_{j}(s)=\mu_{j}(s_{0})
.
By
Lemma

D.4
, we have
\mathrm{sp}(b_{j})\leq 2\mathrm{sp}(r)D(p_{j})\leq 2D(p_{j})
.
Continuing with
Lemma

D.9
, we have

\left\lvert\mu_{2}(s)-\mu_{1}(s)\right\rvert\leq D(p_{j})\left\lVert p_{2}-p_{1}\right\rVert_{\infty}

for both
j=1,2
.
This concludes the proof.

\blacksquare

Lemma D.11

(Unichain bias variations)
.

Let
(r_{1},p_{1})
and
(r_{2},p_{2})
be two Markov reward processes, assume that
p_{1}
is unichain and that
p_{1}\sim p_{2}
.
Denote
g_{j}
and
b_{j}
the gain and bias function of
(r_{j},p_{j})
for
j=1,2
.
We have

\lVert b_{2}-b_{1}\rVert_{\infty}\leq 4D(p_{2})\lVert r_{2}-r_{1}\rVert_{\infty}+\left(2D(p_{2})\mathrm{sp}(b_{1})+\tfrac{1}{2}\mathrm{sp}(b_{1}^{1})\right)\left\lVert p_{2}-p_{1}\right\rVert_{\infty}.

Proof.

We start by bounding
\mathrm{sp}(b_{2}-b_{1})
.
Fix
s_{0}
a recurrent state and consider the transform
p^{\prime}_{j}
that makes
s_{0}
absorbing as well as the reward function
r^{\prime}_{j}(s):=\mathbf{1}(s\neq s_{0})(r_{j}(s)-g_{j}(s))
.
Denote
g^{\prime}_{j}
and
b^{\prime}_{j}
the associated gain and bias functions.
From direct computations, we see that
g^{\prime}_{j}=0
and that
b^{\prime}_{j}(s)=b_{j}(s)-b_{j}(s_{0})
, so in particular
b^{\prime}_{j}-b_{j}\in\mathbf{R}\mathbf{e}
so
\mathrm{sp}(b^{\prime}_{j})=\mathrm{sp}(b_{j})
and
\mathrm{sp}(b^{\prime}_{2}-b^{\prime}_{1})=\mathrm{sp}(b_{2}-b_{1})
.
Moreover,
b^{\prime}_{2}
and
b^{\prime}_{1}
are related as follows:

\begin{split}b^{\prime}_{2}(s)&=\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p^{\prime}_{2}}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{\tau_{s_{0}}-1}r^{\prime}_{2}(S_{t})\right]\\
&\leq\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}\left[\tau_{s_{0}}\right]\left\lVert r^{\prime}_{2}-r^{\prime}_{1}\right\rVert_{\infty}+\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{\tau_{s_{0}}-1}\left(\mathbf{e}_{S_{t}}-p_{1}(S_{t})\right)b^{\prime}_{1}\right]\\
&\leq b_{1}^{\prime}(s)+\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}\left[\tau_{s_{0}}\right]\left(\left\lVert r_{2}-r_{1}\right\rVert_{\infty}+\left\lVert g_{2}-g_{1}\right\rVert_{\infty}+\tfrac{1}{2}\mathrm{sp}(b^{\prime}_{1})\left\lVert p^{\prime}_{2}-p^{\prime}_{1}\right\rVert_{\infty}\right)\\
&\leq b_{1}^{\prime}(s)+\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}\left[\tau_{s_{0}}\right]\left(2\left\lVert r_{2}-r_{1}\right\rVert_{\infty}+\mathrm{sp}(b_{1})\left\lVert p^{\prime}_{2}-p^{\prime}_{1}\right\rVert_{\infty}\right)\end{split}

where the last inequality is a consequence of
Lemma

D.9
and
\mathrm{sp}(b^{\prime}_{1})\leq\mathrm{sp}(b_{1})
.
With the same technique, we lower bound
b^{\prime}_{2}(s)
by
b^{\prime}_{1}(s)
with the same error term.
Remark that
\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{j}}[\tau_{s_{0}}]\leq D(p_{j})
.
So:

\begin{split}\mathrm{sp}(b_{2}-b_{1})=\mathrm{sp}(b^{\prime}_{2}-b^{\prime}_{1})&=(b^{\prime}_{2}-b^{\prime}_{1})(s_{\mathrm{max}})-(b^{\prime}_{2}-b^{\prime}_{1})(s_{\mathrm{\min}})\\
&\leq 2\penalty\ D(p_{2})\left(2\left\lVert r_{2}-r_{1}\right\rVert_{\infty}+\mathrm{sp}(b_{1})\left\lVert p^{\prime}_{2}-p^{\prime}_{1}\right\rVert_{\infty}\right).\end{split}

(D.14)

To transform the bound on the span to a bound on the norm, remark if
\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}
are two vectors and
\mathbf{q}
is a probability distribution, then
\lVert\mathbf{u}-\mathbf{v}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\mathrm{sp}(\mathbf{u}-\mathbf{v})+\lvert\mathbf{q}(\mathbf{u}-\mathbf{v})\rvert
.
Let
\mu_{j}
be the (unique) invariant probability measure under
p_{j}
.
We have

\begin{split}\lVert b_{2}-b_{1}\rVert_{\infty}&\leq\mathrm{sp}(b_{2}-b_{1})+\lvert\mu_{2}(b_{2}-b_{1})\rvert.\end{split}

(D.15)

The left term of (
D.15
) is taken care of with (
D.14
) and we are left to bound
\lvert\mu_{2}(b_{2}-b_{1})\rvert
.
Remark that
b_{j}^{1}
, the
1
-th higher order bias, is the bias of the Markov reward process
(-b_{j},p_{j})
.
We have

\displaystyle\lvert\mu_{2}(b_{2}-b_{1})\rvert=\lvert\mu_{2}b_{1}\rvert

\displaystyle=\lim_{T\to\infty}\frac{1}{T}\left\lvert\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}b_{1}(S_{t})\right]\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\lim_{T\to\infty}\frac{1}{T}\left\lvert\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\left(\mathbf{e}_{S_{t}}-p_{1}(S_{t})\right)b_{1}^{1}\right]\right\rvert

(D.16)

\displaystyle=\lim_{T\to\infty}\frac{1}{T}\left\lvert\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\left(p_{2}(S_{t})-p_{1}(S_{t})\right)b_{1}^{1}\right]\right\rvert\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\mathrm{sp}(b_{1}^{1})\lVert p_{2}-p_{1}\rVert_{\infty}.

Combining (
D.14
), (
D.15
) and (
D.16
), we obtain the desired bound.

\blacksquare

D.3
Results for multichain Markov reward processes

We generalize the inequalities of the unichain setting to general multichain Markov processes.
The new difficulty is that in the multichain setting, a perturbation of the kernel changes the probability of reaching a given recurrent component.
The first result below is key.
It is perhaps surprising, because it shows that the reaching probabilities vary additively and not multiplicatively with respect to kernel modifications.

Definition D.0

(Equivalent chains)
.

We say that two Markov chains are
equivalent
and write
p_{1}\sim p_{2}
if
p_{1}\ll p_{2}\ll p_{1}
.

Lemma D.13

(Reaching probabilities variations)
.

Let
p_{1}\sim p_{2}
be two equivalent Markov chains and
\mathcal{S}^{1}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{S}^{k}_{1}
be the recurrent classes of
p_{1}
.
Let
\tau_{\infty}:=\inf\{t\geq 1:S_{t}\in\mathcal{S}^{1}_{1}\cup\ldots\cup\mathcal{S}^{k}_{1}\}
be the reaching time to one of the recurrent classes and
\tau_{i}:=\inf\{t\geq 1:S_{t}\in\mathcal{S}^{i}_{1}\}
be the reaching time to
\mathcal{S}_{1}^{i}
.
We have

\left\lvert\mathbf{P}^{p_{1}}_{s}(\tau_{i}<\infty)-\mathbf{P}^{p_{2}}_{s}(\tau_{i}<\infty)\right\rvert\leq\min_{j\in\{1,2\}}\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{j}}[\tau_{\infty}]\penalty\ \left\lVert p_{1}-p_{2}\right\rVert_{\infty}.

In case, the bound can be simplified using
\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{j}}[\tau_{\infty}]\leq D(p_{j})
.

Proof.

Consider the reward function
r(S_{t},S_{t+1})=\mathbf{1}(S_{t}\notin\mathcal{S}_{1}^{i}\text{\penalty\ and\penalty\ }S_{t}\in\mathcal{S}_{1}^{i})
, providing reward when the walk crosses the frontier between
\mathcal{S}_{1}^{i}
and its
\mathcal{S}\setminus\mathcal{S}_{1}^{i}
.
For
j\in\{1,2\}
, we denote
g_{j},b_{j}
the gain and bias functions under the Markov reward process
(r,p_{j})
.
Since
p_{1}\sim p_{2}
, they have the same recurrent classes, we have
\mathbf{E}^{p_{j}}[\tau_{i}]<\infty
for
j=1,2
and
g_{j}(s)=0
.
Therefore, for
s\notin\mathcal{S}_{1}^{i}
, we have

\displaystyle b_{j}(s)

\displaystyle:=\lim_{T\to\infty}\mathbf{E}^{p_{j}}_{s}\left[r(S_{1},S_{2})+\ldots+r(S_{T-1},S_{T})\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{T\to\infty}\mathbf{E}^{p_{j}}_{s}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\mathbf{1}(S_{t}\notin\mathcal{S}_{1}^{i}\text{\penalty\ and\penalty\ }S_{t+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{1}^{i})\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{T\to\infty}\mathbf{E}^{p_{j}}_{s}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\mathbf{1}(\tau_{i}=t+1)\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{T\to\infty}\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\mathbf{P}^{p_{j}}_{s}\left(\tau_{i}=t+1\right)=\mathbf{P}^{p_{j}}_{s}\left(2\leq\tau_{i}<\infty\right)=\mathbf{P}^{p_{j}}_{s}(\tau_{i}<\infty)

where the last line use that
s\notin\mathcal{S}_{1}^{i}
.
For
s\in\mathcal{S}_{1}^{i}
, we have
p_{j}(s)=0
.
Our goal is therefore to bound
b_{2}(s)
with respect to
b_{1}(s)
.
For
s\notin\mathcal{S}_{1}^{i}
, we have

\displaystyle b_{2}(s)

\displaystyle=\lim_{T\to\infty}\mathbf{E}^{p_{2}}_{s}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}r(S_{t},S_{t+1})\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{=}\lim_{T\to\infty}\mathbf{E}^{p_{2}}_{s}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\left(\mathbf{e}_{S_{t}}-p_{1}(S_{t})\right)b_{1}\right]=b_{1}(s)+\lim_{T\to\infty}\mathbf{E}^{p_{2}}_{s}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\left(p_{2}(S_{t})-p_{1}(S_{t})\right)b_{1}\right]

where
(\dagger)
follows from Poisson’s equation.
We are left to bound the RHS.
Observe that once
S_{t}
belongs to one of the recurrent components of
p_{1}
, every state
s^{\prime}
that is reachable under
p_{1}
satisfies
b_{1}(s^{\prime})=0
.
Because
p_{1}\sim p_{2}
, this is also true for any state that is reachable under
p_{2}
.
Therefore

\left\lvert\lim_{T\to\infty}\mathbf{E}^{p_{2}}_{s}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\left(p_{2}(S_{t})-p_{1}(S_{t})\right)b_{1}\right]\right\rvert\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbf{E}^{p_{2}}_{s}[\tau_{\infty}]\mathrm{sp}(b_{1})\lVert p_{2}-p_{1}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbf{E}^{p_{2}}_{s}[\tau_{\infty}]\penalty\ \lVert p_{2}-p_{1}\rVert_{\infty}.

In other words,
\lvert p_{1}(s)-p_{2}(s)\rvert\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}[\tau_{\infty}]
.
Because
j=1
and
j=2
play a symmetric role,
\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}[\tau_{\infty}]
can be changed to
\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{1}}[\tau_{\infty}]
.

\blacksquare

Lemma D.14

(Multichain gain variations)
.

Let
p_{1}\sim p_{2}
be two equivalent Markov chains and
\mathcal{S}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{S}_{k}
be the (common) recurrent classes.
Let
\tau_{\infty}:=\inf\{t\geq 1:S_{t}\in\mathcal{S}_{1}\cup\ldots\cup\mathcal{S}_{k}\}
be the reaching time to one of the recurrent classes.
We have

\lVert g_{2}-g_{1}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\lVert r_{2}-r_{1}\rVert_{\infty}+\min_{j\in\{1,2\}}\tfrac{1}{2}\left(\max_{i}\mathrm{sp}(b_{j}|_{\mathcal{S}_{i}})+\tfrac{1}{2}k\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{j}}[\tau_{\infty}]\right)\lVert p_{2}-p_{1}\rVert_{\infty}

In case, the bound can be simplified using
\tfrac{1}{2}(\max_{i}\mathrm{sp}(b_{j}|_{\mathcal{S}_{i}})+\tfrac{1}{2}k\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{j}}[\tau_{\infty}])\leq(1+\tfrac{1}{4}k)D(p_{j})
.

Proof.

Let
s\in\mathcal{S}
.
We have

\displaystyle\lvert g_{2}(s)-g_{1}(s)\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lvert\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathbf{P}_{s}^{p_{2}}(\tau_{i}<\infty)g_{2}(\mathcal{S}_{i})-\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathbf{P}_{s}^{p_{1}}(\tau_{i}<\infty)g_{1}(\mathcal{S}_{i})\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{j}\mathbf{P}_{s}^{p_{2}}(\tau_{i}<\infty)\left\lvert g_{2}(\mathcal{S}_{i})-g_{1}(\mathcal{S}_{i})\right\rvert+\left\lvert\sum_{i=1}^{j}\left(\mathbf{P}_{s}^{p_{2}}(\tau_{i}<\infty)-\mathbf{P}_{s}^{p_{1}}(\tau_{i}<\infty)\right)g_{1}(\mathcal{S}_{i})\right\rvert

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{\leq}\sum_{i=1}^{j}\mathbf{P}_{s}^{p_{2}}(\tau_{i}<\infty)\left(\lVert r_{2}-r_{1}\rVert_{\infty}+\tfrac{1}{2}\mathrm{sp}(b_{1}|_{\mathcal{S}_{i}})\lVert p_{2}-p_{1}\rVert_{\infty}\right)

\displaystyle\phantom{{}\overset{(\dagger)}{\leq}{}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\left\lVert\left(\mathbf{P}_{s}^{p_{2}}(\tau_{i}<\infty)-\mathbf{P}_{s}^{p_{1}}(\tau_{i}<\infty)\right)_{i=1}^{k}\right\rVert_{1}

\displaystyle\overset{(\ddagger)}{\leq}\lVert r_{2}-r_{1}\rVert_{\infty}+\tfrac{1}{2}\left(\max_{i}\mathrm{sp}(b_{1}|_{\mathcal{S}_{i}})+\tfrac{1}{2}k\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{1}}[\tau_{\infty}]\right)\lVert p_{2}-p_{1}\rVert_{\infty}

where
(\dagger)
follows by applying
Lemma

D.9
to the LHS and
(\ddagger)
by applying
Lemma

D.13
to the RHS.

\blacksquare

Lemma D.15

(Multichain invariant measure variations)
.

Let
p_{1}\sim p_{2}
be two equivalent Markov chains and
\mathcal{S}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{S}_{k}
be the (common) recurrent classes.
Let
\tau_{\infty}:=\inf\{t\geq 1:S_{t}\in\mathcal{S}_{1}\cup\ldots\cup\mathcal{S}_{k}\}
be the reaching time to one of the recurrent classes.
Denote
\mu_{j}(-|s)
the asymptotic empirical distribution of visits starting from
s
under
p_{j}
.
We have

\forall s\in\mathcal{S},\hskip 10.00002pt\left\lVert\mu_{2}(-|s)-\mu_{1}(-|s)\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\min_{j\in\{1,2\}}\tfrac{1}{2}\left(\max_{i}D(p_{j}|_{\mathcal{S}_{i}})+\tfrac{1}{2}k\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{j}}[\tau_{\infty}]\right)\lVert p_{2}-p_{1}\rVert_{\infty}.

In case, the bound can be simplified using
\tfrac{1}{2}(\max_{i}D(p_{j}|_{\mathcal{S}_{i}})+\tfrac{1}{2}k\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{j}}[\tau_{\infty}])\leq(1+\tfrac{1}{4}k)D(p_{j})
.

Proof.

This is a consequence of
Lemma

D.14
by considering the reward function
r(s)=\mathbf{1}(s=s_{\infty})
for
s_{\infty}\in\mathcal{S}
a fixed state, similarly to
Lemma

D.10
.
Denote
g_{j},b_{j}
denote the gain and bias function of the Markov reward process
(r,p_{j})
.
Remark that
g_{j}(s_{0})=\mu_{j}(s_{\infty}|s_{0})
.
By
Lemma

D.14
, we have

\lvert\mu_{2}(s_{\infty}|s_{0})-\mu_{1}(s_{\infty}|s_{0})\rvert\leq\min_{j\in\{1,2\}}\tfrac{1}{2}\left(\max_{i}\mathrm{sp}(b_{j}|_{\mathcal{S}_{i}})+\tfrac{1}{2}k\mathbf{E}_{s_{0}}^{p_{j}}[\tau_{\infty}]\right)\lVert p_{2}-p_{1}\rVert_{\infty}.

Because
\mathcal{S}_{i}
is a recurrent class under
p_{j}
, it corresponds to the optimal bias of a communicating model, so by
Lemma

D.4
, we have
\mathrm{sp}(b_{j}|_{\mathcal{S}_{i}})\leq\mathrm{sp}(r)D(p_{j}|_{\mathcal{S}_{i}})\leq D(p_{j}|_{\mathcal{S}_{i}})
.
This provides the desired bound.

\blacksquare

Lemma D.16

(Multichain bias variations)
.

Let
(r_{1},p_{1})
and
(r_{2},p_{2})
be two Markov reward processes with
p_{1}\sim p_{2}
and let
\mathcal{S}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{S}_{k}
be the (common) recurrent classes.
Denote
g_{j},b_{j}
the gain and bias functions of
(r_{j},p_{j})
for
j=1,2
.
Let
\tau_{\infty}:=\inf\{t\geq 1:S_{t}\in\mathcal{S}_{1}\cup\ldots\cup\mathcal{S}_{k}\}
be the reaching time to one of the recurrent classes.
We have

\left\lVert b_{2}-b_{1}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq 6D(p_{2})\lVert r_{2}-r_{1}\rVert_{\infty}+\left(\left(7+\tfrac{1}{2}k\right)D(p_{2})D(p_{1})+2D(p_{1})^{2}\right)\lVert p_{2}-p\rVert_{\infty}.

Following
Lemma

D.5
, when
\lVert p_{2}-p_{1}\rVert_{\infty}\leq{D(p_{1})}^{-1}
, the quantity
D(p_{2})
can be changed to
2D(p_{1})
.

The result can be improved by more carefully tracking the Lipschitz constants throughout.
To lighten the typography—and because our application do not require an optimal result, we overshoot the error term.

Proof.

For
i=1,\ldots,k
, pick
s^{i}_{0}\in\mathcal{S}_{i}
and denote
\mathcal{S}_{0}=\{s^{1}_{0},\ldots,s^{k}_{0}\}
.
For
j=1,2
, let
p^{\prime}_{j}
be the kernel obtained by copying
p_{j}
while making every
s^{i}_{0}
absorbing, and set
r^{\prime}_{j}(s):=\mathbf{1}(s\notin\mathcal{S}_{0})(r_{j}(s)-g_{j}(s))
.
Denote
g^{\prime}_{j}
and
b^{\prime}_{j}
the gain and bias functions of the Markov reward process
(r^{\prime}_{j},p^{\prime}_{j})
.
Observe that
g^{\prime}_{j}=0
and
b^{\prime}_{j}(s_{0}^{i})=0
for all
i=1,\ldots,k
.
Moreover, the biases
b_{j}
and
b^{\prime}_{j}
are linked as follows

\begin{split}b^{\prime}_{j}(s)=\Clim_{T\to\infty}\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p^{\prime}_{j}}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}r^{\prime}_{j}(S_{t})\right]&=\Clim_{T\to\infty}\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p^{\prime}_{j}}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{\tau_{\mathcal{S}_{0}}\wedge T-1}\left(r_{j}(S_{t})-g_{j}(S_{t})\right)\right]\\
&=\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{j}}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{\tau_{\mathcal{S}_{0}}-1}\left(r_{j}(S_{t})-g_{j}(S_{t})\right)\right]\\
&=b_{j}(s)-\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{j}}\left[b(S_{\tau_{\mathcal{S}_{0}}})\right]=b_{j}(s)-\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathbf{P}^{p_{j}}_{s}\left(\tau_{\mathcal{S}_{i}}<\infty\right)b_{j}(s_{0}^{i}).\end{split}

(D.17)

In particular,
\mathrm{sp}(b^{\prime}_{j})\leq 2\penalty\ \mathrm{sp}(b_{j})\leq 4D(p_{j})
by
Lemma

D.4
.
We have

\begin{split}b^{\prime}_{2}(s)=\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{\tau_{\mathcal{S}_{0}}-1}r^{\prime}_{2}(S_{t})\right]&\leq\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}\left[\tau_{\mathcal{S}_{0}}\right]\left\lVert r^{\prime}_{2}-r^{\prime}_{1}\right\rVert_{\infty}+\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{\tau_{\mathcal{S}_{0}}-1}\left(\mathbf{e}_{S_{t}}-p_{1}(S_{t})\right)b^{\prime}_{1}\right]\\
&\overset{(\dagger)}{\leq}b^{\prime}_{1}(s)+\mathbf{E}_{s}^{p_{2}}\left[\tau_{\mathcal{S}_{0}}\right]\left(\left\lVert r^{\prime}_{2}-r^{\prime}_{1}\right\rVert_{\infty}+\tfrac{1}{2}\mathrm{sp}(b^{\prime}_{1})\left\lVert p^{\prime}_{2}-p^{\prime}_{1}\right\rVert_{\infty}\right)\\
&\overset{(\ddagger)}{\leq}b^{\prime}_{1}(s)+D(p_{2})\left(\left\lVert r_{2}-r_{1}\right\rVert_{\infty}+\left\lVert g_{2}-g_{1}\right\rVert_{\infty}+2D(p_{1})\left\lVert p_{2}-p_{1}\right\rVert_{\infty}\right)\\
&\overset{(\lx@sectionsign)}{\leq}b^{\prime}_{1}(s)+D(p_{2})\left(2\left\lVert r_{2}-r_{1}\right\rVert_{\infty}+\left(3+\tfrac{1}{4}k\right)D(p_{1})\left\lVert p_{2}-p_{1}\right\rVert_{\infty}\right).\end{split}

where
(\dagger)
follows from Poisson’s equation,
(\ddagger)
bounds bias span and hitting times by diameter and expands the definition of
r^{\prime}_{j}
, and
(\lx@sectionsign)
invokes the (simplified version of)
Lemma

D.14
to bound
\lVert g_{2}-g_{1}\rVert_{\infty}
.
With the same computations, we upper-bound
b^{\prime}_{2}(s)
relatively to
b^{\prime}_{1}(s)
, leading to the equation:

\left\lvert b_{2}^{\prime}(s)-b_{1}^{\prime}(s)\right\rvert\leq D(p_{2})\left(2\left\lVert r_{2}-r_{1}\right\rVert_{\infty}+\left(3+\tfrac{1}{4}k\right)D(p_{1})\left\lVert p_{2}-p_{1}\right\rVert_{\infty}\right).

(D.18)

Combining (
D.17
) and (
D.18
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lvert b_{2}(s)-b_{1}(s)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lvert b^{\prime}_{2}(s)-b^{\prime}_{1}(s)\right\rvert+\left\lvert\sum_{i=1}^{k}\left(\mathbf{P}^{p_{2}}_{s}\left(\tau_{\mathcal{S}_{i}}<\infty\right)b_{2}(s_{0}^{i})-\mathbf{P}^{p_{1}}_{s}\left(\tau_{\mathcal{S}_{i}}<\infty\right)b_{1}(s_{0}^{i})\right)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lvert b^{\prime}_{2}(s)-b^{\prime}_{1}(s)\right\rvert+\left\lvert\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathbf{P}^{p_{2}}_{s}\left(\tau_{\mathcal{S}_{i}}<\infty\right)\left(b_{2}(s_{0}^{i})-b_{1}(s_{0}^{i})\right)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\phantom{{}\leq\left\lvert b^{\prime}_{2}(s)-b^{\prime}_{1}(s)\right\rvert}+\left\lvert\sum_{i=1}^{k}\left(\mathbf{P}^{p_{2}}_{s}\left(\tau_{\mathcal{S}_{i}}<\infty\right)-\mathbf{P}^{p_{1}}_{s}\left(\tau_{\mathcal{S}_{i}}<\infty\right)\right)b_{1}(s_{0}^{i})\right\rvert

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{\leq}6D(p_{2})\lVert r_{2}-r_{1}\rVert_{\infty}+\left(\left(7+\tfrac{1}{2}k\right)D(p_{2})D(p_{1})+\tfrac{1}{2}\mathrm{sp}(b_{1}^{1})\right)\lVert p_{2}-p\rVert_{\infty}

\displaystyle\overset{(\ddagger)}{\leq}6D(p_{2})\lVert r_{2}-r_{1}\rVert_{\infty}+\left(\left(7+\tfrac{1}{2}k\right)D(p_{2})D(p_{1})+2D(p_{1})^{2}\right)\lVert p_{2}-p\rVert_{\infty}

where
(\dagger)
is obtained by bounding the first term with (
D.18
), the second term with
Lemma

D.11
and the third term with
Lemma

D.13
; and
(\ddagger)
follows from
\mathrm{sp}(b_{1}^{1})\leq 4D(p_{1})^{2}
by applying
Lemma

D.4
twice.

\blacksquare
